Telegram from Major-General Sir Everard Venables, V.C., K.C.M.G., to his son Richard Venables, of St. Austin’s:

> Venables, St. Austin’s. Very well.—Venables

===Chinese===
埃弗拉德·维纳布尔斯少将爵士致其在圣奥斯汀的儿子理查德·维纳布尔斯的电报：

> 维纳布尔斯，圣奥斯汀。很好。——维纳布尔斯

### X / 十

===Original===
Extract from Letter from Richard Venables, of St. Austin’s, to his father Major-General Sir Everard Venables, V.C., K.C.M.G.:

> … Thanks, awfully—

Extract from *The Austinian* of October:

> The following O.A.s have gone into residence this year: At Oxford, J. Scrymgeour, Corpus Christi; R. Venables, Trinity; K. Crespigny-Brown, Balliol.

Extract from the *Daily Mail*’s account of the ’Varsity match of the following summer:

> … The St. Austin’s freshman, Venables, fully justified his inclusion by scoring a stylish fifty-seven. He hit eight fours, and except for a miss-hit in the slips, at 51, which Smith might possibly have secured had he started sooner, gave nothing like a chance. Venables, it will be remembered, played several good innings for Oxford in the earlier matches, notably, his not out contribution of 103 against Sussex—

===Chinese===
圣奥斯汀的理查德·维纳布尔斯致父亲埃弗拉德·维纳布尔斯少将爵士的信（摘录）：

> ……多谢，万分的——

十月号《奥斯汀人》（*The Austinian*）摘录：

> 以下诸位老奥斯汀人（O.A.）今年已入学入住：牛津方面，圣体学院（Corpus Christi）的 J. 斯克里姆杰（J. Scrymgeour），三一学院（Trinity）的 R. 维纳布尔斯，贝利奥尔学院（Balliol）的 K. 克雷斯皮尼-布朗（K. Crespigny-Brown）。

次年夏季大学对抗赛（’Varsity match）中《每日邮报》（*Daily Mail*）的报道摘录：

> ……圣奥斯汀出身的一年级新生维纳布尔斯，以潇洒的五十七分，充分证明了自己的入选当之无愧。他击出八记四分球；除 51 分时在截杀区（slips）的一次擦棒失误——史密斯（Smith）若启动得早些，兴许能把它拿下——之外，几乎未给对方任何机会。读者当还记得，维纳布尔斯在此前各场比赛中已为牛津打出数局好球，尤以对苏塞克斯（Sussex）一役未出局贡献的 103 分著称——

## An International Affair / 一桩国际事件

### Part I / 第一部

===Original===

The whole thing may be said to have begun when Mr. Oliver Ring of New York, changing cars, as he called it, at Wrykyn on his way to London, had to wait an hour for his train. He put in that hour by strolling about the town and seeing the sights, which were not numerous. Wrykyn, except on Market Day, was wont to be wrapped in a primeval calm which very nearly brought tears to the strenuous eyes of the man from Manhattan. He had always been told that England was a slow country, and his visit, now in its third week, had confirmed this opinion: but even in England he had not looked to find such a lotus-eating place as Wrykyn. He looked at the shop windows. They resembled the shop windows of every other country town in England. There was no dash, no initiative about them. They did not leap to the eye and arrest the pedestrian’s progress. They ordered these things, thought Mr. Ring, better in the States. And then something seemed to whisper to him that here was the place to set up a branch of Ring’s Come-One Come-All Up-to-date Stores. During his stroll he had gathered certain pieces of information. To wit, that Wrykyn was where the county families for ten miles round did their shopping, that the population of the town was larger than would appear at first sight to a casual observer, and, finally, that there was a school of six hundred boys only a mile away. Nothing could be better. Within a month he would take to himself the entire trade of the neighbourhood.

===Chinese===

整桩事情，可以说发端于纽约的奥利弗·林格先生（Oliver Ring）。他此行去伦敦，途中在瑞金（Wrykyn）「换车」——这是他自己的叫法——不得已要等上一个钟头的火车。这一个钟头，他拿来在镇上溜达，观光赏景；景致倒不甚众多。瑞金这地方，除了集市日，惯常裹在一层太古的宁静里，那份宁静几乎要催出这位曼哈顿汉子那双奋斗之眼的眼泪来。他一向听人说英国是个慢腾腾的国度，此行已入第三周，更坐实了这一见解：但即便在英国，他也不曾料到会撞见瑞金这般令人浑噩度日的食莲乡。他打量那些橱窗。它们与英格兰其余任何乡镇的橱窗毫无二致，毫无闯劲，毫无首创精神，既不跃入眼帘，也不拽住行人的脚步。这些个事，林格先生心想，还是人家合众国办得地道。接着，冥冥中似有声音向他低语：此地，正是开设林格「来的都是客」新式商店（Ring's Come-one Come-all Up-to-date Stores）分号的宝地。溜达途中，他已搜罗到若干情报。即：方圆十英里的乡绅人家都到瑞金来采买；镇上的人口，比走马观花的路人初见所料想的要多；末了，一英里开外还有一所收着六百名学生的学校。再好不过了。不出一个月，左近的全部买卖就要尽归他的腰包。

===Original===

“It’s a cinch,” murmured Mr. Ring with a glad smile, as he boarded his train, “a lead-pipe cinch.”

Everybody who has moved about the world at all knows Ring’s Come-one Come-all Up-to-date Stores. The main office is in New York. Broadway, to be exact, on the left as you go down, just before you get to Park Row, where the newspapers come from. There is another office in Chicago. Others in St. Louis, St. Paul, and across the seas in London, Paris, Berlin, and, in short, everywhere. The peculiar advantage about Ring’s Stores is that you can get anything you happen to want there, from a motor to a macaroon, and rather cheaper than you could get it anywhere else. England had up to the present been ill-supplied with these handy paradises, the one in Piccadilly being the only extant specimen. But now Mr. Ring in person had crossed the Atlantic on a tour of inspection, and things were shortly to be so brisk that you would be able to hear them whizz.

So an army of workmen invaded Wrykyn. A trio of decrepit houses in the High Street were pulled down with a run, and from the ruins there began to rise like a Phoenix the striking building which was to be the Wrykyn Branch of Ring’s Come-one Come-all Up-to-date Stores.

===Chinese===

「十拿九稳，」林格先生喜气洋洋地低声念叨着登上火车，「不，是铁板钉钉。」

凡在世上多少走动过的人，就没有不知道林格「来的都是客」新式商店的。总管理处设在纽约。确切说，在百老汇（Broadway），往下城方向走靠左手，快到帕克罗（Park Row）的地方——各家报纸就是打那儿来的。芝加哥另设一处。圣路易斯、圣保罗也各有一处，海那边则伦敦、巴黎、柏林——简而言之，无所不在。林格商店的独门优势在于：你随手想要什么，那儿都能弄到，从汽车到蛋白杏仁饼，样样齐全，价钱还比别处便宜些。英格兰此前一直缺供这类便利天堂，皮卡迪利（Piccadilly）的那一家是硕果仅存的孤本。而如今，林格先生亲自渡过大西洋巡阅业务，局面眼看就要红火得叫你能听见嗖嗖的风声。

于是，一支工人大军开进了瑞金。高街上三所风烛残年的老屋，一眨眼拆了个干净；废墟之中，那座注定要出任林格「来的都是客」新式商店瑞金分店的惹眼建筑，开始像凤凰一般拔地而起。

===Original===

The sensation among the tradesmen caused by the invasion was, as may be imagined, immense and painful. The thing was a public disaster. It resembled the advent of a fox in a fowl-run. For years the tradesmen of Wrykyn had jogged along in their comfortable way, each making his little profits, with no thought of competition or modern hustle. And now the enemy was at their doors. Many were the gloomy looks cast at the gaudy building as it grew like a mushroom. It was finished with incredible speed, and then advertisements began to flood the local papers. A special sheaf of bills was despatched to the school.

Dunstable got hold of one, and read it with interest. Then he went in search of his friend Linton to find out what he thought of it.

===Chinese===

不难想象，这场入侵在本地商界激起的震动既巨大又痛切。此事实乃一场公众灾难，阵仗好比狐狸驾临鸡舍。多年以来，瑞金的商人们沿着各自舒坦的老路小颠小跑，各赚各的蝇头小利，浑不知竞争与现代奔忙为何物。而今，大敌已然压门。眼见那座俗艳的楼雨后春笋般节节蹿高，投向它的阴沉目光不知凡几。它以匪夷所思的速度落成，紧接着，广告开始淹灌本地各报。一捆特制的传单专程发往学校。

邓斯特布尔（Dunstable）弄到了一张，读得津津有味。随后他便去寻他的朋友林顿（Linton），探探对方的高见。

===Original===

Linton was at work in the laboratory. He was an enthusiastic, but unskilful, chemist. The only thing he could do with any real certainty was to make oxygen. But he had ambitions beyond that feat, and was continually experimenting in a reckless way which made the chemistry master look wan and uneasy. He was bending over a complicated mixture of tubes, acids, and Bunsen burners when Dunstable found him. It was after school, so that the laboratory was empty, but for them.

“Don’t mind me,” said Dunstable, taking a seat on the table.

“Look out, man, don’t jog. Sit tight, and I’ll broaden your mind for you. I take this bit of litmus paper, and dip it into this bilge, and if I’ve done it right, it’ll turn blue.”

“Then I bet it doesn’t,” said Dunstable.

The paper turned red.

“Hades,” said Linton calmly. “Well, I’m not going to sweat at it any more. Let’s go down to Cook’s.”

===Chinese===

林顿正在实验室里忙活。他是位热忱有余、手艺欠佳的化学家。唯一一件他能办得十拿九稳的事，是制取氧气。但他的雄心不止于此，总是以不管不顾的路数接连做实验，搅得化学老师面容惨淡、心神不宁。邓斯特布尔找到他时，他正俯身在一堆试管、酸类与本生灯的复杂组合之上。其时已放学，实验室里除他们二人外空无一人。

「别管我，」邓斯特布尔说着，在桌沿上坐了。

「留神，伙计，别晃桌子。坐稳了，我这就来替你开扩开扩眼界。我拿起这片石蕊试纸，往这锅泔水里一浸——要是我手法无误，它会变蓝。」

「那我赌它偏不变。」邓斯特布尔说。

试纸变红了。

「见鬼。」林顿不动声色，「得了，我不打算再为它流汗了。走，咱们上库克店（Cook's）去。」

===Original===

Cook’s is the one school institution which nobody forgets who has been to Wrykyn. It is a little confectioner’s shop in the High Street. Its exterior is somewhat forbidding, and the uninitiated would probably shudder and pass on, wondering how on earth such a place could find a public daring enough to support it by eating its wares. But the school went there in flocks. Tea at Cook’s was the alternative to a study tea. There was a large room at the back of the shop, and here oceans of hot tea and tons of toast were consumed. The staff of Cook’s consisted of Mr. Cook, late sergeant in a line regiment, six foot three, disposition amiable, left leg cut off above the knee by a spirited Fuzzy in the last Sudan war; Mrs. Cook, wife of the above, disposition similar, and possessing the useful gift of being able to listen to five people at one and the same time; and an invisible menial, or menials, who made toast in some nether region at a perfectly dizzy rate of speed. Such was Cook’s.

===Chinese===

库克店，是凡在瑞金念过书的人谁也忘不了的一座学校机构。它是高街上的一爿小糕饼糖果铺。门面颇有几分冷峻拒人，外行见了多半会打个寒噤绕道而行，暗暗纳闷这样的铺子究竟从哪儿找得到一拨胆敢以吃它的货色来捧场的公众。可全校学生偏偏成群结队地往那儿涌。到库克店用茶，是与书房茶会分庭抗礼的另一选项。铺子后头有间大屋，汪洋大海的热茶、成吨成吨的吐司都在这里被消灭。库克店的员工班底计有：库克先生，前常备步兵团中士，身高六英尺三，性情和蔼，左腿在上一次苏丹战争中被一位劲头十足的苏丹武士从膝盖上头砍去；库克太太，即上述中士的内助，性情相仿，兼有一门实用的天赋——能同时听五个人讲话；外加一名看不见的仆役（也可能是几名），在某个不见天日的下层角落，以令人头晕目眩的速率烤制吐司。库克店者，如是而已。

===Original===

“Talking of Cook’s,” said Dunstable, producing his pamphlet, “have you seen this? It’ll be a bit of a knockout for them, I should think.”

Linton took the paper, and began to read. Dunstable roamed curiously about the laboratory, examining things.

“What are these little crystal sort of bits of stuff?” he asked, coming to a standstill before a large jar and opening it. “They look good to eat. Shall I try one?”

===Chinese===

「说起库克店，」邓斯特布尔掏出他那张传单，「这个你见过没有？依我看，这够他们挨上一记闷棍的。」

林顿接过传单读了起来。邓斯特布尔在实验室里好奇地转悠，东瞧西看。

「这些亮晶晶、一小块一小块的是什么玩意儿？」他问，在一口大缸前收住脚，揭开了盖子，「瞧着挺好吃。我来尝一个？」

===Original===

“Don’t you be an idiot,” said the expert, looking up. “What have you got hold of? Great Scott, no, don’t eat that stuff.”

“Why not? Is it poison?”

“No. But it would make you sick as a cat. It’s Sal Ammoniac.”

“Sal how much?”

“Ammoniac. You’d be awfully bad.”

“All right, then, I won’t. Well, what do you think of that thing? It’ll be rough on Cook’s, won’t it? You see they advertise a special ‘public-school’ tea, as they call it. It sounds jolly good. I don’t know what buckwheat cakes are, but they ought to be decent. I suppose now everybody’ll chuck Cook’s and go there. It’s a beastly shame, considering that Cook’s has been a sort of school shop so long. And they really depend on the school. At least, one never sees anybody else going there. Well, I shall stick to Cook’s. I don’t want any of your beastly Yankee invaders. Support home industries. Be a patriot. The band then played God Save the King, and the meeting dispersed. But, seriously, man, I am rather sick about this. The Cooks are such awfully good sorts, and this is bound to make them lose a tremendous lot. The school’s simply crawling with chaps who’d do anything to get a good tea cheaper than they’re getting now. They’ll simply scrum in to this new place.”

===Chinese===

「你可别犯傻，」这位专家抬起头来，「你手里抓的什么？天哪，别，可别吃那玩意儿。」

「为什么？有毒么？」

「没毒。可它能叫你吐得七荤八素。这是氯化铵（Sal Ammoniac）。」

「氯化什么？」

「化铵。保管叫你够呛。」

「那好，我不尝了。呃，你觉着那玩意儿怎么样？这对库克店可够狠的，是不是？你瞧，他们登广告卖一种特备的『公学茶』——他们是这么叫的。听着怪美的。荞麦饼是何物我不清楚，想来总过得去。我看往后大伙儿都要甩了库克店往那边跑。这太缺德了——想想库克店给学校当了这么些年的公共铺子。人家实实在在指着学校过活呢。至少，除了学生，就没见旁人进过那门。反正我认准库克店了。你们那种讨厌的扬基侵略者，我是敬谢不敏。支持本国实业。当个爱国者。此时乐队奏起《天佑吾王》，大会就此散会。不过说正经的，伙计，这事我心里挺堵。库克两口子人是顶好的，这一下非叫他们亏掉一大截不可。学校里满地爬的都是这号小子——为了比眼下便宜地吃上一顿好茶，什么都肯干。他们准会跟抢争球似的，呼啦一下涌进那家新铺子。」

===Original===

“Well, I don’t see what we can do,” said Linton, “except keep on going to Cook’s ourselves. Let’s be going now, by the way. We’ll get as many chaps as we can to promise to stick to them. But we can’t prevent the rest going where they like. Come on.”

The atmosphere at Cook’s that evening was heavily charged with gloom. Ex-Sergeant Cook, usually a treasury of jest and anecdote, was silent and thoughtful. Mrs. Cook bustled about with her customary vigour, but she too was disinclined for conversation. The place was ominously empty. A quartet of school house juniors in one corner and a solitary prefect from Donaldson’s completed the sum of the customers. Nobody seemed to want to talk a great deal. There was something in the air which

> said as plain as whisper in the ear,

“The place is haunted.”

and so it was. Haunted by the spectre of that hideous, new, glaring redbrick building down the street, which had opened its doors to the public on the previous afternoon.

“Look there,” said Dunstable, as they came out. He pointed along the street. The doors of the new establishment were congested. A crowd, made up of members of various houses, was pushing to get past another crowd which was trying to get out. The “public-school tea at one shilling” appeared to have proved attractive.

“Look at ’em,” said Dunstable. “Sordid beasts! All they care about is filling themselves. There goes that man Merrett. Rand-Brown with him. Here come four more. Come on. It makes me sick.”

===Chinese===

「依我看，咱们无能为力，」林顿说，「除了自个儿照旧去库克店。对了，这就走吧。咱们去拉尽可能多的人，叫他们答应照旧捧场。可旁人爱去哪儿，咱们拦不住。走。」

那天傍晚，库克店里的空气浸透了愁云。前任中士库克先生，平日是一座笑谈与掌故的宝库，此刻却沉默出神。库克太太照旧风风火火地张罗里外，可她也提不起谈兴。店堂空得叫人心头发毛。一角里坐着学舍（School House）的四名低年级生，外加唐纳森家（Donaldson's）孤零零一位级长，顾客的账目便到此结清。看样子谁也不想多开口。空气里有种东西，

> 像贴着耳朵的低语一样说得明白：

「这地方闹鬼。」

也确实闹鬼。作祟的，是街那头那座刺眼的新红砖楼的幽灵——头一天下午，它才向公众敞开过大门。

「你瞧那儿。」两人走出店门时，邓斯特布尔说。他顺着街一指。那家新商号门口挤得水泄不通。一群由各宿舍人马凑成的人堆正往里涌，又跟另一群正往外挣的人堆顶了牛。看来，「一先令一份的公学茶」确乎证明了自身的魅力。

「瞧瞧这帮家伙，」邓斯特布尔说，「一身铜臭的畜生！满脑子惦记的就是填饱肚皮。那不是梅里特（Merrett）么。跟他搭伙的是兰德-布朗（Rand-Brown）。那边又来了四个。走吧。看得我想吐。」

===Original===

“I wish it would make *them* sick,” said Linton.

“Perhaps it will. … By George!”

He started.

“What’s up?” said Linton.

“Oh, nothing. I was only thinking of something.”

They walked on without further conversation. Dunstable’s brain was working fast. He had an idea, and was busy developing it.

===Chinese===

「我倒巴不得真叫*他们*吐出来。」林顿说。

「说不定真能。……哎呀！」

他猛地一个激灵。

「怎么了？」林顿说。

「哦，没什么。我只是想起了件事。」

两人再没交谈，径直往前走。邓斯特布尔的脑子开足了马力。他有了一个点子，正忙着把它孵化成形。

---

===Original===

The manager of the Wrykyn Branch of Ring’s Come-one Come-all Stores stood at the entrance to his shop on the following afternoon spitting with energy and precision on to the pavement—he was a freeborn American citizen—and eyeing the High Street as a monarch might gaze at his kingdom. He had just completed a highly satisfactory report to headquarters, and was feeling contented with the universe, and the way in which it was managed. Even in the short time since the opening of the store he had managed to wake up the sluggish Britishers as if they had had an electric shock.

“We,” he observed epigrammatically to a passing cat, which had stopped on its way to look at him, “are it.”

As he spoke he perceived a youth coming towards him down the street. He wore a cap of divers colours, from which the manager argued that he belonged to the school. Evidently a devotee of the advertised “public-school” shillingsworth, and one who, as urged by the small bills, had come early to avoid the rush. “Step right in, mister,” he said, moving aside from the doorway. “And what can I do for *you*?”

===Chinese===

次日午后，林格「来的都是客」商店瑞金分店的经理站在店门口，精神抖擞、弹无虚发地朝人行道啐着唾沫——他是一位生而自由的美国公民——一面拿眼打量高街，气派堪比君主巡视自己的王国。他刚向总部交出一份极其令人满意的报告，正对整个宇宙、以及宇宙眼下的经营管理方式感到心满意足。开张至今虽然只有短短数日，他已成功把懒散迟钝的英国佬们电击一般唤醒了。

「咱们，」他对一只过路的猫精辟地发表警句——那猫半途驻足，正拿眼瞧他——「就是正主儿。」

话音未落，他瞥见一个少年沿街朝自己走来。少年头戴一顶杂色拼块的帽子，经理由此断定他是学校的人。显然是广告上那一先令「公学茶」的虔诚信徒，并且遵照小传单的敦劝，赶早前来，以免向隅。「里边请，小先生。」他侧身让开门口说道，「有什么能替*您*效劳的？」

===Original===

“Are you the manager of this place?” asked Dunstable—for the youth was that strategist, and no other.

“On the bull’s eye first time,” replied the manager with easy courtesy. “Will you take a cigar or a coconut?”

“Can I have a bit of a talk with you, if you aren’t busy?”

“Sure. Step right in.”

“Now, sir,” said the manager, “what’s *your* little trouble?”

===Chinese===

「您是这地方的经理么？」邓斯特布尔问道——来者正是那位战略家本尊，如假包换。

「头一句就正中靶心。」经理带着从容不迫的客气答道，「您是来支雪茄呢，还是来个椰子？」

「您要是不忙，我能跟您聊上两句么？」

「当然。里边请。」

「那么，先生，」经理说，「*您*这位有什么小小的烦难？」

===Original===

“It’s about this public school tea business,” said Dunstable. “It’s rather a shame, you see. Before you came barging in, everybody used to go to Cook’s.”

“And now,” interrupted the manager, “they come to us. Correct, sir. We *are* the main stem. And why not?”

“Cook’s such a good sort.”

“I should like to know him,” said the manager politely.

“You see,” said Dunstable, “it doesn’t so much matter about the other things you sell; but Cook’s simply relies on giving fellows tea in the afternoon—”

===Chinese===

「是这么回事，关于这公学茶的买卖。」邓斯特布尔说，「您瞧，这未免有点不厚道。在您横冲直撞开进来之前，大伙儿向来都是去库克店的。」

「而现在，」经理插话道，「他们上咱们这儿来了。没错，先生。咱们*才是*主干道。这有什么不好？」

「库克那人是位大大的好人。」

「我倒愿意见识见识他。」经理客气地说。

「您明白，」邓斯特布尔说，「你们卖旁的什么倒还罢了；可库克家就指着下午给学生们供茶过活——」

===Original===

“One moment, sir,” said the man from the States. “Let me remind you of a little rule which will be useful to you when you butt into the big, cold world. That is, never let sentiment interfere with business. See? Either Ring’s Stores or your friend has got to be on top, and, if I know anything, it’s going to be We. We! And I’m afraid that’s all I can do for you, unless you’ve that hungry feeling, and want to sample our public-school tea at twenty-five cents.”

“No, thanks,” said Dunstable. “Here come some chaps, though, who look as if they might.”

He stepped aside as half a dozen School House juniors raced up.

“For one day only,” said the manager to Dunstable, “you may partake free, if you care to. You have man’s most priceless possession, Cool Cheek. And Cool Cheek, when recognised, should not go unrewarded. Step in.”

“No thanks,” said Dunstable. “You’ll find me at Cook’s if you want me.”

“Kindness,” said he to himself, as Mrs. Cook served him in the depressed way which had now become habitual with her, “kindness having failed, we must try severity.”

===Chinese===

「且慢，先生，」这位来自合众国的汉子说，「容我提醒你一条小规矩，日后你一头闯进这个又大又冷的世道时用得着：千万莫让感情搅了买卖。明白？不是林格商店压在上头，就是你那位朋友压在上头，二者必居其一——要说我懂点什么，上头的准是咱们。咱们！恐怕我能为您效劳的也就这么多了，除非您正有那种饥饿的感觉，想品尝一份我们二十五美分的公学茶。」

「不了，多谢。」邓斯特布尔说，「不过那边倒来了几位小哥，瞧那样子像是正有那种感觉。」

六名学舍低年级生飞奔而来，他侧身让开。

「仅限今日，」经理对邓斯特布尔说，「你若乐意，可以白吃一顿。你拥有人类最无价的财产——冷面厚皮（Cool Cheek）。而冷面厚皮一经识破，便不应不受犒赏。里边请。」

「不了，多谢。」邓斯特布尔说，「要找我，去库克店。」

「怀柔既已失败，」当库克太太以如今已成习惯的蔫头耷脑的架势给他端上茶点时，他暗自忖道，「便当改试严厉。」

### Part II / 第二部

===Original===

Those who knew and liked Dunstable were both pained and disgusted at his behaviour during the ensuing three days. He suddenly exhibited a weird fondness for some of Wrykyn’s least deserving inmates. He walked over to school with Merrett, of Seymour’s, and Ruthven, of Donaldson’s, both notorious outsiders. When Linton wanted him to come and play fives after school, he declined on the ground that he was teaing with Chadwick, of Appleby’s. Now in the matter of absolute outsiderishness Chadwick, of Appleby’s, was to Merrett, of Seymour’s, as captain is to subaltern. Linton was horrified, and said so.

“What do you want to do it for?” he asked. “What’s the point of it? You can’t like those chaps.”

“Awfully good sorts when you get to know them,” said Dunstable.

“You’ve been some time finding it out.”

“I know. Chadwick’s an acquired taste. By the way, I’m giving a tea on Thursday. Will you come?”

“Who’s going to be there?” inquired Linton warily.

===Chinese===

认识并喜爱邓斯特布尔的人，对他随后三天的行止无不又痛心又作呕。他忽然对瑞金几名最不入流的分子表现出一种诡异的眷恋。他同西摩家的梅里特、唐纳森家的鲁斯文（Ruthven）结伴上学——两位都是声名在外的边缘人物。放学后林顿约他去打墙手球，他推辞了，理由是正要同阿普尔比家的查德威克（Chadwick）用茶。而论绝对边缘度，阿普尔比家的查德威克之于西摩家的梅里特，恰如上尉之于少尉。林顿为之骇然，并且照直说了。

「你图什么呀？」他问，「这有什么意思？你不可能喜欢那路人。」

「处熟了都是顶好的人。」邓斯特布尔说。

「你倒花了不少工夫才发现这一点。」

「可不是。查德威克属于得后天培养的口味。对了，周四我办一场茶会。你来不来？」

「都要请谁？」林顿警惕地打听。

===Original===

“Well, Chadwick for one; and Merrett and Ruthven and three other chaps.”

“Then,” said Linton with some warmth, “I think you’ll have to do without me. I believe you’re mad.”

And he went off in disgust to the fives-courts.

When on the following Thursday Dunstable walked into Ring’s Stores with his five guests, and demanded six public-school teas, the manager was perhaps justified in allowing a triumphant smile to wander across his face. It was a signal victory for him. “No free list today, sir,” he said. “Entirely suspended.”

“Never mind,” said Dunstable, “I’m good for six shillings.”

“Free list?” said Merrett, as the manager retired, “I didn’t know there was one.”

===Chinese===

「嗯，查德威克算一个；还有梅里特、鲁斯文，外加另外三个小子。」

「那么，」林顿带了几分火气说，「我看你们只好缺我一个了。我看你是疯了。」

说罢，他满怀厌恶地往墙手球场去了。

到了周四，邓斯特布尔领着五位客人走进林格商店，点下六份公学茶，经理容许一张凯旋的微笑在自家脸上信步游走，或许情有可原。这于他是一场辉煌的胜利。「今日不设免单名单，先生，」他说，「无限期全面废止。」

「不碍事，」邓斯特布尔说，「六个先令，我付得起。」

「免单名单？」经理退下去后，梅里特说，「我不知道还有这么回事。」

===Original===

“There isn’t. Only he and I palled up so much the other day that he offered me a tea for nothing.”

“Didn’t you take it?”

“No. I went to Cook’s.”

“Rotten hole, Cook’s. I’m never going there again,” said Chadwick. “You take my tip, Dun, old chap, and come here.”

“Dun, old chap,” smiled amiably.

“I don’t know,” he said, looking up from the teapot, into which he had been pouring water; “you can be certain of the food at Cook’s.”

“What do you mean? So you can here.”

===Chinese===

「本来没有。不过是前两天他跟我哥俩好得紧，白送过我一顿茶。」

「你没领情？」

「没领。我去库克店了。」

「破窝一个，库克店。我再也不去了。」查德威克说，「听我一句劝，老邓伙计，往后就上这儿来。」

「老邓伙计」和蔼可亲地笑了。

「这个么，」他从茶壶上抬起眼来——壶里的水方才刚灌进去，「库克店的东西，你吃着心里有底。」

「你什么意思？这儿的东西也一样啊。」

===Original===

“Oh,” said Dunstable, “I didn’t know. I’ve never had tea here before. But I’ve often heard that American food upsets one sometimes.”

By this time, the tea having stood long enough, he poured out, and the meal began.

Merrett and his friends were hearty feeders, and conversation languished for some time. Then Chadwick leaned back in his chair, and breathed heavily.

“You couldn’t get stuff like that at Cook’s,” he said.

“I suppose it is a bit different,” said Dunstable. “Have any of you … noticed something queer …?”

===Chinese===

「哦，」邓斯特布尔说，「那我就不清楚了。我先前没在这儿用过茶。不过常听人说，美国的吃食，有时候要叫人肠胃造反。」

这时茶已闷到火候，他逐一斟出，茶会开席。

梅里特和他的朋友们都是食量豪迈之辈，席间谈风一时萧条。随后，查德威克往椅背上一靠，重重地喘起气来。

「这样的货色，库克店你可吃不着。」他说。

「我猜是有点不一样。」邓斯特布尔说，「你们有哪位……觉出点什么不对劲……没有？」

===Original===

Merrett stared at Ruthven. Ruthven stared at Merrett.

“I. …” said Merrett.

“D’you know. …” said Ruthven.

Chadwick’s face was a delicate green.

“I believe,” said Dunstable, “the stuff … was … poisoned. I. …”

===Chinese===

梅里特直勾勾地瞪鲁斯文。鲁斯文直勾勾地瞪梅里特。

「我……」梅里特说。

「你可知道……」鲁斯文说。

查德威克的脸呈现出一种雅致的绿。

「我看，」邓斯特布尔说，「这东西……是……下了毒的。我……」

---

===Original===

“Drink this,” said the school doctor, briskly, bending over Dunstable’s bed with a medicine-glass in his hand, “and be ashamed of yourself. The fact is you’ve over-eaten yourself. Nothing more and nothing less. Why can’t you boys be content to feed moderately?”

“I don’t think I ate much, sir,” protested Dunstable. “It must have been what I ate. I went to that new American place.”

“So *you* went there, too? Why, I’ve just come from attending a bilious boy in Mr. Seymour’s house. He said he had been at the American place, too.”

“Was that Merrett, sir? He was one of the party. We were all bad. We can’t all have eaten too much.”

The doctor looked thoughtful.

“H’m. Curious. Very curious. Do you remember what you had?”

===Chinese===

「把这个喝了，」校医利利索索地说，手里端着药杯，俯身到邓斯特布尔的床前，「然后好好害臊去。实情是：你把自己吃撑了。不多，不少，就是吃撑了。你们这帮小子，怎么就不能知足，适度地吃？」

「我觉得我没吃多少啊，先生。」邓斯特布尔抗议道，「准是吃下去的东西有毛病。我去了那家新开的美国铺子。」

「原来*你*也去了？嗨，我刚从西摩先生宿舍出来，看了个呕吐不止的小子。他说他也上那家美国铺子去了。」

「那是梅里特么，先生？他是我们这一伙的。我们全坏了。总不见得人人都吃多了吧。」

医生现出沉思之色。

「唔。蹊跷。蹊跷得很。还记得吃了什么么？」

===Original===

“I had some things the man called buckwheat cakes, with some stuff he said was maple syrup.”

“Bah. American trash.” The doctor was a staunch Briton, conservative in his views both on politics and on food. “Why can’t you boys eat good English food? I must tell the headmaster of this. I haven’t time to look after the school if all the boys are going to poison themselves. You lie still and try to go to sleep, and you’ll be right enough in no time.”

But Dunstable did not go to sleep. He stayed awake to interview Linton, who came to pay him a visit.

“Well,” said Linton, looking down at the sufferer with an expression that was a delicate blend of pity and contempt, “you’ve made a nice sort of ass of yourself, haven’t you! I don’t know if it’s any consolation to you, but Merrett’s just as bad as you are. And I hear the others are, too. So now you see what comes of going to Ring’s instead of Cook’s.”

===Chinese===

「我吃了些那伙计唤作荞麦饼的东西，还浇了一种他说是枫糖浆的什么。」

「呸。美国垃圾。」这位医生是位铁杆英国人，在政治与饮食两端的见解一概保守。「你们这帮小子，怎么就不能吃点正经的英国饭食？这事我必须禀告校长。全校学生要是都打算给自己下毒，我可没工夫照看学校了。你躺着别动，想法子睡一觉，转眼就没事了。」

可邓斯特布尔没有睡觉。他醒着，专候前来探望的林顿。

「好啊，」林顿低头打量这位病人，脸上的表情是怜悯与鄙夷调配得恰到好处的一剂合方，「你可真把自己出息成了一头像样的蠢驴，是不是！不知这算不算个安慰：梅里特病得跟你一样重。听说其余几个也是。这下你可见识到舍库克店而就林格的下场了。」

===Original===

“And now,” said Dunstable, “if you’ve quite finished, you can listen to me for a bit. …”

“So now you know,” he concluded.

Linton’s face beamed with astonishment and admiration.

“Well, I’m hanged,” he said. “You’re a marvel. But how did you know it wouldn’t poison you?”

“I relied on you. You said it wasn’t poison when I asked you in the lab. My faith in you is touching.”

“But why did you take any yourself?”

===Chinese===

「那么现在，」邓斯特布尔说，「你这要是挖苦够了，不妨听我说上一会儿。……」

「这下你全知道了。」他收尾道。

林顿满面放光，又惊又佩。

「我的老天。」他说，「你真是个奇才。可你怎么知道它毒不倒你？」

「我信得过你嘛。在实验室里我问你时，你亲口说的，它没毒。我对你的这份信仰，实在动人。」

「那你自己为什么也吃了一份？」

===Original===

“Sort of idea of diverting suspicion. But the thing isn’t finished yet. Listen.”

Linton left the dormitory five minutes later with a look of a young disciple engaged on some holy mission.

===Chinese===

「算是转移嫌疑的一点思路。不过这事还没完。听着。」

五分钟后，林顿离开了宿舍，那副神情，俨然一位身负神圣使命的年轻门徒。

### Part III / 第三部

===Original===

“You think the food is unwholesome, then?” said the headmaster after dinner that night.

“Unwholesome!” said the school doctor. “It must be deadly. It must be positively lethal. Here we have six ordinary, strong, healthy boys struck down at one fell swoop as if there were a pestilence raging. Why—”

“One moment,” said the headmaster. “Come in.”

A small figure appeared in the doorway.

“Please, sir,” said the figure in the strained voice of one speaking a “piece” which he has committed to memory. “Mr. Seymour says please would you mind letting the doctor come to his house at once because Linton is ill.”

“What!” exclaimed the doctor. “What’s the matter with him?”

===Chinese===

「那么，你是认为那儿的食物不卫生了？」当晚饭后，校长说。

「不卫生！」校医说，「这简直是致命。简直是不折不扣的杀器。瞧瞧咱们这儿：六个平平常常、结结实实、健健康康的小子，一记横扫，齐齐撂倒，跟瘟疫横行一般。岂——」

「且慢，」校长说，「请进。」

一个小小的人影出现在门口。

「回先生，」那人影开口，嗓音发紧，正是背诵烂熟的「课文」之人特有的腔调，「西摩先生说，劳驾，可否请医生即刻到他宿舍去一趟，因为林顿病了。」

「什么！」医生失声道，「他怎么了？」

===Original===

“Please, sir, I believe it’s buckwheat cakes.”

“What! And here’s another of them!”

A second small figure had appeared in the doorway.

“Sir, please, sir,” said the newcomer, “Mr. Bradfield says may the doctor—”

“And what boy is it *this* time?”

“Please, sir, it’s Brown. He went to Ring’s Stores—”

===Chinese===

「回先生，我猜是荞麦饼。」

「什么！这又来了一个！」

第二个小小的人影已在门口现身。

「先生，回先生，」新来者说，「布拉德菲尔德先生（Bradfield）问，医生可不可以——」

「*这回*病的又是哪个小子？」

「回先生，是布朗（Brown）。他去了林格商店——」

===Original===

The headmaster rose.

“Perhaps you had better go at once, Oakes,” he said. “This is becoming serious. That place is a positive menace to the community. I shall put it out of bounds tomorrow morning.”

And when Dunstable and Linton, pale but cheerful, made their way—slowly, as befitted convalescents—to Cook’s two days afterwards, they had to sit on the counter. All the other seats were occupied.

===Chinese===

校长站起身来。

「看这光景，你最好即刻就去一趟，奥克斯（Oakes）。」他说，「事情闹大了。那地方简直是全社区的一大祸害。明早我就把它列为禁区。」

两天后，邓斯特布尔和林顿——脸色苍白而兴致高昂——以康复期病人应有的步速，缓缓挪到库克店，结果只好坐上柜台。其余的座位，全都有主了。

## Author! / 作者！

===Original===

J. S. M. Babington, of Dacre’s House, was on the horns of a dilemma. Circumstances over which he had had no control had brought him, like another Hercules, to the crossroads, and had put before him the choice between pleasure and duty, or, rather, between pleasure and what those in authority called duty. Being human, he would have had little difficulty in making his decision, had not the path of pleasure been so hedged about by danger as to make him doubt whether after all the thing could be carried through.

The facts in the case were these. It was the custom of the mathematical set to which J. S. M. Babington belonged, 4B to wit, to relieve the tedium of the daily lesson with a species of round game which was played as follows. As soon as the master had taken his seat, one of the players would execute a manoeuvre calculated to draw attention on himself, such as dropping a book or upsetting the blackboard. Called up to the desk to give explanation, he would embark on an eloquent speech for the defence. This was the cue for the next player to begin. His part consisted in making his way to the desk and testifying to the moral excellence of his companion, and giving in full the reasons why he should be discharged without a stain upon his character. As soon as he had warmed to his work he would be followed by a third player, and so on until the standing room around the desk was completely filled with a great cloud of witnesses. The duration of the game varied, of course, considerably. On some occasions it could be played through with such success, that the master would enter into the spirit of the thing, and do his best to book the names of all offenders at one and the same time, a feat of no inconsiderable difficulty. At other times matters would come to a head more rapidly. In any case, much innocent fun was to be derived from it, and its popularity was great. On the day, however, on which this story opens, a new master had been temporarily loosed into the room in place of the Rev. Septimus Brown, who had been there as long as the oldest inhabitant could remember. The Rev. Septimus was a wrangler, but knew nothing of the ways of the human boy. His successor, Mr. Reginald Seymour, was a poor mathematician, but a good master. He had been, moreover, a Cambridge Rugger Blue. This fact alone should have ensured him against the customary pleasantries, for a Blue is a man to be respected. It was not only injudicious, therefore, but positively wrong of Babington to plunge against the blackboard on his way to his place. If he had been a student of Tennyson, he might have remembered that the old order is in the habit of changing and yielding place to the new.

===Chinese===

戴克家（Dacre's House）的 J. S. M. 巴宾顿（J. S. M. Babington）正陷于进退两难的窘境。若干全然不由他掌控的情势，把他像又一位赫拉克勒斯（Hercules）似的带到了十字路口，摆在他面前的是享乐与责任之间的抉择——或者更确切地说，是享乐与当权者所谓的责任之间的抉择。既是凡人，他本不难拿定主意，怎奈享乐之路四下里危机丛生，害他拿不准这件事到头来究竟办不办得成。

个中情由如下。J. S. M. 巴宾顿所属的那个数学班——即 4B 班——有个惯技，用来调剂每日功课的单调，玩法如下：老师刚一落座，某位玩家便要耍一手足以把众人目光引到自己身上的招数，比如掉落一本书，或是掀翻黑板。被唤到讲桌前交代缘由时，他便发表一篇雄辩滔滔的辩护词。这就是下一位玩家出场的提示。他的戏份是挤到讲桌跟前，为同伴高尚的品德作证，并逐一详述此人应当无罪开释、清白无损的种种理由。待他渐入佳境，第三位玩家又接踵而上，如此鱼贯不绝，直到讲桌四周的立足之地被一大团如云的见证人挤得满满当当。一局的长短，自然颇有出入。有些场次能一路打得如此圆满，以致老师也入了戏，竭力想一气记下全体肇事者的大名——这可是桩难度不容小觑的绝技。另一些时候，事态则更快图穷匕见。无论如何，这项游戏能产出大量无辜的乐趣，因而极受欢迎。然而在本篇故事开场的这一天，一位新老师被临时投放进教室，顶替塞普蒂默斯·布朗（Septimus Brown）牧师——此公在那里的年头，连资格最老的住户也追忆不迭了。塞普蒂默斯牧师是位剑桥数学优等生（wrangler），却对人类少年的习性一窍不通。他的继任者雷金纳德·西摩（Reginald Seymour）先生数学蹩脚，人却是位好老师。何况他还当过剑桥橄榄球队的Blue（校队荣誉队员）。单凭这一条，便足以保他免受例行的诙谐礼遇，因为Blue是受人敬重的人物。因此，巴宾顿在走向座位的路上一个箭步撞向黑板，不但是失策，简直是原则性的错误。他若研习过丁尼生（Tennyson），或许还记得：旧秩序素有更迭之习，总要让位于新。

===Original===

Mr. Seymour looked thoughtfully for a moment at the blackboard.

“That was rather a crude effort,” he said pleasantly to Babington, “you lack finesse. Pick it up again, please.”

Babington picked it up without protest. Under the rule of the Rev. Septimus this would have been the signal for the rest of the class to leave their places and assist him, but now they seemed to realize that there was a time for everything, and that this was decidedly no time for indoor games.

===Chinese===

西摩先生对着黑板出神地望了片刻。

「手法相当粗陋啊，」他和颜悦色地对巴宾顿说，「你欠缺一点细腻。请把它重新捡起来。」

巴宾顿一声不吭地把它捡了起来。若在塞普蒂默斯牧师治下，这本该是全班离座前来支援的号令；可如今众人似乎都悟到了凡事皆有定时，而眼下断然不是开展室内游戏的时辰。

===Original===

“Thank you,” said Mr. Seymour, when the board was in its place again. “What is your name? Eh, what? I didn’t quite hear.”

“Babington, sir.”

“Ah. You had better come in tomorrow at two and work out examples three hundred to three-twenty in Hall and Knight. There is really plenty of room to walk in between that desk and the blackboard. It only wants practice.”

What was left of Babington then went to his seat. He felt that his reputation as an artistic player of the game had received a shattering blow. Then there was the imposition. This in itself would have troubled him little. To be kept in on a half-holiday is annoying, but it is one of those ills which the flesh is heir to, and your true philosopher can always take his gruel like a man.

===Chinese===

「多谢，」黑板复位之后，西摩先生说，「你叫什么名字？呃，什么？我没太听清。」

「巴宾顿，先生。」

「啊。你最好明天两点过来，把《霍尔与奈特》（Hall and Knight）第三百至第三百二十题做出来。讲桌和黑板之间其实大有余地容人行走，只欠练习罢了。」

巴宾顿剩下的那点残余随后挪回了座位。他感到，自己作为这项游戏的艺术型玩家的声名，挨了粉碎性的一击。接下来还有罚课。这本身倒难不倒他。半日假被关在校中固然气人，但此乃血肉之躯与生俱来的诸般苦难之一，而真正的哲学家向来能像个男子汉似的，把那碗罚粥一饮而尽。

===Original===

But it so happened that by the evening post he had received a letter from a cousin of his, who was a student at Guy’s, and from all accounts was building up a great reputation in the medical world. From this letter it appeared that by a complicated process of knowing people who knew other people who had influence with the management, he had contrived to obtain two tickets for a morning performance of the new piece that had just been produced at one of the theatres. And if Mr. J. S. M. Babington wished to avail himself of the opportunity, would he write by return, and be at Charing Cross Underground bookstall at twenty past two.

Now Babington, though he objected strongly to the drama of ancient Greece, was very fond of that of the present day, and he registered a vow that if the matter could possibly be carried through, it should be. His choice was obvious. He could cut his engagement with Mr. Seymour, or he could keep it. The difficulty lay rather in deciding upon one or other of the alternatives. The whole thing turned upon the extent of the penalty in the event of detection.

That was his dilemma. He sought advice.

===Chinese===

可不巧，晚间邮班给他送来了表哥的一封信。这位表哥在盖伊医院（Guy's）求学，据各方说法，正在医学界挣下一番了不得的声名。据信中所言，经过一番「认识的人又认识别的对经理层有影响的人」的复杂运作，他设法摘到了两张戏票，看某家剧院刚上演的新剧的日场。倘若 J. S. M. 巴宾顿先生有意乘此良机，请即刻回信，并于两点二十分到查令十字（Charing Cross）地铁站的书报摊前候驾。

巴宾顿虽对古希腊的戏剧深恶痛绝，对当今的戏剧却热爱得很，遂立下一誓：此事但凡有一线办成的可能，就务必办成。他的选择显而易见：要么爽西摩先生的约，要么守约。难处倒在两案之间如何定夺。整件事的枢纽，系于一旦败露，刑罚几何。

这便是他的两难。他去请教高明。

===Original===

“I should risk it,” said his bosom friend Peterson.

“I shouldn’t advise you to,” remarked Jenkins.

Jenkins was equally a bosom friend, and in the matter of wisdom in no way inferior to Peterson.

“What would happen, do you think?” asked Babington.

“Sack,” said one authority.

“Jaw, and double impot,” said another.

===Chinese===

「换我就赌一把。」他的挚友彼得森（Peterson）说。

「我劝你别冒这个险。」詹金斯（Jenkins）发表高见。

詹金斯同样是位挚友，且就智慧而论，丝毫不逊于彼得森。

「你说会有什么后果？」巴宾顿问。

「开除。」一位权威断言。

「训一顿，外加双倍罚课。」另一位权威说。

===Original===

“The *Daily Telegraph*,” muttered the tempter in a stage aside, “calls it the best comedy since Sheridan.”

“So it does,” thought Babington. “I’ll risk it.”

“You’ll be a fool if you do,” croaked the gloomy Jenkins. “You’re bound to be caught.” But the Ayes had it. Babington wrote off that night accepting the invitation.

It was with feelings of distinct relief that he heard Mr. Seymour express to another master his intention of catching the twelve-fifteen train up to town. It meant that he would not be on the scene to see him start on the Hall and Knight. Unless luck were very much against him, Babington might reasonably hope that he would accept the imposition without any questions. He had taken the precaution to get the examples finished overnight, with the help of Peterson and Jenkins, aided by a weird being who actually appeared to like algebra, and turned out ten of the twenty problems in an incredibly short time in exchange for a couple of works of fiction (down) and a tea (at a date). He himself meant to catch the one-thirty, which would bring him to town in good time. Peterson had promised to answer his name at roll-call, a delicate operation, in which long practice had made him, like many others of the junior members of the House, no mean proficient.

===Chinese===

「《每日电讯报》，」诱惑者用舞台旁白的腔调低声道，「称它是谢里登（Sheridan）以来最佳喜剧。」

「可不是嘛。」巴宾顿心想。「我赌了。」

「你赌就是傻瓜。」阴郁的詹金斯嘎声道，「你注定会被逮个正着。」但赞成票占了上风。当晚巴宾顿便去信应下了邀请。

他听见西摩先生对另一位老师宣称，要赶十二点一刻的火车进城，心中顿觉宽慰不已。这意味着，西摩先生不会在场看着他动笔做《霍尔与奈特》。只要运气不与他作对到极点，巴宾顿大可指望这桩罚课被照单全收、不问一句。他已未雨绸缪，连夜把习题赶完——彼得森与詹金斯出力相助，另有一位看上去当真心代数如命的怪人加盟，以匪夷所思的速度解出二十题中的十题，报酬是两本小说（赊账）和一顿茶点（改日兑现）。他自己则打算赶一点半的车，时间宽裕地进城。彼得森已应承点名时代他应到——这是一项精细的手术，长期历练已使他——一如宿舍里许多低年级成员——练就颇为不俗的造诣。

---

===Original===

It would be pleasant for a conscientious historian to be able to say that the one-thirty broke down just outside Victoria, and that Babington arrived at the theatre at the precise moment when the curtain fell and the gratified audience began to stream out. But truth, though it crush me. The one-thirty was so punctual that one might have thought that it belonged to a line other than the line to which it did belong. From Victoria to Charing Cross is a journey that occupies no considerable time, and Babington found himself at his destination with five minutes to wait. At twenty past his cousin arrived, and they made their way to the theatre. A brief skirmish with a liveried menial in the lobby, and they were in their seats.

===Chinese===

一位恪尽职守的史家若能如此落笔，想必会颇为惬意：一点半的车偏偏在维多利亚（Victoria）站外抛锚，巴宾顿抵达剧院之时，恰逢大幕落下、心满意足的观众开始鱼贯而出。然而，纵使真理将我压碎，亦须直书。一点半的车准时得让人疑心它根本不属于它所属的那条铁路。从维多利亚到查令十字是一段耗时无多的旅程，巴宾顿抵达碰头地点时，尚有五分钟好等。两点二十分，表哥翩然而至，两人结伴同赴剧院。在门厅里与一名穿号衣的仆役短短交锋一回，他们便落座了。

===Original===

Some philosopher, of extraordinary powers of intuition, once informed the world that the best of things come at last to an end. The statement was tested, and is now universally accepted as correct. To apply the general to the particular, the play came to an end amidst uproarious applause, to which Babington contributed an unstinted quotum, about three hours after it had begun.

“What do you say to going and grubbing somewhere?” asked Babington’s cousin, as they made their way out.

“Hullo, there’s that man Richards,” he continued, before Babington could reply that of all possible actions he considered that of going and grubbing somewhere the most desirable. “Fellow I know at Guy’s, you know,” he added, in explanation. “I’ll get him to join us. You’ll like him, I expect.”

===Chinese===

某位直觉超凡的哲学家，曾向世人宣告：天下至美之物，终有尽时。此论经过检验，如今已被普遍公认为确论。由一般推及个别：开演约三小时后，这出戏在一片沸反盈天的喝彩中落幕——巴宾顿也为这份喝彩贡献了毫不吝惜的一份。

「咱们找地方打一趟牙祭如何？」出场时，巴宾顿的表哥问道。

「喂，那不是理查兹（Richards）嘛。」不等巴宾顿答话——他正要表示，在一切可能的行动之中，他以找地方打牙祭为最上策——表哥又接着说道。「我在盖伊医院的熟人，知道吧。」他补充说明道。「我叫上他一块儿来。我想你会喜欢他的。」

===Original===

Richards professed himself delighted, and shook hands with Babington with a fervour which seemed to imply that until he had met him life had been a dreary blank, but that now he could begin to enjoy himself again. “I should like to join you, if you don’t mind including a friend of mine in the party,” said Richards. “He was to meet me here. By the way, he’s the author of that new piece﻿—*The Way of the World*.”

“Why, we’ve just been there.”

“Oh, then you will probably like to meet him. Here he is.”

As he spoke a man came towards them, and, with a shock that sent all the blood in his body to the very summit of his head, and then to the very extremities of his boots, Babington recognized Mr. Seymour. The assurance of the programme that the play was by Walter Walsh was a fraud. Nay worse, a downright and culpable lie. He started with the vague idea of making a rush for safety, but before his paralysed limbs could be induced to work, Mr. Seymour had arrived, and he was being introduced (oh, the tragic irony of it) to the man for whose benefit he was at that very moment supposed to be working out examples three hundred to three-twenty in Hall and Knight.

===Chinese===

理查兹声称不胜欣喜，同巴宾顿握手握得热切无比，那架势仿佛在说：未遇此人之前，他的人生只是一片凄凉的空白，而今总算可以重新快活起来了。「我很乐意入伙，只要各位不介意我这伙人里再添我的一位朋友。」理查兹说。「他约好了在这儿同我碰头。对了，他就是那出新戏的作者——《世道》（The Way of the World）。」

「咦，我们刚看完的就是这出。」

「哦，那你们八成乐意见他。这就来了。」

说话间，一个男子朝他们走来。一阵惊悸直贯全身，先把他浑身的血液轰上头顶之巅，再轰到靴尖之末——巴宾顿认出了西摩先生。节目单上言之凿凿，说此剧出自沃尔特·沃尔什（Walter Walsh）的手笔，纯属欺诈骗局。不，更糟，是一句彻头彻尾、罪不可赦的谎言。他起了一个模糊的念头，想夺路保命，可不等瘫痪的四肢恢复运转，西摩先生已然驾到，而他正被引见（哦，何其悲怆的反讽）给这位先生——为了这位先生的利益，他此刻本该正在某处埋头演算《霍尔与奈特》第三百至第三百二十题。

===Original===

Mr. Seymour shook hands, without appearing to recognize him. Babington’s blood began to resume its normal position again, though he felt that this seeming ignorance of his identity might be a mere veneer, a wile of guile, as the bard puts it. He remembered, with a pang, a story in some magazine where a prisoner was subjected to what the lighthearted inquisitors called the torture of hope. He was allowed to escape from prison, and pass guards and sentries apparently without their noticing him. Then, just as he stepped into the open air, the chief inquisitor tapped him gently on the shoulder, and, more in sorrow than in anger, reminded him that it was customary for condemned men to remain *inside* their cells. Surely this was a similar case. But then the thought came to him that Mr. Seymour had only seen him once, and so might possibly have failed to remember him, for there was nothing special about Babington’s features that arrested the eye, and stamped them on the brain for all time. He was rather ordinary than otherwise to look at. At tea, as bad luck would have it, the two sat opposite one another, and Babington trembled. Then the worst happened. Mr. Seymour, who had been looking attentively at him for some time, leaned forward and said in a tone evidently devoid of suspicion: “Haven’t we met before somewhere? I seem to remember your face.”

===Chinese===

西摩先生同他握了手，神情间毫无认出他的迹象。巴宾顿的血液开始回归原位，尽管他觉得，这种佯装不识，恐怕只是一层薄薄的伪装——用诗翁的话说，「奸狡之伎」。他心头一紧，想起某本杂志上的一则故事：一名囚犯受了那些兴高采烈的审判官所谓的「希望之刑」。他被放出牢房，一路穿过卫兵与哨位，众人仿佛全未把他放在眼里。可就在他踏进露天空气的一刻，大审判官轻轻拍了拍他的肩，悲多于怒地提醒他：待决之囚循例应留在牢房「里面」。眼下想必正是同类案件。但转念一想，西摩先生统共只见过他一面，兴许压根没记住他——巴宾顿的五官本无任何特别之处，足以叫人一眼触目、永镌脑际。论相貌，他与其说有过人之处，不如说平平无奇。偏上天捉弄，用茶时两人恰恰对桌而坐，巴宾顿抖如筛糠。接着，最坏的事发生了。西摩先生对他凝视有顷，俯身向前，用一种显然绝无猜疑的腔调说道：「咱们从前在哪儿见过吧？我好像记得你这张脸。」

===Original===

“Er﻿—no, no,” replied Babington. “That is, I think not. We may have.”

“I feel sure we have. What school are you at?”

Babington’s soul began to writhe convulsively.

“What, what school? Oh, what *school*? Why, er﻿—I’m at﻿—er﻿—Uppingham.”

Mr. Seymour’s face assumed a pleased expression.

“Uppingham? Really. Why, I know several Uppingham fellows. Do you know Mr. Morton? He’s a master at Uppingham, and a great friend of mine.”

===Chinese===

「呃——不，不，」巴宾顿答道，「就是说，我想没有。也许有过。」

「我敢肯定见过。你在哪所学校？」

巴宾顿的灵魂开始剧烈地扭动。

「什么，哪所学校？哦，哪所『学校』？呃——我在——呃——阿平厄姆（Uppingham）。」

西摩先生的面容浮现出欣悦之色。

「阿平厄姆？真的。嗨，我认识好几位阿平厄姆的人。你认识莫顿（Morton）先生吗？他在阿平厄姆当老师，是我的一位至交。」

===Original===

The room began to dance briskly before Babington’s eyes, but he clutched at a straw, or what he thought was a straw.

“Uppingham? Did I say Uppingham? Of course, I mean Rugby, you know, Rugby. One’s always mixing the two up, you know. Isn’t one?”

Mr. Seymour looked at him in amazement. Then he looked at the others as if to ask which of the two was going mad, he or the youth opposite him. Babington’s cousin listened to the wild fictions which issued from his lips in equal amazement. He thought he must be ill. Even Richards had a fleeting impression that it was a little odd that a fellow should forget what school he was at, and mistake the name Rugby for that of Uppingham, or vice versa. Babington became an object of interest.

“I say, Jack,” said the cousin, “you’re feeling all right, aren’t you? I mean, you don’t seem to know what you’re talking about. If you’re going to be ill, say so, and I’ll prescribe for you.”

“Is he at Rugby?” asked Mr. Seymour.

===Chinese===

房间开始在巴宾顿眼前轻快地旋转，但他死死抓住一根救命稻草——或者他以为是稻草的东西。

「阿平厄姆？我说的是阿平厄姆吗？当然，我是说拉格比（Rugby），知道吧，拉格比。这两家总让人搞混，不是吗？」

西摩先生愕然望着他，又望向旁人，仿佛在问：这二位之中究竟哪一位在发疯，是他自己，还是对面这位少年。巴宾顿的表哥听他从嘴里源源涌出这一派胡言，同样愕然，心想他必是病了。连理查兹也心头掠过一丝印象：一个家伙竟会忘记自己在哪所学校上学，还把拉格比错当成阿平厄姆，或者反过来，未免有几分古怪。巴宾顿成了一桩引人瞩目的对象。

「喂，杰克，」表哥说，「你没什么不舒服吧？我是说，你好像不知道自己在讲什么。要是不舒服就吱一声，我给你开方子。」

「他在拉格比上学？」西摩先生问。

===Original===

“No, of course he’s not. How could he have got from Rugby to London in time for a morning performance? Why, he’s at St. Austin’s.”

Mr. Seymour sat for a moment in silence, taking this in. Then he chuckled. “It’s all right,” he said, “he’s not ill. We have met before, but under such painful circumstances that Master Babington very thoughtfully dissembled, in order not to remind me of them.”

He gave a brief synopsis of what had occurred. The audience, exclusive of Babington, roared with laughter.

“I suppose,” said the cousin, “you won’t prosecute, will you? It’s really such shocking luck, you know, that you ought to forget you’re a master.”

Mr. Seymour stirred his tea and added another lump of sugar very carefully before replying. Babington watched him in silence, and wished that he would settle the matter quickly, one way or the other.

“Fortunately for Babington,” said Mr. Seymour, “and unfortunately for the cause of morality, I am not a master. I was only a stopgap, and my term of office ceased today at one o’clock. Thus the prisoner at the bar gets off on a technical point of law, and I trust it will be a lesson to him. I suppose you had the sense to do the imposition?”

===Chinese===

「不，他当然不在。他怎么可能从拉格比及时赶到伦敦看一场日场戏？嗨，他在圣奥斯汀（St. Austin's）。」

西摩先生默坐片刻，消化这条信息。随即呵呵一笑。「没事，」他说，「他没病。我们先前见过，只是当时的情形太过难堪，巴宾顿少爷体贴入微，特意装作不识，免得勾起我的回忆。」

他把事情始末简要讲了一遍。除巴宾顿以外，满座哄然大笑。

「我想，」表哥说，「你不会提出起诉吧？这运气实在糟得骇人，按理你该忘了自己当老师这一码事。」

西摩先生搅了搅茶，又极尽仔细地添进一块方糖，方才作答。巴宾顿默然注视着他，只盼他快些把这桩公案了结，不管往哪头了结。

「于巴宾顿是万幸，」西摩先生说，「于道德大业则是不幸——我并不是老师。我不过是个临时替工，任期到今天下午一点整届满。于是这位受审被告凭一纸法律技术细则脱了罪，我相信这于他是一课教训。我料你有那个脑子，罚课是做了的吧？」

===Original===

“Yes, sir, I sat up last night.”

“Good. Now, if you’ll take my advice, you’ll reform, or another day you’ll come to a bad end. By the way, how did you manage about roll-call today?”

“I thought that was an awfully good part just at the end of the first act,” said Babington.

Mr. Seymour smiled. Possibly from gratification.

===Chinese===

「做了，先生，我昨晚熬夜赶的。」

「很好。听我一句劝，你还是改过自新吧，不然早晚不得善终。对了，今天点名你是怎么应付过去的？」

「我觉得第一幕结尾那段戏真是妙极了。」巴宾顿说。

西摩先生莞尔一笑。或许是心中得意。

---

===Original===

“Well, how did it go off?” asked Peterson that night.

“Don’t, old chap,” said Babington, faintly.

“I told you so,” said Jenkins at a venture.

But when he had heard the whole story he withdrew the remark, and commented on the wholly undeserved good luck some people seemed to enjoy.

===Chinese===

「喂，事情进行得怎么样？」当晚彼得森问。

「别提了，老兄。」巴宾顿有气无力地说。

「我早说过吧。」詹金斯姑妄言之。

然而听罢全部始末，他收回了自己的话，转而议论起某些人似乎坐享的那种全然不配的好运来。

## Bradshaw’s Little Story / 布拉德肖的小故事

===Original===
The qualities which in later years rendered Frederick Wackerbath Bradshaw so conspicuous a figure in connection with the now celebrated affair of the European, African, and Asiatic Pork Pie and Ham Sandwich Supply Company frauds, were sufficiently in evidence during his school career to make his masters prophesy gloomily concerning his future. The boy was in every detail the father of the man. There was the same genial unscrupulousness, upon which the judge commented so bitterly during the trial, the same readiness to seize an opportunity and make the most of it, the same brilliance of tactics. Only once during those years can I remember an occasion on which Justice scored a point against him. I can remember it, because I was in a sense responsible for his failure. And he can remember it, I should be inclined to think, for other reasons. Our then Headmaster was a man with a straight eye and a good deal of muscular energy, and it is probable that the talented Frederick, in spite of the passage of years, has a tender recollection of these facts.

It was the eve of the Euripides examination in the Upper Fourth. Euripides is not difficult compared to some other authors, but he does demand a certain amount of preparation. Bradshaw was a youth who did less preparation than anybody I have ever seen, heard of, or read of, partly because he preferred to peruse a novel under the table during prep, but chiefly, I think, because he had reduced cribbing in form to such an exact science that he loved it for its own sake, and would no sooner have come tamely into school with a prepared lesson than a sportsman would shoot a sitting bird. It was not the marks that he cared for. He despised them. What he enjoyed was the refined pleasure of swindling under a master’s very eye. At the trial the judge, who had, so ran report, been himself rather badly bitten by the Ham Sandwich Company, put the case briefly and neatly in the words, “You appear to revel in villainy for villainy’s sake,” and I am almost certain that I saw the beginnings of a gratified smile on Frederick’s expressive face as he heard the remark. The rest of our study﻿—the juniors at St. Austin’s pigged in quartets﻿—were in a state of considerable mental activity on account of this Euripides examination. There had been House-matches during the preceding fortnight, and House-matches are not a help to study, especially if you are on the very fringe of the cock-house team, as I was. By dint of practising every minute of spare time, I had got the eleventh place for my fielding. And, better still, I had caught two catches in the second innings, one of them a regular gallery affair, and both off the captain’s bowling. It was magnificent, but it was not Euripides, and I wished now that it had been. Mellish, our form-master, had an unpleasant habit of coming down with both feet, as it were, on members of his form who failed in the book-papers.

===Chinese===
弗雷德里克·瓦克巴思·布拉德肖（Frederick Wackerbath Bradshaw）日后在那桩如今已声名大噪的「欧洲、非洲暨亚洲猪肉派与火腿三明治供应公司」诈骗案中，是个何等惹眼的人物；而成就这份惹眼的种种品性，在他的学生时代便已彰显无遗，害得各位老师纷纷对他的前程作起阴郁的预言。这孩子事无巨细，一概是成年后那个男人的前身。那份和蔼可亲的肆无忌惮——庭审之际法官痛下针砭的正是这一条——那份见到机会便一口咬住、咬住便榨到最足的敏捷，那身战术上的光华，一样不缺。那些年里，我记得正义一方在他手上得分，仅得一回。我记得这桩事，是因为从某种意义上说，他那一回的失利该由我负责。至于他本人，我倾向于认为，他是本着别样的理由记住它的。我们那时的校长，目光端准，膂力充沛；想来那位才华横溢的弗雷德里克尽管岁月流转，对这两桩事实仍怀有温柔的追忆。

其时正值上四年级（Upper Fourth）欧里庇得斯（Euripides）考试的前夜。比起另外几位作家，欧里庇得斯算不得难，但毕竟要求一定分量的准备。布拉德肖这个少年，准备功课之少，在我亲眼见过、亲耳听说、亲阅读到过的一切人等中稳居榜首：一半是因为晚自习时他更乐意在桌子底下攻读小说，但我想，主要还是因为他已把课堂夹带锤炼成一门精确的科学，乃至为夹带而爱夹带——正如运动家决不肯射一只蹲着的鸟，他也决不肯温顺地带着预习好的功课踏进课堂。他在乎的不是分数。分数他是不屑的。他所享受的，是在老师眼皮子底下行骗的那种精微乐趣。庭审时，据传法官本人在火腿三明治公司手里也栽过不小的跟头；他把案子概括得简洁而漂亮：「你似乎惯于为作恶而作恶，且乐在其中。」此言入耳之际，我几乎可以肯定，弗雷德里克那张富于表情的脸上，有一丝心满意足的笑意初绽。我们书房里的其余几位——圣奥斯汀（St. Austin's）的低年级生照例四人合居一间书房——也都因这场欧里庇得斯考试而处于高度的脑力活跃状态。此前两周一直在打宿舍对抗赛，而宿舍赛于学业是无甚帮助的，尤其当你像我一样，正吊在宿舍冠军队正选的边缘上。凭着把每一分钟闲暇都投进练球，我凭守备挣下了正选第十一人的位置。更妙的是，第二局里我接杀了两记，其中一记纯属表演给看台瞧的花活，而且两记都是从队长投出的球上摘下来的。壮则壮矣，惜非欧里庇得斯；如今我倒巴不得它是。我们的班主任梅利什（Mellish）则有个叫人不痛快的习惯：凡在课本卷上失手的班员，他总仿佛双脚齐落，把人家踏个正着。

===Original===
We were working, therefore, under forced draught, and it was distinctly annoying to see the wretched Bradshaw lounging in our only armchair with one of Rider Haggard’s best, seemingly quite unmoved at the prospect of Euripides examinations. For all he appeared to care, Euripides might never have written a line in his life.

Kendal voiced the opinion of the meeting.

“Bradshaw, you worm,” he said. “Aren’t you going to do *any* work?”

“Think not. What’s the good? Can’t get up a whole play of Euripides in two hours.”

“Mellish’ll give you beans.”

“Let him.”

===Chinese===
于是我们都在强风鼓炉之下用功，眼见可恶的布拉德肖歪在我们唯一的扶手椅里，捧着赖德·哈格德（Rider Haggard）一部压卷之作，面对迫在眉睫的欧里庇得斯考试似乎全然无动于衷，实在叫人光火。照他那副神气，欧里庇得斯这辈子一行字没写过也与他毫不相干。

肯德尔（Kendal）道出了与会者的共同意见。

「布拉德肖，你这个懒虫，」他说，「你打算*一点*书都不念吗？」

「我想是不念了。念有什么用？两个钟头也啃不下欧里庇得斯一整本戏。」

「梅利什会叫你好看的。」

「随他。」

===Original===
“You’ll get a jolly bad report.”

“Shan’t get a report at all. I always intercept it before my guardian can get it. He never says anything.”

“Mellish’ll probably run you in to the Old Man,” said White, the fourth occupant of the study.

Bradshaw turned on us with a wearied air.

“Oh, do give us a rest,” he said. “Here you are just going to do a most important exam, and you sit jawing away as if you were paid for it. Oh, I say, by the way, who’s setting the paper tomorrow?”

===Chinese===
「你会拿到一份糟透了的成绩单。」

「我根本拿不到成绩单。我总在监护人到手之前把它半路截下。他从来一个字不提。」

「梅利什多半会把你扭送到老头子（the Old Man，指校长）那儿去。」书房的第四位住户怀特（White）说。

布拉德肖面带倦色，掉过头来对付我们。

「噢，饶了我们吧，」他说，「眼看就要参加一场顶要紧的考试，你们倒坐在这儿白话个没完，仿佛还有人给你们发工钱。哦对了，我说，明天卷子是谁出？」

===Original===
“Mellish, of course,” said White.

“No, he isn’t,” I said. “Shows what a lot you know about it. Mellish is setting the Livy paper.”

“Then, who’s doing this one?” asked Bradshaw.

“Yorke.”

Yorke was the master of the Upper Fifth. He generally set one of the upper fourth book-papers.

===Chinese===
「自然是梅利什。」怀特说。

「不，不是他。」我说，「可见你们对这事知道得多么可怜。梅利什出的是李维（Livy）那份卷。」

「那么这份谁出？」布拉德肖问。

「约克（Yorke）。」

约克是上五年级（Upper Fifth）的班主任。上四年级的课本卷，照例有一份归他出。

===Original===
“Certain?” said Bradshaw.

“Absolutely.”

“Thanks. That’s all I wanted to know. By Jove, I advise you chaps to read this. It’s grand. Shall I read out this bit about a fight?”

“No!” we shouted virtuously, all together, though we were dying to hear it, and we turned once more to the loathsome inanities of the second chorus. If we had been doing Homer, we should have felt more in touch with Bradshaw. There’s a good deal of similarity, when you come to compare them, between Homer and Haggard. They both deal largely in bloodshed, for instance. As events proved, the Euripides paper, like many things which seem formidable at a distance, was not nearly so bad as I had expected. I did a fair-to-moderate paper, and Kendal and White both seemed satisfied with themselves. Bradshaw confessed without emotion that he had only attempted the last half of the last question, and on being pressed for further information, merely laughed mysteriously, and said vaguely that it would be all right.

===Chinese===
「拿得准？」布拉德肖说。

「万无一失。」

「多谢。我想知道的就是这个。嗬，我劝你们几位读读这本书，妙极了。要不要我把讲打斗的这段念出来听听？」

「不要！」我们正气凛然地齐声喝道，尽管心里痒得要命；随即又埋头去对付第二场合唱歌里那些可憎的痴话。倘若我们读的是荷马（Homer），本可以觉得跟布拉德肖更有共同语言。认真比较起来，荷马与哈格德的相似之处着实不少——比如，两位都大量经营流血事件。后来的事实证明，这份欧里庇得斯卷子同许多远看可畏的东西一样，远没有我预想的那般可怕。我答了一份中不溜偏上的卷子，肯德尔和怀特看上去也都对自己满意。布拉德肖不动声色地招认，他只做了末一道题的后半；再被追问详情，他只神秘地一笑，含糊说届时自见分晓。

===Original===
It now became plain that he had something up his sleeve. We expressed a unanimous desire to know what it was.

“You might tell a chap,” I said.

“Out with it, Bradshaw, or we’ll lynch you,” added Kendal.

Bradshaw, however, was not to be drawn. Much of his success in the paths of crime, both at school and afterwards, was due to his secretive habits. He never permitted accomplices.

===Chinese===
这下事情清楚了：他袖中另有乾坤。我们一致表达了想知道那是什么的愿望。

「跟哥们儿说说呗。」我说。

「快招，布拉德肖，不然我们对你动私刑。」肯德尔补上一句。

然而布拉德肖滴水不漏。他在犯罪之路上——无论在校时还是日后——多有建树，很大程度正得力于这种守口如瓶的习惯。他从不容许有同伙。

---

===Original===
On the following Wednesday the marks were read out. Out of a possible hundred I had obtained sixty﻿—which pleased me very much indeed﻿—White, fifty-five, Kendal, sixty-one. The unspeakable Bradshaw’s net total was four.

Mellish always read out bad marks in a hushed voice, expressive of disgust and horror, but four percent was too much for him. He shouted it, and the form yelled applause, until Ponsonby came in from the Upper Fifth next door with Mr. Yorke’s compliments, “and would we recollect that his form were trying to do an examination.”

When order had been restored, Mellish settled his glasses and glared through them at Bradshaw, who, it may be remarked, had not turned a hair.

===Chinese===
接下来那个周三宣读分数。满分一百，我得了六十分——这份成绩实在让我喜出望外——怀特五十五，肯德尔六十一。那位言语不足以形容的布拉德肖，净值四分。

梅利什宣读低分，向来压低嗓音，以示嫌恶与惊骇，但四个百分点超出了这套程序的承受范围。他是吼出来的，全班顿时鼓噪喝彩，直到隔壁上五年级的庞森比（Ponsonby）过来，转达约克先生的问候，「并请我们体念一下，他那个班正经努力考试呢」。

秩序恢复之后，梅利什扶正眼镜，隔着镜片怒视布拉德肖。附带说一句，此君自始至终面不改色。

===Original===
“Bradshaw,” he said, “how do you explain this?”

It was merely a sighting shot, so to speak. Nobody was ever expected to answer the question. Bradshaw, however, proved himself the exception to the rule.

“I can explain, sir,” he said, “if I may speak to you privately afterwards.”

I have seldom seen anyone so astonished as Mellish was at these words. In the whole course of his professional experience, he had never met with a parallel case. It was hard on the poor man not to be allowed to speak his mind about a matter of four percent in a book-paper, but what could he do? He could not proceed with his denunciation, for if Bradshaw’s explanation turned out a sufficient excuse, he would have to withdraw it all again, and vast stores of golden eloquence would be wasted. But, then, if he bottled up what he wished to say altogether, it might do him a serious internal injury. At last he hit on a compromise. He said, “Very well, Bradshaw, I will hear what you have to say,” and then sprang, like the cat in the poem, “all claws,” upon an unfortunate individual who had scored twenty-nine, and who had been congratulating himself that Bradshaw’s failings would act as a sort of lightning-conductor to him. Bradshaw worked off his explanation in under five minutes. I tried to stay behind to listen, on the pretext of wanting to tidy up my desk, but was ejected by request. Bradshaw explained that his statement was private.

===Chinese===
「布拉德肖，」他说，「这个你作何解释？」

不妨说，这不过是一发试射。谁也没被指望回答这个问题。然而布拉德肖证明了自己正是这条通例的例外。

「我能解释，先生，」他说，「如果放学后可以单独同您谈。」

我极少见到谁像梅利什听见这句话时那样惊讶。在他执教生涯的全部历程中，还从未遇到过可比的先例。为一桩课本卷上区区四分的事，竟不许他把心里的话一吐为快，这对那位可怜人未免苛刻；可他又有什么办法？控诉不能照常进行——万一布拉德肖的解释到头来构成充分的托辞，他就得把说过的话尽数收回，满库的金色雄辩就全糟蹋了。可话又说回来，倘若把想说的话整个儿憋回去，恐怕又要在内里造成严重的创伤。末了他想出一条折中之计。他说：「好吧，布拉德肖，你要说什么，我听着。」随即像诗里那只猫一样，「浑身是爪」地扑向一个倒霉蛋——此人得了二十九分，先前正自庆幸布拉德肖的失分能给他起一枚避雷针的作用。布拉德肖不到五分钟便把解释卸载完毕。我借口要收拾课桌，想留下偷听，却被应本人之请驱逐出场——布拉德肖声明，他的陈述属于机密。

===Original===
After a time they came out together like long-lost brothers, Mellish with his hand on Bradshaw’s shoulder. It was some small comfort to me to remember that Bradshaw had the greatest dislike to this sort of thing.

It was evident that Bradshaw, able exponent of the art of fiction that he was, must have excelled himself on this occasion. I tried to get the story out of him in the study that evening. White and Kendal assisted. We tried persuasion first. That having failed, we tried taunts. Then we tried kindness. Kendal sat on his legs, and I sat on his head, and White twisted his arm. I think that we should have extracted something soon, either his arm from its socket or a full confession, but we were interrupted. The door flew open, and Prater (the same being our Housemaster, and rather a good sort) appeared.

“Now then, now then,” he said. Prater’s manner is always abrupt.

“What’s this? I can’t have this. I can’t have this. Get up at once. Where’s Bradshaw?”

===Chinese===
过了一阵，两人一同出来，好似失散多年的兄弟，梅利什还把手搭在布拉德肖肩上。想到布拉德肖平生最厌恶这一套，我心里获得了小小的安慰。

显然，布拉德肖虽说是虚构艺术的一位老练大家，这一回也必定是超水平发挥了。当晚在书房里，我设法从他嘴里把故事挖出来。怀特和肯德尔从旁协助。我们先试了劝导。劝导失败，改试嘲讽。然后试了温情。肯德尔坐上他的双腿，我坐上他的脑袋，怀特拧他的胳膊。我想，要不了多久我们总能榨出点什么——要么是他的胳膊脱臼，要么是全盘招供——可惜进程被打断了。门猛地开了，普拉特（Prater）出现——此公正是我们的舍监，人还挺不错。

「哎，哎。」他说。普拉特的作风向来直截了当。

「这是干什么？这可不行。这可不行。马上起来。布拉德肖呢？」

===Original===
I rose gracefully to my feet, thereby disclosing the classic features of the lost one.

“The Headmaster wants to see you at once, Bradshaw, at the School House. You others had better find something to do, or you will be getting into trouble.”

He and Bradshaw left together, while we speculated on the cause of the summons.

We were not left very long in suspense. In a quarter of an hour Bradshaw returned, walking painfully, and bearing what, to the expert’s eye, are the unmistakable signs of a “touching up,” which, being interpreted, is corporal punishment.

===Chinese===
我优雅地站起身来，从而暴露了失踪者那张古典式的脸。

「校长要立刻见你，布拉德肖，在学舍。你们其余几个最好找点正经事做，不然就要惹麻烦了。」

他和布拉德肖一同走了，我们则猜测起这次传召的缘由。

悬念没有悬挂多久。一刻钟后，布拉德肖回来了，步履维艰，身上带着行家眼里一望而知的「拾掇」痕迹——「拾掇」者，翻译过来就是体罚。

===Original===
“Hullo,” said White, as he appeared, “what’s all this?”

“How many?” enquired the statistically-minded Kendal. “You’ll be thankful for this when you’re a man, Bradshaw.”

“That’s what I always say to myself when I’m touched up,” added Kendal.

I said nothing, but it was to me that the wounded one addressed himself.

===Chinese===
「嗬，」他一露面，怀特便说，「这是唱的哪一出？」

「几下？」具有统计学头脑的肯德尔探询道。「等你长大成人，会感激这一顿的，布拉德肖。」

「每逢我挨拾掇，也总是拿这话宽慰自己。」肯德尔又补一句。

我一言不发，可伤员偏偏拣上了我，冲我发话。

===Original===
“You utter ass,” he said, in tones of concentrated venom.

“Look here, Bradshaw﻿—” I began, protestingly.

“It’s all through you﻿—you idiot,” he snarled. “I got twelve.”

“Twelve isn’t so dusty,” said White, critically. “Most I ever got was six.”

“But why was it?” asked Kendal. “That’s what we want to know. What have you been and gone and done?”

===Chinese===
「你这个十足的蠢驴。」他说，语调里浓缩着怨毒。

「听着，布拉德肖——」我开口抗议。

「全怪你——你这个白痴，」他咬牙切齿，「我吃了十二记。」

「十二下不算糟。」怀特以品鉴的口吻说，「我最惨也就挨过六下。」

「可到底为什么呀？」肯德尔问，「这才是我们想知道的。你究竟闯了什么祸？」

---

===Original===
“It’s about that Euripides paper,” said Bradshaw.

“Ah!” said Kendal.

“Yes, I don’t mind telling you about it now. When Mellish had me up after school today, I’d got my yarn all ready. There wasn’t a flaw in it anywhere as far as I could see. My idea was this. I told him I’d been to Yorke’s room the day before the exam, to ask him if he had any marks for us. That was all right. Yorke was doing the two Unseen papers, and it was just the sort of thing a fellow would do to go and ask him about the marks.”

“Well?”

“Then when I got there he was out, and I looked about for the marks, and on the table I saw the Euripides paper.”

“By Jove!” said Kendal. We began to understand, and to realize that here was a mastermind.

===Chinese===
「为的就是那份欧里庇得斯卷子。」布拉德肖说。

「啊哈！」肯德尔说。

「是，如今我倒不介意跟你们说了。今天放学后梅利什把我叫去，我早已把整套说辞备好。据我看，它通身上下找不出一个破绽。我的思路是这样的。我告诉他，考试前一天我去过约克的房间，问他手上有没有我们的分数。这一层站得住。约克出的是那两份即席卷，跑去问他分数的事，正是一个学生会干出来的那种事。」

「然后呢？」

「然后我到了那儿，他不在。我就四下找分数，一看桌上，摆着那份欧里庇得斯卷子。」

「嗬！」肯德尔说。我们开始懂了，并且意识到眼前这位是位运筹帷幄的大才。

===Original===
“Well, of course, I read it, not knowing what it was, and then, as the only way of not taking an unfair advantage, I did as badly as I could in the exam. That was what I told Mellish. Any beak would have swallowed it.”

“Well, didn’t he?”

“Mellish did all right, but the rotter couldn’t keep it to himself. Went and told the Old Man. The Old Man sent for me. He was as decent as anything at first. That was just his guile. He made me describe exactly where I had seen the paper, and so on. That was rather risky, of course, but I put it as vaguely as I could. When I had finished, he suddenly whipped round, and said, ‘Bradshaw, why are you telling me all these lies?’ That’s the sort of thing that makes you feel rather a wreck. I was too surprised to say anything.”

===Chinese===
「呃，当然啦，我不知道那是什么，就把它读了；随后，作为不占不公平便宜的唯一办法，我在考场上尽量往坏里答。我就是这么对梅利什说的。随便哪位老师都会照单全收。」

「结果他没有？」

「梅利什信得好好的，可那老混蛋憋不住话，跑去告诉了老头子。老头子便传我去。起初他和气得无以复加——那正是他的狡计。他让我详详细细描述是在哪儿看见那份卷子的，等等。这一步当然相当冒险，但我尽量说得含糊。等我说完，他猛然翻脸，来了一句：『布拉德肖，你为什么要对我编这一通谎？』这种事最能让人当场化作一堆残骸。我惊得一句话也说不出来。」

===Original===
“I can guess the rest,” said Kendal. “But how on earth did he know it was all lies? Why didn’t you stick to your yarn?”

“And, besides,” I put in, “where do I come in? I don’t see what I’ve got to do with it.”

Bradshaw eyed me fiercely. “Why, the whole thing was your fault,” he said. “You told me Yorke was setting the paper.”

“Well, so he did, didn’t he?”

“No, he didn’t. The Old Man set it himself,” said Bradshaw, gloomily.

===Chinese===
「剩下的我猜得出了。」肯德尔说，「可他究竟怎么看穿通篇是谎？你当时怎么不咬死不改口？」

「再说，」我插嘴道，「我搭的哪一杠子？我看不出这事跟我有什么相干。」

布拉德肖凶狠地瞪着我。「嘿，整件事全坏在你手里，」他说，「是你告诉我，卷子由约克出。」

「呃，那不正是他出的吗？」

「不，不是他出的。卷子是老头子亲自出的。」布拉德肖阴郁地说。

## Harrison’s Slight Error / 哈里森的小小失误

===Original===
The one o’clock down express was just on the point of starting. The engine-driver, with his hand on the lever, whiled away the moments, like the watchman in *The Agamemnon*, by whistling. The guard endeavoured to talk to three people at once. Porters flitted to and fro, cleaving a path for themselves with trucks of luggage. The Usual Old Lady was asking if she was right for some place nobody had ever heard of. Everybody was saying goodbye to everybody else, and last, but not least, P. St. H. Harrison, of St. Austin’s, was strolling at a leisurely pace towards the rear of the train. There was no need for him to hurry. For had not his friend, Mace, promised to keep a corner seat for him while he went to the refreshment room to lay in supplies? Undoubtedly he had, and Harrison, as he watched the struggling crowd, congratulated himself that he was not as other men. A corner seat in a carriage full of his own particular friends, with plenty of provisions, and something to read in case he got tired of talking﻿—it would be perfect.

So engrossed was he in these reflections, that he did not notice that from the opposite end of the platform a youth of about his own age was also making for the compartment in question. The first intimation he had of his presence was when the latter, arriving first at the door by a short head, hurled a bag on to the rack, and sank gracefully into the identical corner seat which Harrison had long regarded as his own personal property. And to make matters worse, there was no other vacant seat in the compartment. Harrison was about to protest, when the guard blew his whistle. There was nothing for it but to jump in and argue the matter out en route. Harrison jumped in, to be greeted instantly by a chorus of nine male voices. “Outside there! No room! Turn him out!” said the chorus. Then the chorus broke up into its component parts, and began to address him one by one.

===Chinese===
一点钟开行的下行快车眼看就要发车了。司机手搭在操纵杆上，学着《阿伽门农》（*The Agamemnon*）里守望人的样子，靠吹哨子消磨时光。列车长努力同时应付三个人。搬运工推着行李车往来穿梭，硬给自己开出一条路。照例必有的那位老太太，正在打听自己坐这趟车到不到某个谁也没听说过的地方。人人都在同人人道别；最后——但也绝非最不重要——圣奥斯汀（St. Austin's）的 P. St. H. 哈里森（P. St. H. Harrison），正迈着闲适的步子朝列车尾部踱去。他用不着赶。他的朋友梅斯（Mace）不是答应过，趁他去茶点室采办补给，替他留一个角落座位吗？毫无疑问，答应过；哈里森望着苦苦拥挤的人群，暗自庆幸自己不似凡夫俗子。满车厢知交中的一个角落座，吃食充裕，再备上一册读物以防谈兴阑珊——这将十全十美。

他全神贯注于这些盘算，竟未察觉月台另一头也有一位年纪同他相仿的少年，正朝那节车厢进发。直到对方以一鼻之差抢先抵达车门，把提包甩上行李架、优雅地陷进那个哈里森久已视为自家私产的角落座位，他方才头一回得知此人的存在。更糟的是，车厢里再没有别的空座。哈里森正要抗议，列车长吹响了哨子。别无他法，只好跳上车，在路上把这桩公案理论个明白。哈里森跳了上去，立刻受到九条男嗓齐声合唱的迎接。「外头的！没座儿！把他轰出去！」合唱队唱道。随后合唱队分解为各个声部，开始挨个向他致辞。

===Original===
“You rotter, Harrison,” said Babington, of Dacre’s, “what do you come barging in here for? Can’t you see we’re five aside already?”

“Hope you’ve brought a sardine-opener with you, old chap,” said Barrett, the peerless pride of Philpott’s, “ ’cos we shall jolly well need one when we get to the good old Junct-i-on. Get up into the rack, Harrison, you’re stopping the ventilation.”

The youth who had commandeered Harrison’s seat so neatly took another unpardonable liberty at this point. He grinned. Not the timid, deprecating smile of one who wishes to ingratiate himself with strangers, but a good, six-inch grin right across his face. Harrison turned on him savagely.

“Look here,” he said, “just you get out of that. What do you mean by bagging my seat?”

===Chinese===
「你这坏蛋，哈里森，」戴克家（Dacre's）的巴宾顿（Babington）说，「你闯进来做什么？没看见我们这儿五对五早就凑齐了吗？」

「但愿你随身带了开沙丁鱼罐头的小起子，老伙计，」菲尔波特家（Philpott's）那无与伦比的骄傲巴雷特（Barrett）说，「因为等我们到了那可爱的老枢——纽——站，这物件是断断少不得的。上行李架去坐着吧，哈里森，你把风都挡住了。」

那位如此干净利落地征用了哈里森座位的少年，此时又添了一桩不可原谅的放肆。他咧嘴笑了。不是那种有心讨好陌生人的怯生生赔笑，而是横贯脸面、足足六英寸宽的一场大笑。哈里森凶狠地掉头对付他。

「听着，」他说，「你给我从那儿起来。你抢占我的座位，是什么意思？」

===Original===
“Are you a director of this line?” enquired the youth politely. Roars of applause from the interested audience. Harrison began to feel hot and uncomfortable.

“Or only the Emperor of Germany?” pursued his antagonist.

More applause, during which Harrison dropped his bag of provisions, which were instantly seized and divided on the share and share alike system, among the gratified Austinians.

“Look here, none of your cheek,” was the shockingly feeble retort which alone occurred to him. The other said nothing. Harrison returned to the attack.

“Look here,” he said, “are you going to get out, or have I got to make you?”

Not a word did his opponent utter. To quote the bard: “The stripling smiled.” To tell the truth, the stripling smiled inanely.

The other occupants of the carriage were far from imitating his reserve. These treacherous friends, realizing that, for those who were themselves comfortably seated, the spectacle of Harrison standing up with aching limbs for a journey of some thirty miles would be both grateful and comforting, espoused the cause of the unknown with all the vigour of which they were capable.

===Chinese===
「请问您是这条铁路的董事吗？」少年彬彬有礼地问道。兴致盎然的观众轰然喝彩。哈里森开始觉得浑身燥热，坐立不安。

「还是说，仅仅是德意志皇帝？」他的对手乘胜追击。

喝彩声中，哈里森手里那袋吃食落了地，当即被查抄上去，按人人有份、平均分配的体制，分发给了心满意足的众位圣奥斯汀人。

「听着，少来放肆。」——他所能想起的全部回嘴，就这一句，单薄得骇人。对方一言不发。哈里森重整旗鼓。

「听着，」他说，「你是自己出去，还是非得我来请你出去？」

他的对手一字不吐。引诗人一句：「少年郎笑了。」说句实话，这少年郎笑得傻里傻气。

车厢里其余诸公，可绝无效仿他那份缄默之意。这伙背信弃义的朋友意识到：对于自己已然安坐的人来说，眼看哈里森拖着酸痛的四肢、一站三十来英里，实在是既悦目又暖心的大观；于是他们便各尽所能，全力拥护起那位无名氏的事业。

===Original===
“Beastly bully, Harrison,” said Barrett. “Trying to turn the kid out of his seat! Why can’t you leave the chap alone? Don’t you move, kid.”

“Thanks,” said the unknown, “I wasn’t going to.”

“Now you see what comes of slacking,” said Grey. “If you’d bucked up and got here in time you might have bagged this seat I’ve got. By Jove, Harrison, you’ve no idea how comfortable it is in this corner.”

“Punctuality,” said Babington, “is the politeness of princes.”

And again the unknown maddened Harrison with a “best-on-record” grin.

“But, I say, you chaps,” said he, determined as a last resource to appeal to their better feelings (if any), “Mace was keeping this seat for me, while I went to get some grub. Weren’t you, Mace?” He turned to Mace for corroboration. To his surprise, Mace was nowhere to be seen.

===Chinese===
「恶霸，哈里森。」巴雷特说，「想把小家伙从座位上轰走！你就不能放过人家？小家伙，你可千万别动。」

「多谢，」无名氏说，「我也没打算动。」

「现在明白磨蹭的下场了吧。」格雷（Grey）说，「你当初要是麻利点儿、赶个正着，说不定就把我这个座抢到手了。嗬，哈里森，你绝对想不到这个角落坐着有多舒坦。」

「守时，」巴宾顿说，「乃君王之礼。」

无名氏又拿出一记破纪录级的咧嘴大笑，把哈里森气得冒烟。

「可是，我说，各位老兄，」他说，横下心来向诸位的恻隐之心（倘若有的话）作最后一搏，「梅斯是替我留的这个座，我好去买点吃的。是不是，梅斯？」他转向梅斯寻求佐证。令他吃惊的是，梅斯连个影儿都不见。

===Original===
His sympathetic schoolfellows grasped the full humour of the situation as one man, and gave tongue once more in chorus.

“You weed,” they yelled joyfully, “you’ve got into the wrong carriage. Mace is next door.”

And then, with the sound of unquenchable laughter ringing in his ears, Harrison gave the thing up, and relapsed into a disgusted silence. No single word did he speak until the journey was done, and the carriage emptied itself of its occupants at the Junction. The local train was in readiness to take them on to St. Austin’s, and this time Harrison managed to find a seat without much difficulty. But it was a bitter moment when Mace, meeting him on the platform, addressed him as a rotter, for that he had not come to claim the corner seat which he had been reserving for him. They had had, said Mace, a rattling good time coming down. What sort of a time had Harrison had in *his* carriage? Harrison’s reply was not remarkable for its clearness.

The unknown had also entered the local train. It was plain, therefore, that he was coming to the School as a new boy. Harrison began to wonder if, under these circumstances, something might not be done in the matter by way of levelling up things. He pondered. When St. Austin’s station was reached, and the travellers began to stream up the road towards the College, he discovered that the newcomer was a member of his own House. He was standing close beside him, and heard Babington explaining to him the way to Merevale’s. Merevale was Harrison’s Housemaster.

===Chinese===
他那些富于同情的同学们万众一心，领会了局面中全部的幽默，再度齐声开腔。

「你这草包，」他们兴高采烈地嚷道，「你上错车厢了。梅斯在隔壁。」

于是，在那浇灭不掉的笑声萦绕耳畔之中，哈里森把这事撂下了，重新陷入满怀厌恶的沉默。直到旅程终了、车厢在枢纽站将乘客倾倒一空，他再没吐过一个字。接他们去圣奥斯汀的短途慢车已经整装待发，这一回哈里森没费多大周折就觅得了一个座位。可是在月台上迎面撞见他的梅斯开口便骂他坏蛋——怨他不来认领人家一直替他留着的那个角落座——那一刻，滋味真是苦涩。据梅斯说，他们一路过来痛快极了。那么哈里森在*他*那节车厢里，又痛快出个什么名堂？哈里森的回答，算不得以清晰见长。

那无名氏也上了这趟慢车。由此可见，他显然是来学校报到的新生。哈里森开始琢磨：值此情形，此事之中是否可以有所作为，好把账面扳平。他思量着。到了圣奥斯汀站，旅客们开始沿大路涌向学院，这时他发现那新生竟是自己宿舍的人——他正好站在近旁，听见巴宾顿在给他指点去梅雷维尔家（Merevale's）的路。梅雷维尔（Merevale）便是哈里森的舍监。

===Original===
It was two minutes after he had found out this fact that the Grand Idea came to Harrison. He saw his way now to a revenge so artistic, so beautifully simple, that it was with some difficulty that he restrained himself from bursting into song. For two pins, he felt, he could have done a cakewalk.

He checked his emotion. He beat it steadily back, and quenched it. When he arrived at Merevale’s, he went first to the matron’s room. “Has Venables come back yet?” he asked.

Venables was the head of Merevale’s House, captain of the School cricket, wing three-quarter of the School Fifteen, and a great man altogether.

===Chinese===
查明这一事实两分钟后，那条宏图大计便降临到哈里森脑中。此刻他看清了自己的复仇之路，如此艺术，又简洁得如此漂亮，害他费了好一番力气才按捺住没有放声高歌。他觉得只消再给他两个铜板，他就能当场走一段步态舞（cakewalk）。

他约束住了情绪。他把情绪稳稳击退，又一把浇熄。到得梅雷维尔家，他先去舍监太太屋里。「维纳布尔斯（Venables）回来了吗？」他问。

维纳布尔斯是梅雷维尔家的宿舍之首，校板球队队长，校十五人橄榄球队（School Fifteen）的翼锋，总而言之是宿舍里的一位大人物。

===Original===
“Yes,” said the matron, “he came back early this afternoon.”

Harrison knew it. Venables always came back early on the last day of the holidays.

“He was upstairs a short while ago,” continued the matron. “He was putting his study tidy.”

Harrison knew it. Venables always put his study tidy on the last day of the holidays. He took a keen and perfectly justifiable pride in his study, which was the most luxurious in the House.

“Is he there now?” asked Harrison.

===Chinese===
「回来了，」舍监太太说，「今天下午早早就回来了。」

这他早知道。假期最后一天，维纳布尔斯总是回得早。

「方才他还上楼来着，」舍监太太接着说，「收拾他的书房来着。」

这他早知道。假期最后一天，维纳布尔斯总要把书房收拾停当。他对自己的书房怀有一种热切而全然师出有名的自豪——那是全宿舍最豪华的一间。

「他这会儿在那儿吗？」哈里森问。

===Original===
“No. He has gone over to see the Headmaster.”

“Thanks,” said Harrison, “it doesn’t matter. It wasn’t anything important.”

He retired triumphant. Things were going excellently well for his scheme.

His next act was to go to the fags’ room, where, as he had expected, he found his friend of the train. Luck continued to be with him. The unknown was alone.

“Hullo!” said Harrison.

===Chinese===
「不在。他过校长那儿去了。」

「谢谢，」哈里森说，「不打紧。不是什么要紧事。」

他凯旋而退。他的计划进展得顺遂极了。

他的下一步行动是去听差房（fags' room）。不出所料，他在那里找到了火车上结下的那位「朋友」。运气仍旧站在他这边。无名氏独自一人。

「喂！」哈里森说。

===Original===
“Hullo!” said the fellow-traveller. He had resolved to follow Harrison’s lead. If Harrison was bringing war, then war let it be. If, however, his intentions were friendly, he would be friendly too.

“I didn’t know you were coming to Merevale’s. It’s the best House in the School.”

“Oh!”

“Yes, for one thing, everybody except the kids has a study.”

“What? Not really? Why, I thought we had to keep to this room. One of the chaps told me so.”

===Chinese===
「喂！」同车旅人说。他已决意唯哈里森马首是瞻。哈里森若是携战争而来，那就战争好了；然而他若心怀善意，他也报以善意。

「我还不知道你要来梅雷维尔家呢。这是全校最好的宿舍。」

「哦！」

「是啊，单说一条：这里除了小豆丁，人人都有书房。」

「什么？真的？我还以为我们只能守着这间屋子呢。有位哥们儿是这么跟我说的。」

===Original===
“Trying to green you, probably. You must look out for that sort of thing. I’ll show you the way to your study, if you like. Come along upstairs.”

“Thanks, awfully. It’s awfully good of you,” said the gratified unknown, and they went upstairs together.

One of the doors which they passed on their way was open, disclosing to view a room which, though bare at present, looked as if it might be made exceedingly comfortable.

“That’s my den,” said Harrison. It was perhaps lucky that Graham, to whom the room belonged, in fact, as opposed to fiction, did not hear the remark. Graham and Harrison were old and tried foes. “This is yours.” Harrison pushed open another door at the end of the passage.

===Chinese===
「八成是想涮你。这一类把戏你可得留神。你若愿意，我领你去你的书房。跟我上楼吧。」

「多谢多谢，你真是太好啦。」心满意足的无名氏说，两人便一同上了楼。

途中经过的一扇房门敞开着，露出一间屋子：眼下虽然空空如也，看去却大可布置得极其舒服。

「那是我的窝。」哈里森说。这间屋子事实上的主人——而非虚构中的主人——格雷厄姆（Graham）没有听见这句话，或许是件幸事。格雷厄姆和哈里森是久经考验的老对头。「这间是你的。」哈里森推开走廊尽头的另一扇门。

===Original===
His companion stared blankly at the Oriental luxury which met his eye. “But, I say,” he said, “are you sure? This seems to be occupied already.”

“Oh, no, that’s all right,” said Harrison, airily. “The chap who used to be here left last term. He didn’t know he was going to leave till it was too late to pack up all his things, so he left his study as it was. All you’ve got to do is to cart the things out into the passage and leave them there. The Moke’ll take ’em away.”

The Moke was the official who combined in a single body the duties of butler and bootboy at Merevale’s House. “Oh, right-ho!” said the unknown, and Harrison left him.

===Chinese===
他的同伴呆呆地凝视映入眼帘的那派东方豪奢。「可是，我说，」他道，「你拿得准吗？这屋子看着分明已经有人住了。」

「噢，不，没关系的。」哈里森轻描淡写地说，「原先在这儿的那位上学期走了。他自己也是直到临走才知道要走，来不及把东西统统打点，书房便原样留下了。你只消把这些物件搬到走廊里搁着。穆克（the Moke）自会来弄走。」

穆克是梅雷维尔家那位将管家与擦鞋小厮两份职责集于一身的校工。「噢，好嘞！」无名氏说。哈里森便丢下他走了。

===Original===
Harrison’s idea was that when Venables returned and found an absolute stranger placidly engaged in wrecking his carefully-tidied study, he would at once, and without making inquiries, fall upon that absolute stranger and blot him off the face of the earth. Afterwards it might possibly come out that he, Harrison, had been not altogether unconnected with the business, and then, he was fain to admit, there might be trouble. But he was a youth who never took overmuch heed for the morrow. Sufficient unto the day was his motto. And, besides, it was distinctly worth risking. The main point, and the one with which alone the House would concern itself, was that he had completely taken in, scored off, and overwhelmed the youth who had done as much by him in the train, and his reputation as one not to be lightly trifled with would be restored to its former brilliance. Anything that might happen between himself and Venables subsequently would be regarded as a purely private matter between man and man, affecting the main point not at all.

About an hour later a small Merevalian informed Harrison that Venables wished to see him in his study. He went. Experience had taught him that when the Head of the House sent for him, it was as a rule as well to humour his whim and go. He was prepared for a good deal, for he had come to the conclusion that it was impossible for him to preserve his incognito in the matter, but he was certainly not prepared for what he saw.

===Chinese===
哈里森的算盘是：待维纳布尔斯归来，发觉一个十足的外人正心平气和地洗劫他精心收拾停当的书房，必然不问情由，当即扑向那十足的外人，将其从地球表面抹去。事后或许会东窗事发，查出他哈里森与本案不无牵连；到那时，他不得不承认，可能有麻烦。但他是个从不为明日过分操心的少年，「一天的难处一天当」便是他的座右铭。况且，这险分明值得一冒。要紧的是——宿舍上下关心的也仅此一端——他把火车上那位如法炮制过他的少年彻头彻尾地蒙了、挫了、压倒了，他「不可轻易招惹」的名声便将重焕昔日的光华。至于他本人与维纳布尔斯之间日后无论发生什么，都将被视为男人与男人之间纯然的私事，于主旨毫发无损。

约莫一个钟头后，梅雷维尔家的一个小个子来通知哈里森：维纳布尔斯要在书房见他。他去了。经验教训过他：宿舍之首相召，照例还是迁就一下这兴致、走一遭为妙。他已作了相当的准备，因为他断定，这件事里自己是瞒不住身份的；但他所见到的景象，他可万万没有准备。

===Original===
Venables and the stranger were seated in two armchairs, apparently on the very best of terms with one another. And this, in spite of the fact that these two armchairs were the only furniture left in the study. The rest, as he had noted with a grin before he had knocked at the door, was picturesquely scattered about the passage.

“Hullo, Harrison,” said Venables, “I wanted to see you. There seems to have been a slight mistake somewhere. Did you tell my brother to shift all the furniture out of the study?”

===Chinese===
维纳布尔斯和那陌生人分坐两把扶手椅，看那光景，彼此的交情好得无以复加。而这还是在书房里仅剩的家具便是这两把扶手椅的情况下。至于其余家什——他敲门之前已咧着嘴清点过了——正错落有致地散布在走廊里。

「喂，哈里森，」维纳布尔斯说，「我正要见你。这中间似乎出了点小小的差错。是你叫我弟弟把书房的家具统统搬到外面去的吗？」

===Original===
Harrison turned a delicate shade of green.

“Your﻿—er﻿—brother?” he gurgled.

“Yes. I ought to have told you my brother was coming to the Coll this term. I told the Old Man and Merevale and the rest of the authorities. Can’t make out why I forgot you. Slipped my mind somehow. However, you seem to have been doing the square thing by him, showing him round and so on. Very good of you.”

Harrison smiled feebly. Venables junior grinned. What seemed to Harrison a mystery was how the brothers had managed to arrive at the School at different times. The explanation of which was in reality very simple. The elder Venables had been spending the last week of the holidays with MacArthur, the captain of the St. Austin’s Fifteen, the same being a day boy, suspended within a mile of the School.

“But what I can’t make out,” went on Venables, relentlessly, “is this furniture business. To the best of my knowledge I didn’t leave suddenly at the end of last term. I’ll ask if you like, to make sure, but I fancy you’ll find you’ve been mistaken. Must have been thinking of someone else. Anyhow, we thought you must know best, so we lugged all the furniture out into the passage, and now it appears there’s been a mistake of sorts, and the stuff ought to be inside all the time. So would you mind putting it back again? We’d help you, only we’re going out to the shop to get some tea. You might have it done by the time we get back. Thanks, awfully.”

===Chinese===
哈里森的面色泛起一层精妙的绿。

「你的——呃——弟弟？」他咕噜道。
