“*Under* the bed? What’s the good of that? Did you go over every inch of the strip of carpet leading to the chair with a magnifying-glass?”

“Hadn’t got a magnifying-glass.”

===Chinese===
「怎么样？」他回来时斯科特说。「有线索么？」

「没有。」

「失窃现场你彻底勘察过了？」

「我看了床底下。」

「床*底下*？那顶什么用？那条通向椅子的地毯，你可曾拿着放大镜逐寸检视过？」

「没放大镜。」

===Original===
“Then you’d better buck up and get one, if you’re going to be a detective. Do you think Sherlock Holmes ever moved a step without his? Not much. Well, anyhow. Did you find any footprints or tobacco ash?”

“There was a jolly lot of dust about.”

“Did you preserve a sample?”

“No.”

“My word, you’ve a lot to learn. Now, weighing the evidence, does anything strike you?”

“No.”

===Chinese===
「那你要当侦探，最好麻利点儿弄一柄来。你以为夏洛克·福尔摩斯哪一步离得开他的放大镜？休想。好吧，且不提这个。你可发现脚印或烟灰了？」

「四下里倒有一大摊灰。」

「你留样了没有？」

「没有。」

「我的天，你要学的还多着呢。现在，把证据掂量掂量，可有什么触动了你？」

「没有。」

===Original===
“You’re a bright sort of sleuthhound, aren’t you! It seems to me I’m doing all the work on this case. I’ll have to give you another leg-up. Considering the time when the quid disappeared, I should say that somebody in the dormitory must have collared it. How many fellows are there in Evans’ dormitory?”

“I don’t know.”

“Cut along and find out.”

The detective reluctantly trudged off once more.

===Chinese===
「你可是条机灵的警犬，是不是！照我看，这案子全是我一人代劳。我只好再扶你一把。考虑到那一镑钱失踪的时辰，我得说，必是宿舍里某个人顺手牵了羊。埃文斯的宿舍里有几个人？」

「我不知道。」

「快去查明白。」

这位侦探又一次老大不情愿地蹭出去了。

===Original===
“Well?” said Scott, on his return.

“Seven,” said Pillingshot. “Counting Evans.”

“We needn’t count Evans. If he’s ass enough to steal his own quids, he deserves to lose them. Who are the other six?”

“There’s Trent. He’s prefect.”

“The Napoleon of Crime. Watch his every move. Yes?”

===Chinese===
「怎么样？」他回来时斯科特说。

「七个，」皮林肖特说。「连埃文斯算在内。」

「埃文斯不用算。他要是蠢到偷自己的钱，丢了也活该。另外六个是谁？」

「有个特伦特（Trent）。他是级长。」

「犯罪界的拿破仑（the Napoleon of Crime）。盯住他的一举一动。还有？」

===Original===
“Simms.”

“A dangerous man. Sinister to the core.”

“And Green, Berkeley, Hanson, and Daubeny.”

“Every one of them well known to the police. Why, the place is a perfect Thieves’ Kitchen. Look here, we must act swiftly, young Pillingshot. This is a black business. We’ll take them in alphabetical order. Run and fetch Berkeley.”

===Chinese===
「西姆斯（Simms）。」

「危险人物。坏到了骨髓里。」

「还有格林（Green）、伯克利（Berkeley）、汉森（Hanson）和多贝尼（Daubeny）。」

「个个都是警局挂号的老相识。好家伙，这地方简直是一座十全十美的贼窝（Thieves' Kitchen）。听着，我们必须行动神速，小皮林肖特。这是桩黑道勾当。咱们按字母顺序开审。跑去把伯克利带来。」

===Original===
Berkeley, interrupted in a game of Halma, came unwillingly.

“Now then, Pillingshot, put your questions,” said Scott. “This is a black business, Berkeley. Young Evans has lost a sovereign—”

“If you think I’ve taken his beastly quid—!” said Berkeley warmly.

“Make a note that, on being questioned, the man Berkeley exhibited suspicious emotion. Go on. Jam it down.”

Pillingshot reluctantly entered the statement under Berkeley’s indignant gaze.

“Now then, carry on.”

===Chinese===
伯克利正下哈尔马棋（Halma，一种跳棋），半局被搅，老大不愿意地来了。

「好了，皮林肖特，发问吧，」斯科特说。「这是桩黑道勾当，伯克利。小埃文斯丢了一枚沙弗林金币——」

「你要是以为我拿了他那混账的一镑钱——！」伯克利满腔热愤地说。

「记下来：受审时，伯克利其人情绪激愤，形迹可疑。接着问。快记下去。」

皮林肖特在伯克利怒目而视之下，老大不情愿地录下了这条供词。

「好了，继续。」

===Original===
“You know, it’s all rot,” protested Pillingshot. “I never said Berkeley had anything to do with it.”

“Never mind. Ask him what his movements were on the night of the—what was yesterday?—on the night of the sixteenth of July.”

Pillingshot put the question nervously.

“I was in bed, of course, you silly ass.”

“Were you asleep?” inquired Scott.

===Chinese===
「你可知道，这全是胡闹，」皮林肖特抗议道。「我从来没说伯克利与此事有何相干。」

「不必管。问问他，在——昨天是几号来着？——在七月十六日夜里，他的行踪如何。」

皮林肖特战战兢兢地把问题提了出来。

「我自然是在床上，你这头蠢驴。」

「你睡着了没有？」斯科特盘问道。

===Original===
“Of course I was.”

“Then how do you know what you were doing? Pillingshot, make a note of the fact that the man Berkeley’s statement was confused and contradictory. It’s a clue. Work on it. Who’s next? Daubeny. Berkeley, send Daubeny up here.”

“All right, Pillingshot, you wait,” was Berkeley’s exit speech.

Daubeny, when examined, exhibited the same suspicious emotion that Berkeley had shown; and Hanson, Simms, and Green behaved in a precisely similar manner.

“This,” said Scott, “somewhat complicates the case. We must have further clues. You’d better pop off now, Pillingshot. I’ve got a Latin Prose to do. Bring me reports of your progress daily, and don’t overlook the importance of trifles. Why, in ‘Silver Blaze’ it was a burnt match that first put Holmes on the scent.”

===Chinese===
「自然睡着了。」

「那你怎么知道自己当时在做什么？皮林肖特，记下这一事实：伯克利其人供词混乱，自相矛盾。这是条线索。顺着查下去。下一个是谁？多贝尼。伯克利，打发多贝尼上来。」

「好啊，皮林肖特，你等着，」这便是伯克利的退场辞。

多贝尼受审时，表现出了与伯克利如出一辙的形迹可疑的情绪；汉森、西姆斯和格林的举止也分毫不差地相仿。

「如此一来，」斯科特说，「案情便有些复杂了。我们必须搜罗更多线索。你现在最好退下，皮林肖特。我还有一篇拉丁散文要写。每日向我汇报进展，且莫小看琐细之物的分量。要知道，在《银色马》（'Silver Blaze'）里，正是半根烧过的火柴，最先引福尔摩斯上了嗅迹。」

===Original===
Entering the junior day-room with some apprehension, the sleuthhound found an excited gathering of suspects waiting to interview him.

One sentiment animated the meeting. Each of the five wanted to know what Pillingshot meant by it.

“What’s the row?” queried interested spectators, rallying round.

“That cad Pillingshot’s been accusing us of bagging Evans’ quid.”

“What’s Scott got to do with it?” inquired one of the spectators.

Pillingshot explained his position.

===Chinese===
这条警犬提心吊胆地走进低年级活动室，发现一群群情激愤的嫌疑犯正候着他，要与他面谈。

会议被同一种情绪鼓动着：五个人个个都想知道，皮林肖特这是什么意思。

「出什么事了？」看热闹的人围拢上来，好奇地打听。

「那个浑蛋皮林肖特，硬赖我们偷了埃文斯的一镑钱。」

「这跟斯科特有什么相干？」一个看热闹的问。

皮林肖特把自己的处境交代了一番。

===Original===
“All the same,” said Daubeny, “you needn’t have dragged us into it.”

“I couldn’t help it. He made me.”

“Awful ass, Scott,” admitted Green.

Pillingshot welcomed this sign that the focus of popular indignation was being shifted.

“Shoving himself into other people’s business,” grumbled Pillingshot.

“Trying to be funny,” Berkeley summed up.

===Chinese===
「话虽如此，」多贝尼说，「你也犯不着把我们拖下水。」

「我没办法。是他逼的。」

「斯科特真是头大蠢驴，」格林深表赞同。

众怒的矛头眼看要转移——这一迹象，皮林肖特求之不得。

「专爱把手伸进别人的闲事，」皮林肖特咕哝道。

「一味想耍滑稽，」伯克利总结道。

===Original===
“Rotten at cricket, too.”

“Can’t play a yorker for nuts.”

“See him drop that sitter on Saturday?”

So that was all right. As far as the junior day-room was concerned, Pillingshot felt himself vindicated.

But his employer was less easily satisfied. Pillingshot had hoped that by the next day he would have forgotten the subject. But, when he went into the study to get tea ready, up it came again.

===Chinese===
「板球也打得稀烂。」

「约克球一概没法招架。」

「星期六他把那个送到嘴边的球都漏了，瞧见没有？」

如此便万事大吉。就低年级活动室一方面言，皮林肖特自觉已雪清冤屈。

无奈他的雇主可不这么容易打发。皮林肖特原指望到第二天，他就把这事忘个干净。可是待他进书房预备茶点时，话头又被提了起来。

===Original===
“Any clues yet, Pillingshot?”

Pillingshot had to admit that there were none.

“Hullo, this won’t do. You must bustle about. You must get your nose to the trail. Have you cross-examined Trent yet? No? Well, there you are, then. Nip off and do it now.”

“But, I say, Scott! He’s a prefect!”

“In the dictionary of crime,” said Scott sententiously, “there is no such word as prefect. All are alike. Go and take down Trent’s statement.”

===Chinese===
「有线索了没有，皮林肖特？」

皮林肖特只得承认一无所获。

「喂，这可不成。你得奔走起来。你得把鼻子贴上嗅迹。特伦特你盘问过了没有？没有？瞧，症结就在这儿。赶紧去，这就办。」

「可是，我说，斯科特！他是级长啊！」

「在罪案的辞典里，」斯科特一本正经地说，「可没有『级长』这个词。人人一视同仁。去，把特伦特的口供录下来。」

===Original===
To tax a prefect with having stolen a sovereign was a task at which Pillingshot’s imagination boggled. He went to Trent’s study in a sort of dream.

A hoarse roar answered his feeble tap. There was no doubt about Trent being in. Inspection revealed the fact that the prefect was working and evidently ill-attuned to conversation. He wore a haggard look and his eye, as it caught that of the collector of statements, was dangerous.

“Well?” said Trent, scowling murderously.

Pillingshot’s legs felt perfectly boneless.

“*Well?*” said Trent.

Pillingshot yammered.

===Chinese===
指控一位级长偷了金币——这样的差事，皮林肖特的想象力光是想想便直打退堂鼓。他恍恍惚惚地去了特伦特的书房。

他怯生生叩了叩门，里面一声嘶吼权作应答。特伦特在不在，已毫无疑义。观察表明，这位级长正在用功，且显然无意与人叙话。他面容憔悴，那目光一触到这位口供采集员，便露出凶险之色。

「怎么样？」特伦特说，眉头紧锁，杀气腾腾。

皮林肖特的两条腿顿觉寸骨皆无。

「*怎么样？*」特伦特又说。

皮林肖特呜哩呜噜起来。

===Original===
“*Well?*”

The roar shook the window, and Pillingshot’s presence of mind deserted him altogether.

“Have you bagged a sovereign?” he asked.

There was an awful silence, during which the detective, his limbs suddenly becoming active again, banged the door, and shot off down the passage.

He reentered Scott’s study at the double.

===Chinese===
「*怎——么——样？*」

吼声震得窗玻璃直颤，皮林肖特的镇定彻底弃他而去。

「你偷没偷过一枚沙弗林金币？」他问。

接下来是一片骇人的寂静。趁着这片寂静，侦探的四肢突然重新活络起来，砰地撞上门，顺着走廊一溜烟射了出去。

他一路飞奔，重新进了斯科特的书房。

===Original===
“Well?” said Scott. “What did he say?”

“Nothing.”

“Get out your notebook, and put down, under the heading ‘Trent’: ‘Suspicious silence.’ A very bad lot, Trent. Keep him under constant espionage. It’s a clue. Work on it.”

Pillingshot made a note of the silence, but later on, when he and the prefect met in the dormitory, felt inclined to erase it. For silence was the last epithet one would have applied to Trent on that occasion. As he crawled painfully into bed Pillingshot became more than ever convinced that the path of the amateur detective was a thorny one.

This conviction deepened next day.

===Chinese===
「怎么样？」斯科特说。「他说什么了？」

「什么也没说。」

「掏出你的记事本，在『特伦特』条目下记上：『沉默，可疑。』特伦特此人，绝非善类。对他保持不间断的监视。这是条线索。顺着查下去。」

皮林肖特把这条沉默记了下来，可是稍后，当他和这位级长在宿舍里碰头时，却直想把它抹掉。因为在那一场合，「沉默」恰是人们最不可能加诸特伦特头上的字眼。当他忍着周身疼痛爬上床时，皮林肖特比以往任何时候都更加确信：业余侦探之路，是一条荆棘之路。

次日，这一信念愈发根深蒂固了。

===Original===
Scott’s help was possibly well meant, but it was certainly inconvenient. His theories were of the brilliant, dashing order, and Pillingshot could never be certain who and in what rank of life the next suspect would be. He spent that afternoon shadowing the Greaser (the combination of boot-boy and butler who did the odd jobs about the school house), and in the evening seemed likely to be about to move in the very highest circles. This was when Scott remarked in a dreamy voice, “You know, I’m told the old man has been spending a good lot of money lately. …”

To which the burden of Pillingshot’s reply was that he would do anything in reason, but he was blowed if he was going to cross-examine the headmaster.

“It seems to me,” said Scott sadly, “that you don’t *want* to find that sovereign. Don’t you like Evans, or what is it?”

It was on the following morning, after breakfast, that the close observer might have noticed a change in the detective’s demeanour. He no longer looked as if he were weighed down by a secret sorrow. His manner was even jaunty.

===Chinese===
斯科特的协助兴许是出于好意，但必定碍事。他的理论尽属于那种辉煌而鲁莽的一路，皮林肖特永远拿不准，下一个嫌疑犯会是谁、又身居何种阶层。那天下午，他的光阴全花在跟踪「油子」（the Greaser，此公集擦靴小厮与管家于一身，包揽学舍里的一切杂活）上，到了晚上，看光景他竟要打入顶顶上流的圈子了。因为斯科特用如梦似幻的声调说道：「你可知道，我听说老头子近来花起钱来可是大手大脚的。……

对此，皮林肖特的答话大意是：合乎情理的事他无有不从，可要是叫他去盘问校长，那他宁可给雷劈了。

「照我看，」斯科特悲哀地说，「你根本就*不想*找回那枚金币。你是不喜欢埃文斯呢，还是怎么的？」

到了第二天早晨，早饭之后，眼光锐利的观察者兴许会察觉这位侦探举止有变。他不再一副被隐秘哀愁压弯了腰的模样，风度甚至称得上轻快活泼。

===Original===
Scott noticed it.

“What’s up?” he inquired. “Got a clue?”

Pillingshot nodded.

“What is it? Let’s have a look.”

“Sh—h—h!” said Pillingshot mysteriously.

===Chinese===
斯科特便察觉到了。

「怎么回事？」他问。「摸着线索了？」

皮林肖特点点头。

「是什么？拿出来瞧瞧。」

「嘘——！」皮林肖特神秘兮兮地说。

===Original===
Scott’s interest was aroused. When his fag was making tea in the afternoon, he questioned him again.

“Out with it,” he said. “What’s the point of all this silent mystery business?”

“Sherlock Holmes never gave anything away.”

“Out with it.”

“Walls have ears,” said Pillingshot.

“So have you,” replied Scott crisply, “and I’ll smite them in half a second.”

===Chinese===
斯科特的兴致被吊了起来。下午他的学仆沏茶时，他又盘问了一遍。

「快招吧，」他说。「这一套不声不响的神秘把戏，究竟是什么名堂？」

「夏洛克·福尔摩斯从来不动声色、不露口风。」

「快招。」

「隔墙有耳，」皮林肖特说。

「你也有耳，」斯科特干脆利落地回敬道，「再过半秒钟，我就要叫它们挨拍子了。」

===Original===
Pillingshot sighed resignedly, and produced an envelope. From this he poured some dried mud.

“Here, steady on with my tablecloth,” said Scott. “What’s this?”

“Mud.”

“What about it?”

“Where do you think it came from?”

“How should I know? Road, I suppose.”

===Chinese===
皮林肖特听天由命地叹了口气，掏出一只信封，从里面倒出一些干泥巴来。

「喂，我的桌布可经不起这么折腾，」斯科特说。「这是什么？」

「泥巴。」

「泥巴又怎样？」

「你猜它是打哪儿来的？」

「我怎么会知道？路上呗，想必。」

===Original===
Pillingshot smiled faintly.

“Eighteen different kinds of mud about here,” he said patronisingly. “This is flowerbed mud from the house front-garden.”

“Well? What about it?”

“Sh—h—h!” said Pillingshot, and glided out of the room.

===Chinese===
皮林肖特淡淡一笑。

「这一带共有十八种不同的泥巴，」他屈尊俯就地说道。「这一种，是学舍前院花坛里的泥。」

「嗯？那又如何？」

「嘘——！」皮林肖特说罢，飘然出了房门。

---

===Original===
“Well?” asked Scott next day. “Clues pouring in all right?”

“Rather.”

“What? Got another?”

Pillingshot walked silently to the door and flung it open. He looked up and down the passage. Then he closed the door and returned to the table, where he took from his waistcoat-pocket a used match.

Scott turned it over inquiringly.

===Chinese===
「怎么样？」第二天斯科特问。「线索源源不断地涌进来了吧？」

「涌得很。」

「什么？又得了一条？」

皮林肖特默不作声地走到门边，猛地把门拉开。他朝走廊两头张了张，然后关上门，回到桌前，从马甲口袋里取出一根用过的火柴。

斯科特好奇地把它翻来覆去地看。

===Original===
“What’s the idea of this?”

“A clue,” said Pillingshot. “See anything queer about it? See that rummy brown stain on it?”

“Yes.”

“Blood!” snorted Pillingshot.

“What’s the good of blood? There’s been no murder.”

Pillingshot looked serious.

===Chinese===
「这有什么讲究？」

「一条线索，」皮林肖特说。「可看出什么古怪没有？可看见上面那块蹊跷的褐色污渍没有？」

「看见了。」

「血！」皮林肖特哼道。

「血顶什么用？又没出过命案。」

皮林肖特神色凝重起来。

===Original===
“I never thought of that.”

“You must think of everything. The worst mistake a detective can make is to get switched off on to another track while he’s working on a case. This match is a clue to something else. You can’t work on it.”

“I suppose not,” said Pillingshot.

“Don’t be discouraged. You’re doing fine.”

“I know,” said Pillingshot. “I shall find that quid all right.”

“Nothing like sticking to it.”

===Chinese===
「这我倒没想到。」

「你必须什么都想到。侦探能犯的最糟糕的错误，莫过于正办着一桩案子，又被岔到另一条道上去。这根火柴是另一桩案子的线索。你顺着它查不了本案。」

「想必是查不了，」皮林肖特说。

「别泄气。你干得挺不赖。」

「我知道，」皮林肖特说。「那一镑钱我准能找回来。」

「天下事就贵在一个锲而不舍。」

===Original===
Pillingshot shuffled, then rose to a point of order.

“I’ve been reading those Sherlock Holmes stories,” he said, “and Sherlock Holmes always got a fee if he brought a thing off. I think I ought to, too.”

“Mercenary young brute.”

“It has been a beastly sweat.”

“Done you good. Supplied you with a serious interest in life. Well, I expect Evans will give you something—a jewelled snuffbox or something—if you pull the thing off.”

===Chinese===
皮林肖特蹭了蹭脚，随即提出一项程序问题。

「我近来一直在读那些夏洛克·福尔摩斯的故事，」他说，「夏洛克·福尔摩斯只要办成一件事，总有一笔酬金进账。我想我也理应如此。」

「唯利是图的小畜生。」

「这可是件苦不堪言的差事。」

「对你大有好处。让你的人生得了一项严肃的兴趣。嗯，你要是把这事办成了，我料想埃文斯会赏你点东西——镶宝石的鼻烟盒之类的。」

===Original===
“*I* don’t.”

“Well, he’ll buy you a tea or something.”

“He won’t. He’s not going to break the quid. He’s saving up for a camera.”

“Well, what are you going to do about it?”

Pillingshot kicked the leg of the table.

“*You* put me on to the case,” he said casually.

===Chinese===
「*我*可不指望。」

「嗯，那他会请你吃顿茶点什么的。」

「他不会的。他舍不得破开那一镑钱。他正攒钱要买照相机呢。」

「那么，你打算怎么办？」

皮林肖特踢了踢桌子腿。

「当初可是*你*把这案子派给我的，」他慢条斯理地说。

===Original===
“What! If you think I’m going to squander—”

“I think you ought to let me off fagging for the rest of the term.”

Scott reflected.

“There’s something in that. All right.”

“Thanks.”

“Don’t mention it. You haven’t found the quid yet.”

===Chinese===
「什么！你要是以为我会挥霍——」

「我想你这学期剩下的日子，应该免了我当学仆的差事。」

斯科特思忖片刻。

「这话有点道理。行吧。」

「多谢。」

「不必客气。那一镑钱你可还没找回来呢。」

===Original===
“I know where it is.”

“Where?”

“Ah!”

“Fool,” said Scott.

---

===Chinese===
「我知道它在哪儿。」

「在哪儿？」

「啊哈！」

「傻子，」斯科特说。

---

===Original===
After breakfast next day Scott was seated in his study when Pillingshot entered.

“Here you are,” said Pillingshot.

He unclasped his right hand and exhibited a sovereign. Scott inspected it.

“Is this the one?” he said.

“Yes,” said Pillingshot.

===Chinese===
第二天早饭后，斯科特正坐在书房里，皮林肖特走了进来。

「给你，」皮林肖特说。

他松开攥紧的右拳，亮出一枚沙弗林金币。斯科特细细验看。

「就是这一枚？」他说。

「正是，」皮林肖特说。

===Original===
“How do you know?”

“It *is*. I’ve sifted all the evidence.”

“Who had bagged it?”

“I don’t want to mention names.”

“Oh, all right. As he didn’t spend any of it, it doesn’t much matter. Not that it’s much catch having a thief roaming at large about the house. Anyhow, what put you on to him? How did you get on the track? You’re a jolly smart kid, young Pillingshot. How did you work it?”

===Chinese===
「你怎么知道的？」

「它*就是*。全部证据我都筛过一遍了。」

「是谁昧下的？」

「我不便说出姓名。」

「噢，好吧。反正他一分未花，也就无关紧要。只不过学舍里有个贼逍遥法外，终究算不得什么体面事。不管怎样，你是怎么盯上他的？怎么寻到踪迹的？你可真是个机灵鬼，小皮林肖特。你是怎么破的案？」

===Original===
“I have my methods,” said Pillingshot with dignity.

“Buck up. I shall have to be going over to school in a second.”

“I hardly like to tell you.”

“Tell me! Dash it all, I put you on to the case. I’m your employer.”

“You won’t touch me up if I tell you?”

“I will if you don’t.”

===Chinese===
「我自有我的方法，」皮林肖特庄严地说。

「麻利点儿。我马上就得往学校那边去了。」

「我不大好意思告诉你。」

「告诉我！真是活见鬼，这案子还是我派给你的。我是你的雇主。」

「我要是说了，你不揍我吧？」

「你不说才揍。」

===Original===
“But not if I do?”

“No.”

“And how about the fee?”

“That’s all right. Go on.”

“All right then. Well, I thought the whole thing over, and I couldn’t make anything out of it at first, because it didn’t seem likely that Trent or any of the other fellows in the dormitory had taken it; and then suddenly something Evans told me the day before yesterday made it all clear.”

===Chinese===
「说了就不揍？」

「不揍。」

「那酬金怎么说？」

「少不了你的。说吧。」

「那好吧。呃，我把整桩事情前前后后想了一遍，起初怎么也理不出头绪，因为特伦特也好、宿舍里旁的人也好，都不像是拿了的；后来，前天埃文斯跟我说的一句话，忽然把一切都挑明了。」

===Original===
“What was that?”

“He said that the matron had just given him back his quid, which one of the housemaids had found on the floor by his bed. It had dropped out of his pocket that first night.”

Scott eyed him fixedly. Pillingshot coyly evaded his gaze.

“That was it, was it?” said Scott.

Pillingshot nodded.

“It was a clue,” he said. “I worked on it.”

===Chinese===
「什么话？」

「他说，舍监太太刚把那一镑钱还给了他，是一个宿舍女仆在他床边地板上捡到的。头天夜里，钱从他口袋里滑了出来。」

斯科特目不转睛地盯着他。皮林肖特忸怩地避开了这道目光。

「原来是这么回事，是吗？」斯科特说。

皮林肖特点点头。

「那是条线索，」他说。「我顺着查下去了。」

## Shields' and the Cricket Cup / 希尔兹家与板球杯

===Original===
The house cricket cup at Wrykyn has found itself on some strange mantelpieces in its time. New talent has a way of cropping up in the house matches. Tail-end men hit up fifties, and bowlers who have never taken a wicket before except at the nets go on fifth change, and dismiss first eleven experts with deliveries that bounce twice and shoot. So that nobody is greatly surprised in the ordinary run of things if the cup does not go to the favourites, or even to the second or third favourites. But one likes to draw the line. And Wrykyn drew it at Shields'. And yet, as we shall proceed to show, Shields' once won the cup, and that, too, in a year when Donaldson's had four first eleven men and Dexter's three.

Shields' occupied a unique position at the School. It was an absolutely inconspicuous house. There were other houses that were slack or wild or both, but the worst of these did something. Shields' never did anything. It never seemed to want to do anything. This may have been due in some degree to Mr. Shields. As the housemaster is, so the house is. He was the most inconspicuous master on the staff. He taught a minute form in the junior school, where earnest infants wrestled with somebody’s handy book of easy Latin sentences, and depraved infants threw cunningly compounded ink-balls at one another and the ceiling. After school he would range the countryside with a pickle bottle in search of polly woggles and other big game, which he subsequently transferred to slides and examined through a microscope till an advanced hour of the night. The curious part of the matter was that his house was never riotous. Perhaps he was looked on as a noncombatant, one whom it would be unfair and unsporting to rag. At any rate, a weird calm reigned over the place; and this spirit seemed to permeate the public lives of the Shieldsites. They said nothing much and they did nothing much and they were very inoffensive. As a rule, one hardly knew they were there.

Into this abode of lotus-eaters came Clephane, a day boy, owing to the departure of his parents for India. Clephane wanted to go to Donaldson's. In fact, he said so. His expressions, indeed, when he found that the whole thing had been settled, and that he was to spend his last term at school at a house which had never turned out so much as a member of the Gym Six, bordered on the unfilial. It appeared that his father had met Mr. Shields at dinner in the town﻿—a fact to which he seemed to attach a mystic importance. Clephane’s criticism of this attitude of mind was of such a nature as to lead his father to address him as Archibald instead of Archie.

However, the thing was done, and Clephane showed his good sense by realising this and turning his energetic mind to the discovery of the best way of making life at Shields' endurable. Fortune favoured him by sending to the house another day boy, one Mansfield. Clephane had not known him intimately before, though they were both members of the second eleven; but at Shields' they instantly formed an alliance. And in due season﻿—or a little later﻿—the house matches began. Henfrey, of Day's, the Wrykyn cricket captain, met Clephane at the nets when the drawing for opponents had been done.

===Chinese===
瑞金（Wrykyn）的宿舍板球杯，历年也曾栖身于几座出人意料的壁炉架。宿舍赛向来是新秀破土而出的怪地方：棒尾的末流击球手动辄打出五十分，除了在练习网（the nets）里从未拿过柱门的投球手排上第五换投，用弹跳两回、贴地急蹿的球，把校一队的行家们一一送上出局路。因此，奖杯若没落进热门手中——甚至连二号、三号热门也不曾沾边——寻常情势下没谁会大惊小怪。只是凡事总要有个限度，而瑞金把这限度画在了希尔兹家（Shields'）。然而，且容下文细细道来：希尔兹家居然有一回捧走了奖杯，而且那一年，唐纳森家（Donaldson's）坐拥四名校一队队员，德克斯特宿舍（Dexter's）也有三名。

希尔兹家在这学校里占着一个独一无二的位置。它是一所绝对不起眼的宿舍。别家宿舍也有散漫的、撒野的，或者二者兼备的，但其中最不堪者也总能折腾出点什么。希尔兹家从来无所作为，而且似乎从来就不想有什么作为。这在某种程度上或许要归因于希尔兹先生（Mr. Shields）。有什么样的舍监，就有什么样的宿舍。他是全体教员中最不起眼的一位，在初级部教一个微型班级——班里的用功娃娃们正与某氏那册简易拉丁句便览手册角力，堕落的娃娃们则把精心调配的墨水纸团掷向彼此和天花板。放学后，他便携一只腌菜瓶巡行乡野，猎取蝌蚪之属与其他巨兽，随后将其移置载玻片，置于显微镜下端详，直至夜深。此事的蹊跷之处在于，他的宿舍从不骚乱。或许众人把他看作非战斗人员，拿他恶作剧未免有失公道、有失运动家风度。总之，那地方笼罩着一种怪异的宁静，而这份精气神似乎也浸透了希尔兹家众学子的公开生活。他们话说得不多，事做得不多，而且全然无害。平日里，人们几乎察觉不到他们的存在。

便是这座食莲人的安乐窝，迎来了克莱芬（Clephane）——一名走读生，因父母双双去了印度而迁入。克莱芬本想去唐纳森家。事实上，他就是这样说的。待他发觉整桩事已成定局、自己竟要在校内最后一学期寄宿于一家连「体操六杰」（Gym Six）成员都从未出产过的宿舍时，他的言辞实在近乎忤逆。据说他父亲在镇上某次晚宴上结识了希尔兹先生——此事在他父亲眼里似乎带有某种神秘的分量。克莱芬对这一心态的批评，其措辞之性质，致使他父亲不再唤他「阿奇」（Archie），改口称他「阿奇博尔德」（Archibald）。

然而木已成舟，克莱芬也颇识时务，认清这一点之后，便把他那颗精力充沛的头脑转向一个课题：如何在希尔兹家把日子过得下去。命运眷顾了他，给这宿舍又送来一名走读生，曼斯菲尔德（Mansfield）其人。两人此前交情不深，虽则同为校二队队员；但在希尔兹家，他们当即结成了同盟。到了时节——或者稍晚一点——宿舍赛开锣。对阵抽签已毕，戴家（Day's）的亨弗雷（Henfrey）——瑞金板球队长——在练习网边遇上了克莱芬。

===Original===
“Just the man I wanted to see,” said Henfrey. “I suppose you’re captain of Shields' lot, Clephane? Well, you’re going to scratch as usual, I suppose?”

For the last five seasons that lamentable house had failed to put a team into the field. “You’d better,” said Henfrey, “we haven’t overmuch time as it is. That match with Paget's team has thrown us out a lot. We ought to have started the house matches a week ago.”

“Scratch!” said Clephane. “Don’t you wish we would! My good chap, we’re going to get the cup.”

“You needn’t be a funny ass,” said Henfrey in his complaining voice, “we really are awfully pushed. As it is we shall have to settle the opening rounds on the first innings. That’s to say, we can only give ’em a day each; if they don’t finish, the winner of the first innings wins. You might as well scratch.”

“I can’t help your troubles. By rotten mismanagement you have got the house-matches crowded up into the last ten days of term, and you come and expect me to sell a fine side like Shields' to get you out of the consequences of your reckless act. My word, Henfrey, you’ve sunk pretty low. Nice young fellow Henfrey was at one time, but seems to have got among bad companions. Quite changed now. Avoid him as much as I can. Leave me, Henfrey, I would be alone.”

===Chinese===
「正是我要找的人，」亨弗雷说，「我猜你是希尔兹家的队长吧，克莱芬？那么，你们今年照旧弃权，对吧？」

过去五个赛季，那座可悲的宿舍连一支队伍都未能派出。「你们最好如此，」亨弗雷说，「我们的时间本就不宽裕。跟佩吉特家（Paget's）那场球把日程搅乱了不少。宿舍赛本该一星期前就开打的。」

「弃权！」克莱芬道，「你巴不得吧！我的好伙计，我们是要去捧杯的。」

「犯不上耍贫嘴，」亨弗雷用他那抱怨腔说道，「我们实在赶得很。如今头几轮只好以第一局定胜负。也就是说，每场只给一天；打不完的，第一局领先者胜。你们还是弃权的好。」

「你的难处与我何干。全凭你那糟糕透顶的管理，宿舍赛才被挤到学期最后十天，如今你倒跑来，指望我出卖希尔兹家这样一支劲旅，好把你鲁莽行事的后果给兜起来。天哪，亨弗雷，你堕落得可以啊。亨弗雷从前也是个体面的后生，如今似乎交上了坏朋友，整个人全变了。我如今能躲多远躲多远。你走吧，亨弗雷，我想独自静静。」

===Original===
“But you can’t raise a team.”

“Raise a team! Do you happen to know that half the house is *biting* itself with agony because we can’t find room for all? Shields gives stump-cricket soiriees in his study after prep. One every time you hit the ball, two into the bowl of goldfish, and out if you smash the microscope.”

“Well,” said Henfrey viciously, “if you want to go through the farce of playing one round and making idiots of yourselves, you’ll have to wait a bit. You’ve got a bye in the first round.”

===Chinese===
「可你们拉不起一支队伍。」

「拉队伍！你可知道，全宿舍一半人正急得把自己『咬』得死去活来，只因我们腾不出位置安插所有人？希尔兹先生每晚自习后都在书房里举办桩网球（stump-cricket）晚会：每击中一球记一分，打进金鱼缸记两分，砸了显微镜立即出局。」

「好吧，」亨弗雷恶狠狠地说，「你们若想演一遍打一轮、当众出丑的闹剧，那也还得等上一等。你们首轮轮空。」

===Original===
Clephane told the news to Mansfield after tea. “I’ve been and let the house in for a rollicking time,” he said, abstracting the copy of Latin verses which his friend was doing, and sitting on them to ensure undivided attention to his words. “Wanting to score off old Henfrey﻿—I have few pleasures﻿—I told him that Shields' was not going to scratch. So we are booked to play in the second round of the housers. We drew a bye for the first. It would be an awful rag if we could do something. We *must* raise a team of some sort. Henfrey would score so if we didn’t. Who’s there, d’you think, that can play?”

Mansfield considered the question thoughtfully. “They all *play*, I suppose,” he said slowly, “if you can call it playing. What I mean to say is, cricket’s compulsory here, so I suppose they’ve all had an innings or two at one time or another in the eightieth game or so. But if you want record-breakers, I shouldn’t trust to Shields' too much.”

“Not a bit. So long as we put a full team into the field, that’s all I care about. I’ve often wondered what it’s like to go in first and bowl unchanged the whole time.”

“You’ll do that all right,” said Mansfield. “I should think Shields' bowling ran to slow grubs, to judge from the look of ’em. You’d better go and see Wilkins about raising the team. As head of the house, he probably considers himself captain of cricket.”

===Chinese===
茶点之后，克莱芬把消息通报了曼斯菲尔德。「我刚给全宿舍揽了一档子狂欢，」他说着，抽走朋友正在誊的拉丁诗功课，一屁股坐在上面，以确保对方能全神贯注听他讲话。「想占老亨弗雷的上风——我的乐事本就不多——我告诉他，希尔兹家不弃权了。于是我们定好在宿舍赛第二轮出场，首轮抽了个轮空。要是咱们能折腾出点名堂，那可就是天大的乐子了。咱们无论如何也得拉起一支队伍。不然亨弗雷可就占足上风了。你想想，这里有谁能打？」

曼斯菲尔德若有所思地掂量着这个问题。「他们都『会打』，我想，」他慢吞吞地说，「如果你管那也叫打的话。我是说，这里板球是必修，所以我想，他们多多少少都在大约第八十号的比赛里上过一两回场击球。不过你要是想找破纪录的角色，希尔兹家就别太指望了。」

「一点不在乎。只要能把一支满员的队伍拉上场，我就别无所求了。我常想，头一个上场击球、再从头到尾独个儿投到底，是何等光景。」

「这你准能如愿，」曼斯菲尔德说，「照那些尊容判断，希尔兹家的投球想必尽是慢吞吞的软球。你最好去找威尔金斯（Wilkins）商量组队的事。身为宿舍之长，他八成自封为板球队长。」

===Original===
Wilkins, however, took a far more modest view of his position. The notion of leading a happy band of cricketers from Shields' into the field had, it seemed, small attractions for him. But he went so far as to get a house list, and help choose a really representative team. And as details about historic teams are always welcome, we may say that the averages ranged from 3.005 to 8.14. This last was Wilkins’ own and was, as he would have been the first to admit, substantially helped by a contribution of nineteen in a single innings in the fifth game.

So the team was selected, and Clephane turned out after school next day to give them a little fielding-practice. To his surprise the fielding was not so outrageous as might have been expected. All the simpler catches were held, and one or two of the harder as well. Given this form on the day of their appearance in public, and Henfrey might be disappointed when he came to watch and smile sarcastically. A batting fiasco is not one half so ridiculous as maniac fielding.

In the meantime the first round of the house matches had been played off, and it would be as well to describe at this point the positions of the rival houses and their prospects. In the first place, there were only four teams really in the running for the cup, Day's (headed by the redoubtable Henfrey), Spence's, who had Jackson, that season a head and shoulders above the other batsmen in the first eleven﻿—he had just wound up the school season with an average of 51.3, Donaldson's, and Dexter's. All the other house teams were mainly tail.

Now, in the first round the powerful quartet had been diminished by the fact that Donaldson's had drawn Dexter's, and had lost to them by a couple of wickets.

For the second round Shields' drew Appleby's, a poor team. Space on the Wrykyn field being a consideration, with three house matches to be played off at the same time, Clephane’s men fought their first battle on rugged ground in an obscure corner. As the captain of cricket ordered these matters, Henfrey had naturally selected the best bit of turf for Day's *v.* Dexter's. That section of the ground which was sacred to the school second-eleven matches was allotted to Spence's *v.* the School House. The idle public divided its attention between the two big games, and paid no attention to the death struggle in progress at the far end of the field. Whereby it missed a deal of quiet fun.

===Chinese===
然而威尔金斯对自己的地位抱持着谦逊得多的看法。率领一队希尔兹家的快乐板球手开赴赛场——这念头于他似乎魅力寥寥。但他到底还是取来一份宿舍名单，帮着挑了一支真正有代表性的队伍。既然关于历史名队的细节向来受欢迎，我们不妨奉告：全队平均分自 3.005 至 8.14 不等。后一个数字是威尔金斯本人的，而且——正如他会头一个承认的——全仗第五场比赛里单局贡献的十九分撑持门面。

队伍就这样选定，次日放学后，克莱芬到场给他们做了一点守场训练。令他诧异的是，守场水平并没有预想中那么离谱。简单些的高飞球都接稳了，难接的也接住了一两个。倘若公开亮相那天也能保持这个水准，亨弗雷前来观战、准备讥讽一笑时，恐怕要大失所望。击球砸锅的滑稽，还不及疯子式守场的一半。

与此同时，宿舍赛首轮已经打完，此处正好交代一下各家宿舍的座次与前景。首先，真正有望夺杯的只有四支队伍：戴家（由那位令人生畏的亨弗雷领衔）、坐拥杰克逊（Jackson）的斯宾塞家（Spence's）——那一季他在校一队击球手中高出一头，刚刚以 51.3 的平均分为校赛季收官——还有唐纳森家和德克斯特宿舍。其余各队的棒次，大半是长尾。

而在首轮之中，这强大的四强便已折损一路：唐纳森家抽中了德克斯特宿舍，以两座柱门之差败北。

第二轮，希尔兹家抽中了阿普尔比宿舍（Appleby's）——一支弱旅。瑞金球场地方有限，三场宿舍赛又须同时打完，克莱芬的兵马便在某个偏僻角落的坑洼地皮上打响了第一仗。这些安排既由板球总队长一手掌管，亨弗雷自然把最好的一片草皮划给了戴家对德克斯特宿舍之战。专供校二队比赛之用的那片圣地，则拨给了斯宾塞家对学舍（School House）。闲得无聊的看客们把注意力平分给两场大赛，对球场远端正在进行的殊死之战不闻不问。他们因此错过了不少悄无声息的好戏。

===Original===
I say death struggle advisedly. Clephane had won his second-eleven cap as a fast bowler. He had failed to get into the first eleven because he was considered too erratic. Put these two facts together, and you will suspect that dark deeds were wrought on the men of Appleby in that lonely corner of the Wrykyn meadow.

The pitch was not a good one. As a sample of the groundman’s art it was sketchy and amateurish; it lacked finish. Clephane won the toss, took a hasty glance at the corrugated turf, and decided to bat first. The wicket was hardly likely to improve with use.

He and Mansfield opened the batting. He stood three feet out of his ground, and smote. The first four balls he took full pitch. The last two, owing to a passion for variety on the part of the bowler, were long hops. At the end of the over Shields' score was twenty-four. Mansfield pursued the same tactics. When the first wicket fell, seventy was on the board. A spirit of martial enthusiasm pervaded the ranks of the house team. Mild youths with spectacles leaped out of their ground like tigers, and snicked fours through the slips. When the innings concluded, blood had been spilt﻿—from an injured finger﻿—but the total was a hundred and two.

Then Clephane walked across to the School shop for a vanilla ice. He said he could get more devil, as it were, into his bowling after a vanilla ice. He had a couple.

When he bowled his first ball it was easy to see that there was truth in the report of the causes of his inclusion in the second eleven and exclusion from the first. The batsman observed somewhat weakly, “Here, I *say*!” and backed towards square leg. The ball soared over the wicket-keep’s head and went to the boundary. The bowler grinned pleasantly, and said he was just getting his arm in.

===Chinese===
我说「殊死之战」，是斟酌过的。克莱芬凭快球投手的身手，挣得过一顶校二队正式帽（cap）。他之所以没能进校一队，是因为众人嫌他失之飘忽。把这两桩事实合起来一想，你便会疑心：在那瑞金草场孤零零的角落里，阿普尔比宿舍众人是遭了何等阴毒的黑手。

球道不佳。作为场务老儿技艺的样品，它粗疏外行，欠了收尾的功夫。克莱芬掷币获胜，匆匆一瞥那洗衣板似的草皮，决定先击。这柱门，越用只会越糟。

他和曼斯菲尔德开局击球。他站出击球线三英尺，挥棒痛击。头四个球，他都在落地之前迎个正着。后两个，因投球手一时兴起要换换花样，成了又高又慢的弹跳球。一轮打完，希尔兹家已得二十四分。曼斯菲尔德如法炮制。第一座柱门落地时，记分牌上已书七十分。一股杀伐的豪气弥漫全队。戴眼镜的文弱少年如猛虎般跃出击球线，从截杀区的缝隙里蹭出一个个四分球。这一局收场时，血也见了——出自一根挂彩的手指——总分一百零二。

随后克莱芬踱到学校小卖部，去买香草冰淇淋。他说，香草冰淇淋下肚，投球里便多少能多添几分狠劲。他吃了两份。

等他投出第一球，种种传言——他何以进得了二队、进不了一队——便都得到了印证。击球手有气无力地抗议了一声「喂，我『说』！」，一路退向侧外野。那球从接柱手头顶掠过，直坠边界。投球手愉快地咧嘴一笑，说胳膊才刚活动开。

===Original===
The second ball landed full-pitch on the batsman’s right thigh. The third was another full pitch, this time on the top of the middle stump, which it smashed. With profound satisfaction the batsman hobbled to the trees, and sat down. “Let somebody else have a shot,” he said kindly.

Appleby's made twenty-eight that innings.

Their defeat by an innings and fifty-three runs they attributed subsequently to the fact that only seven of the team could be induced to go to the wickets in the second venture.

“So you’ve managed to win a match,” grunted Henfrey, “I should like to have been there.”

“You might just as well have been,” said Clephane, “from what they tell me.”

At which Henfrey became abusive, for he had achieved an “egg” that afternoon, and missed a catch; which things soured him, though Day's had polished off Dexter's handsomely.

===Chinese===
第二球腾空不落地，正中击球手右大腿。第三球又是一记腾空直球，这回落在中柱顶端，把柱击得粉碎。击球手心满意足地一瘸一拐挪到树边坐下。「让别人也来试试手气，」他和善地说。

阿普尔比宿舍那一局得了二十八分。

至于最终以整整一局加五十三分之差落败，他们事后归因于一个事实：第二局上阵，全队只有七个人好说歹说才肯走向柱门。

「你们到底还是赢了一场，」亨弗雷咕哝道，「我真该在场看看。」

「照他们跟我讲的情形，你在不在场也差不多。」克莱芬说。

这话引得亨弗雷破口大骂，因为那天下午他吃了一枚「鸭蛋」，又漏接一球；尽管戴家已把德克斯特宿舍漂漂亮亮地料理干净，这两桩事还是让他心里发酸。

===Original===
“Well,” he said at length, “you’re in the semifinal now, of all weird places. You’d better play Spence's next. When can you play?”

“Henfrey,” said Clephane, “I have a bright, open, boyish countenance, but I was not born yesterday. You want to get a dangerous rival out of the way without trouble, so you set Shields' to smash up Spence's. No, Henfrey. I do not intend to be your catspaw. We will draw lots who is to play which. Here comes Jackson. We’ll toss odd man out.”

And when the coins fell there were two tails and one head; and the head belonged to the coin of Clephane.

“So, you see,” he said to Henfrey, “Shields' is in the final. No wonder you wanted us to scratch.”

I should like this story to end with a vivid description of a tight finish. Considering that Day's beat Spence's, and consequently met Shields' in the final, that would certainly be the most artistic ending. Henfrey batting﻿—Clephane bowling﻿—one to tie, two to win, one wicket to fall. Up goes the ball! Will the lad catch it!! He fumbles it. It falls. All is over. But look! With a supreme effort﻿—and so on.

The real conclusion was a little sensational in its way, but not nearly so exciting as that.

===Chinese===
「好吧，」他终于开口，「你们如今居然待进了半决赛这等怪地方。下一轮最好对斯宾塞家。你们什么时候能打？」

「亨弗雷，」克莱芬说，「我生着一张明朗坦荡的孩子脸，可我不是昨天才出世的。你想不费吹灰之力除掉一个危险对手，便支使希尔兹家去碾碎斯宾塞家。不，亨弗雷。我不打算当你的猫爪子，替你火中取栗。咱们来抽签决定谁对谁。杰克逊来了。我们抛硬币，异者得利。」

硬币落定，两枚反面，一枚正面；那枚正面，正是克莱芬的。

「所以你瞧，」他对亨弗雷说，「希尔兹家进决赛了。难怪你先前巴不得我们弃权。」

我本愿这篇故事以一段扣人心弦的决胜描写收束。鉴于戴家击败了斯宾塞家、从而与希尔兹家会师决赛，那无疑是艺术性最强的结局。亨弗雷击球——克莱芬投球——再得一分即平，两分即胜，而柱门只剩最后一座。球腾空了！那小伙子接得住吗！！他失手了。球落地了。全完了。且慢！但见他奋起神威——如此等等。

真实的结局自有一番耸动，却远不及这般惊心动魄。

===Original===
The match between Day's and Shields' opened in a conventional enough manner. Day's batted first, and made two hundred and fifty. Henfrey carried his bat for seventy-six, and there were some thirties. For Shields' Clephane and Mansfield made their usual first-wicket stand, and the rest brought the total up to ninety-eight. At this point Henfrey introduced a variation on custom. The match was a three days’ match. In fact, owing to the speed with which the other games had been played, it could, if necessary, last four days. The follow-on was, therefore, a matter for the discretion of the side which led. Henfrey and his team saw no reason why they should not have another pleasant spell of batting before dismissing their opponents for the second time and acquiring the cup. So in they went again, and made another two hundred and fifty odd, Shields' being left with four hundred and twelve to make to win.

On the morning after Day's second innings, a fag from Day's brought Clephane a message from Henfrey. Henfrey was apparently in bed. He would be glad if Clephane would go and see him in the dinner-hour. The interview lasted fifteen minutes. Then Clephane burst out of the house, and dashed across to Shields' in search of Mansfield.

===Chinese===
戴家对希尔兹家之战，开局相当中规中矩。戴家先击，得二百五十分。亨弗雷带着七十六分全程未出局，另有几人打出三十分上下。希尔兹家这边，克莱芬和曼斯菲尔德照例完成开局的首柱合作，其余人把总分抬到九十八。此时亨弗雷变了一回惯例。这是一场三天赛。事实上，由于前几场打得快，若有必要，拖到四天也使得。因此，是否让对手「跟赛再战」（follow-on，即令落后一方紧接着再击一局），全凭领先一方裁量。亨弗雷和他的队伍看不出有什么理由，不趁着第二次把对手料理出局、把奖杯收入囊中之前，再痛快打上一阵。于是他们再度上场，又添了二百五十多分，留给希尔兹家的，是四百一十二分的取胜任务。

戴家第二局之后的那个上午，戴家一名低年级听差（fag）给克莱芬捎来亨弗雷的口信。亨弗雷显然卧床不起。他欢迎克莱芬于午饭钟点前去一叙。会面历时一刻钟。随后克莱芬冲出戴家的楼，直奔希尔兹家去找曼斯菲尔德。

===Original===
“I say, *have* you heard?” he shouted.

“What’s up?”

“Why, every man in Day's team, bar two kids, is in bed. Ill. Do you mean to say you haven’t heard? They thought they’d got that house cup safe, so all the team except the two kids, fags, you know, had a feed in honour of it in Henfrey’s study. Some ass went and bought a bad rabbit pie, and now they’re laid up. Not badly, but they won’t be out for a day or two.”

“But what about the match?”

“Oh, that’ll go on. I made a point of that. They can play subs.”

===Chinese===
「我说，你『到底』听说了没有？」他嚷道。

「出什么事了？」

「嗐，戴家全队上下，除两个小毛头外，全都躺倒了。病了。你莫非还没听说？他们自忖宿舍杯稳落囊中，于是全队——除了那两个当听差的小毛头，你知道的——在亨弗雷的书房里会了一顿庆功宴。哪个蠢货跑去买了只变质的兔肉派，这下全撂倒了。病得不算重，可一两天内出不了门。」

「可比赛怎么办？」

「哦，照打不误。这一点我特意敲定了。他们可以上替补。」

===Original===
Mansfield looked thoughtful.

“But I say,” he said, “it isn’t very sporting, is it? Oughtn’t we to wait or something?”

“Sporting! My dear chap, a case like this mustn’t be judged by ordinary standards. We can’t spoil the giant rag of the century because it isn’t quite sporting. Think what it means﻿—Shields' getting the cup! It’ll keep the school laughing for terms. What do you want to spoil people’s pleasure for?”

“Oh, all right,” said Mansfield.

“Besides, think of the moral effect it’ll have on the house. It may turn it into the blood house of Wrykyn. Shields himself may get quite sportive. We mustn’t miss the chance.”

===Chinese===
曼斯菲尔德显出若有所思的神色。

「可是我说，」他道，「这不太光明磊落吧？咱们是不是该等一等，或者怎样？」

「光明磊落！亲爱的伙计，这等案子岂能用寻常标准来衡量。总不能因为它不够光明磊落，就毁了本世纪头一场特大闹剧。想想这是什么意思——希尔兹家捧杯！够全校笑上好几个学期。你何苦去败大家的兴？」

「哦，好吧。」曼斯菲尔德说。

「再说，想想这事对全宿舍的精神感召。说不定能让它变成瑞金头一号的威风宿舍（blood house）。连希尔兹先生本人，兴许也会生出几分运动豪情。这机会万万错不得。」

===Original===
The news having got about the school, Clephane and Mansfield opened their second innings to the somewhat embarrassed trundling of Masters Royce and Tibbit, of the Junior School, before a substantial and appreciative audience.

Both played carefully at first, but soon getting the measure of the bowling (which was not deep) began to hit out, and runs came quickly. At fifty, Tibbit, understudying Henfrey as captain of the side, summoned to his young friend Todby from short leg, and instructed him to “have a go” at the top end.

It was here that Clephane courteously interfered. Substitutes, he pointed out, were allowed, by the laws of cricket, only to field, not to bowl. He must, therefore, request friend Todby to return to his former sphere of utility, where, he added politely, he was a perfect demon.

===Chinese===
消息传遍全校之后，克莱芬和曼斯菲尔德在一大群识货看客的注视下开始了第二局击球，为他们投球的，是初级部的小爷罗伊斯（Royce）与蒂比特（Tibbit），那投球多少带几分窘迫。

两人起初打得谨慎，但很快便摸清了这投球的深浅（其实深不到哪里去），于是放开手脚猛击，分数来得飞快。到五十分时，顶替亨弗雷执掌队长的蒂比特把他的小朋友托德比（Todby）从腿侧近位唤来，吩咐他到上端去「投一手」。

便在此时，克莱芬彬彬有礼地出面干预了。他指出，按板球规则，替补只准上场守场，不准投球。因此，他必须请托德比小朋友回到他原先的用武之地去——他客客气气地补上一句——在那儿，他可是一员魔神。

===Original===
“But, blow it,” said Master Tibbit, who (alas!) was addicted to the use of strong language, “Royce and I can’t bowl the whole blessed time.”

“You’ll have to, I’m afraid,” said Clephane with the kindly air of a doctor soothing a refractory patient. “Of course, you can take a spell at grubs whenever you like.”

“Oh, darn!” said Master Tibbit.

Shortly afterwards Clephane made his century.

===Chinese===
「可是，搞什么鬼，」蒂比特小爷说——（呜呼！）此君惯说粗话——「总不能叫罗伊斯和我该死的一直投个没完吧。」

「恐怕非如此不可，」克莱芬说着，面带医生安抚犟脾性病人的和蔼神情，「当然啰，二位什么时候想投慢软球，随时请便。」

「哦，该死的！」蒂比特小爷说。

不多时，克莱芬拿下了他的百分。

---

===Original===
The match ended late on the following afternoon in a victory for Shields' by nine wickets, and the scene at the School Shop when Royce and Tibbit arrived to drown their sorrows and moisten their dry throats with ginger beer is said by eyewitnesses to have been something quite out of the common run.

The score sheet of the match is also a little unusual. Clephane’s three hundred and one (not out) is described in the *Wrykinian* as a “masterly exhibition of sound yet aggressive batting.” How Henfrey described it we have never heard.

===Chinese===
次日下午将尽时，比赛以希尔兹家九柱在手之胜告终。罗伊斯与蒂比特来到学校小卖部，欲借姜汁啤酒浇愁、润一润发干的喉咙——据目击者称，当时那一幕，大大出平常轨。

这场比赛的记分表也有些不寻常。克莱芬的三百零一分（未出局），在《瑞金人》（Wrykinian）上被形容为「一场稳健而凌厉的击球大师表演」。至于亨弗雷如何形容它，我们从未听说。

## The Autograph Hunters / 签名猎人们

===Original===
Dunstable had his reasons for wishing to obtain Mr. Montagu Watson’s autograph, but admiration for that gentleman’s novels was not one of them.

It was nothing to him that critics considered Mr. Watson one of the most remarkable figures in English literature since Scott. If you had told him of this, he would merely have wondered in his coarse, material way how much Mr. Watson gave the critics for saying so. To the reviewer of the *Weekly Booklover* the great man’s latest effort, *The Soul of Anthony Carrington* (Popgood and Grooly: 6s.) seemed “a work that speaks eloquently in every line of a genius that time cannot wither nor custom stale.” To Dunstable, who got it out of the school library, where it had been placed at the request of a literary prefect, and read the first eleven pages, it seemed rot, and he said as much to the librarian on returning it.

Yet he was very anxious to get the novelist’s autograph. The fact was that Mr. Day, his housemaster, a man whose private life was in other ways unstained by vicious habits, collected autographs. Also Mr. Day had behaved in a square manner towards Dunstable on several occasions in the past, and Dunstable, always ready to punish bad behaviour in a master, was equally anxious to reward and foster any good trait which he might exhibit.

===Chinese===
邓斯特布尔（Dunstable）之所以想弄到蒙塔古·沃森（Montagu Watson）先生的亲笔签名，自有他的道理，但对那位先生小说的仰慕，不在其列。

评论界将沃森先生视为自司各特（Scott）以来英国文坛最杰出的人物之一，这话在他那里全无分量。你若把这话讲给他听，他只会以他那粗鄙、唯物的思路暗自琢磨：沃森先生为这句话塞给评论家们多少钱。在《每周书迷》（*Weekly Booklover*）的书评人看来，这位伟人的最新力作《安东尼·卡林顿的灵魂》（*The Soul of Anthony Carrington*，波普古德与格鲁利出版社出版，定价六先令）是「一部字里行间雄辩地昭示着天才的作品——时间无法使之凋萎，习俗无法使之陈腐」。而在邓斯特布尔看来——他是从学校图书馆借的这本书，它因某位风雅级长的请求而被购入馆藏，他读了开头十一页——这书纯属胡扯，还书时他对管理员也照实这么说了。

然而他却一门心思想弄到这位小说家的亲笔签名。事实是：他的舍监戴先生（Mr. Day）——此人的私生活在别的方面倒不曾让恶习玷污——有收集签名的癖好。而且戴先生以往有几回待邓斯特布尔行事相当公道；而邓斯特布尔对付老师的不良行为向来出手及时，对老师可能展现的任何优良品性，也同样热切地乐于奖掖栽培。

===Original===
On the occasion of the announcement that Mr. Watson had taken the big white house near Chesterton, a couple of miles from the school, Mr. Day had expressed in Dunstable’s hearing a wish that he could add that celebrity’s signature to his collection. Dunstable had instantly determined to play the part of a benevolent Providence. He would get the autograph and present it to the housemaster, as who should say, “see what comes of being good.” It would be pleasant to observe the innocent joy of the recipient, his childlike triumph, and his amazement at the donor’s ingenuity in securing the treasure. A touching scene—well worth the trouble involved in the quest.

And there would be trouble. For Mr. Montagu Watson was notoriously a foe to the autograph-hunter. His curt, typewritten replies (signed by a secretary) had damped the ardour of scores of brave men and—more or less—fair women. A genuine Montagu Watson was a prize in the autograph market.

===Chinese===
沃森先生租下切斯特顿（Chesterton）附近那幢大白宅子的消息公布之际——那地方离学校两英里——戴先生曾在邓斯特布尔听得见的地方表示，但愿能把那位名人的签名添进自己的收藏。邓斯特布尔当即打定主意，要来扮演一位乐善好施的天公。他要弄到那签名，再献到舍监跟前，仿佛在说：「瞧瞧，积德行善便有这样的好处。」届时观赏受赠者那无邪的欢喜、孩童般的得胜，以及他对献宝人猎获至宝的机巧所发的惊叹，想必其乐无穷。感人的一幕——为这趟追寻费些周折，值。

而周折是必定有的。因为蒙塔古·沃森先生是出了名的签名猎人的克星。他那简慢的打字回信（由秘书代签）已经浇熄过数十位勇士、以及——或多或少算是——淑女们的热忱。一份货真价实的蒙塔古·沃森，在签名市场上可是稀世珍品。

===Original===
Dunstable was a man of action. When Mark, the boot-boy at Day’s, carried his burden of letters to the post that evening, there nestled among them one addressed to M. Watson, Esq., The White House, Chesterton. Looking at it casually, few of his friends would have recognised Dunstable’s handwriting. For it had seemed good to that man of guile to adopt for the occasion the role of a backward youth of twelve years old. He thought tender years might touch Mr. Watson’s heart.

This was the letter:

> Dear Sir
> —I am only a littel boy, but I think your books ripping. I often wonder how you think of it all. Will you please send me your ortograf? I like your books very much. I have named my white rabit Montagu after you. I punched Jones II in the eye today becos he didn’t like your books. I have spent the only penny I have on the stampe for this letter which I might have spent on tuck. I want to be like Maltby in *The Soul of Anthony Carrington* when I grow up.
> Your sincere reader,
> P. A. Dunstable.

It was a little unfortunate, perhaps, that he selected Maltby as his ideal character. That gentleman was considered by critics a masterly portrait of the cynical *roui*. But it was the only name he remembered.

===Chinese===
邓斯特布尔是个行动派。当晚，戴家（Day's）的擦鞋小厮马克（Mark）抱着他那一摞信去邮局投递，其中安卧着一封，抬头写着：切斯特顿白宅，蒙塔古·沃森先生台启。随手扫上一眼，他的朋友里没几个能认出那是邓斯特布尔的笔迹。因为这位诡诈之士觉得，在这个场合不妨扮一个十二岁、心智迟钝的少年。他寻思，稚龄或许能动一动沃森先生的心。

信是这样写的：

> 亲爱的先生
> ——我只是一个小男骇，可我觉得您的书棒透了。我常常纳闷，这一切您都是怎么想出来的。能不能请您把您的亲笔签明寄给我？我非常喜欢您的书。我给我家的小白免子起了名，叫蒙塔古，就是照着您起的。今天我一拳打在琼斯二世的眼睛上，因伪他不喜欢您的书。我把仅有的一便士花在了这封信的由票上，这笔钱本来能拿去买零嘴的。等我长大了，我想变成《安东尼·卡林顿的灵魂》里的马尔特比（Maltby）。
> 您诚挚的读者，
> P. A. 邓斯特布尔

他挑中马尔特比当理想人物，或许是有点小小的失着。那位先生在评论界看来，是一幅愤世嫉俗的*浪荡子*（roui）的传神画像。可这是他记得起的唯一名字。

===Original===
“Hot stuff!” said Dunstable to himself, as he closed the envelope.

“Little beast!” said Mr. Watson to himself as he opened it. It arrived by the morning post, and he never felt really himself till after breakfast.

“Here, Morrison,” he said to his secretary, later in the morning: “just answer this, will you? The usual thing—thanks and most deeply grateful, y’know.”

Next day the following was included in Dunstable’s correspondence:

> Mr. Montagu Watson presents his compliments to Mr. P. A. Dunstable, and begs to thank him for all the kind things he says about his work in his letter of the 18th inst., for which he is deeply grateful.

===Chinese===
「够劲的货色！」邓斯特布尔封上信封时自忖道。

「小畜生！」沃森先生拆信时自忖道。信是早班邮差送来的，而早饭之前，他从没真正还过魂。

「喂，莫里森（Morrison），」上午稍晚的时候他对秘书说，「把这封回了，行吗？老一套——多谢啦，感激不尽啦，你懂的。」

第二天，邓斯特布尔的往来信函中添了下面这一封：

> 蒙塔古·沃森先生谨向 P. A. 邓斯特布尔先生致意，多蒙先生于本月18日来函中对拙作谬赞种种，谨此致谢，不胜感荷。

===Original===
“Foiled!” said Dunstable, and went off to Seymour’s to see his friend Linton.

“Got any notepaper?” he asked.

“Heaps,” said Linton. “Why? Want some?”

“Then get out a piece. I want to dictate a letter.”

Linton stared.

===Chinese===
「失手了！」邓斯特布尔说罢，动身去西摩家（Seymour's）找他的朋友林顿（Linton）。

「有信纸吗？」他问。

「多的是，」林顿说，「怎么？要用？」

「那就掏一张出来。我要口述一封信。」

林顿瞪眼了。

===Original===
“What’s up? Hurt your hand?”

Dunstable explained.

“Day collects autographs, you know, and he wants Montagu Watson’s badly. Pining away, and all that sort of thing. Won’t smile until he gets it. I had a shot at it yesterday, and got this.”

Linton inspected the document.

“So I can’t send up another myself, you see.”

===Chinese===
「怎么了？手伤了？」

邓斯特布尔解释了一番。

「你知道，戴先生收集签名，他可太想要蒙塔古·沃森的了。人都熬得形销骨立了，诸如此类。不到手就笑不出来。我昨天试过一回，得了这么个东西。」

林顿检视了那份文书。

「所以你瞧，我自己没法再寄一封了。」

===Original===
“Why worry?”

“Oh, I’d like to put Day one up. He’s not been bad this term. Come on.”

“All right. Let her rip.”

Dunstable let her rip.

> Dear Sir—I cannot refrain from writing to tell you what an inestimable comfort your novels have been to me during years of sore tribulation and distress—

“Look here,” interrupted Linton with decision at this point. “If you think I’m going to shove my name at the end of this rot, you’re making the mistake of a lifetime.”

===Chinese===
「操哪门子心？」

「嗐，我想成全戴先生一桩好事。这学期他干得不算坏。来吧。」

「行。你敞开了来。」

邓斯特布尔便敞开了来。

> 亲爱的先生——数年来我身处苦楚忧患之中，您的小说于我不啻无价慰藉，此情此恩，令我不能自已，提笔奉告——

「我说，」林顿在此处断然打断，「你要是以为我会把自己的名字签在这堆胡话末尾，那你可是犯了一辈子最大的错。」

===Original===
“Of course not. You’re a widow who has lost two sons in South Africa. We’ll think of a good name afterwards. Ready?”

> “Ever since my darling Charles Herbert and Percy Lionel were taken from me in that dreadful war, I have turned for consolation to the pages of *The Soul of Anthony Carrington* and—”

“What, another?” asked Linton.

“There’s one called *Pancakes*.”

“Sure? Sounds rummy.”

===Chinese===
「当然不会。你是一位在南非失去了两个儿子的寡妇。名字咱们回头想个好听的。预备好了？」

> 「自从我亲爱的查尔斯·赫伯特（Charles Herbert）和珀西·莱昂内尔（Percy Lionel）在那场可怕的战争中被夺去之后，我便转向《安东尼·卡林顿的灵魂》的书页寻求慰藉，并且——」

「怎么，还有一本？」林顿问。

「有一本叫《煎饼》（*Pancakes*）的。」

「拿得准？听着够古怪的。」

===Original===
“That’s all right. You have to get a queer title nowadays if you want to sell a book.”

“Go on, then. Jam it down.”

> “﻿—and *Pancakes*. I hate to bother you, but if you could send me your autograph I should be more grateful than words can say. Yours admiringly.”

“What’s a good name? How would Dorothy Maynard do?”

“You want something more aristocratic. What price Hilda Foulke-Ponsonby?”

Dunstable made no objection, and Linton signed the letter with a flourish.

===Chinese===
「没错儿。如今你想卖书，就得起个古怪书名。」

「那就接着写。快落笔。」

> 「——和《煎饼》。我不愿打扰您，可您若能赐寄亲笔签名，我的感激将非言语所能表达。您的敬慕者。」

「起个什么名字好？多萝西·梅纳德（Dorothy Maynard）怎么样？」

「得来个更贵族气些的。希尔达·富尔克-庞森比（Hilda Foulke-Ponsonby）这个名字如何？」

邓斯特布尔并无异议，林顿便挥毫签上了这个名字。

===Original===
They installed Mrs. Foulke-Ponsonby at Spiking’s in the High Street. It was not a very likely address for a lady whose blood was presumably of the bluest, but they could think of none except that obliging stationer who would take in letters for them.

There was a letter for Mrs. Foulke-Ponsonby next day. Whatever his other defects as a correspondent, Mr. Watson was at least prompt with his responses.

Mr. Montagu Watson presented his compliments, and was deeply grateful for all the kind things Mrs. Foulke-Ponsonby had said about his work in her letter of the 19th inst. He was, however, afraid that he scarcely deserved them. Her opportunities of deriving consolation from *The Soul of Anthony Carrington* had been limited by the fact that that book had only been published ten days before: while, as for *Pancakes*, to which she had referred in such flattering terms, he feared that another author must have the credit of any refreshment her bereaved spirit might have extracted from that volume, for he had written no work of such a name. His own *Pan Wakes* would, he hoped, administer an equal quantity of balm.

Mr. Secretary Morrison had slept badly on the night before he wrote this letter, and had expended some venom upon its composition.

===Chinese===
他们把富尔克-庞森比太太安顿在了高街（the High Street）的斯派金店（Spiking's）。对一位血统想必蓝到了顶的太太而言，这住址未免不大相称，可除了那家有求必应的文具商，他们实在想不出还有谁肯替他们代收信件。

第二天，富尔克-庞森比太太便有信可收。沃森先生作为通信人，别的毛病姑且不论，回信至少勤快。

蒙塔古·沃森先生谨致问候，并对富尔克-庞森比太太本月19日来函中对拙作所说的种种好话深表谢忱。只是他恐怕自己当不起这些美言。她从《安东尼·卡林顿的灵魂》中获取慰藉的机会，受限于这样一个事实：该书出版至今不过十日；至于她以那般溢美之词提及的《煎饼》，他担心她那居丧的心灵从该卷中汲取的任何滋养，功劳都得记到另一位作者头上——因为他从未写过以此为名的作品。他自己的《潘醒》（*Pan Wakes*），他希望，也能敷上等量的甘膏。

秘书莫里森先生写这封信的前一夜睡得很坏，行文之际便耗费了几分毒液。

===Original===
“Sold again!” said Dunstable.

“You’d better chuck it now. It’s no good,” said Linton.

“I’ll have another shot. Then I’ll try and think of something else.”

Two days later Mr. Morrison replied to Mr. Edgar Habbesham-Morley, of 3a, Green Street, Park Lane, to the effect that Mr. Montagu Watson was deeply grateful for all the kind things, etc.—

3a, Green Street was Dunstable’s home address.

At this juncture the Watson-Dunstable correspondence ceases, and the relations become more personal.

===Chinese===
「又被涮了一回！」邓斯特布尔说。

「你最好趁早收手。没用的。」林顿说。

「我再试一回。回头再想别的辙。」

两天后，莫里森先生给公园巷（Park Lane）绿街（Green Street）3a 号的埃德加·哈贝沙姆-莫利先生（Edgar Habbesham-Morley）回了信，大意是说，蒙塔古·沃森先生对种种美言深为感激，等等——

绿街3a号，是邓斯特布尔自家的住址。

至此，沃森与邓斯特布尔之间的翰墨往来宣告终止，双边关系转入更为贴身的阶段。

===Original===
On the afternoon of the twenty-third of the month, Mr. Watson, taking a meditative stroll through the wood which formed part of his property, was infuriated by the sight of a boy.

He was not a man who was fond of boys even in their proper place, and the sight of one in the middle of his wood, prancing lightly about among the nesting pheasants, stirred his never too placid mind to its depths.

He shouted.

The apparition paused.

“Here! Hi! you boy!”

“Sir?” said the stripling, with a winning smile, lifting his cap with the air of a D’Orsay.

===Chinese===
当月二十三日下午，沃森先生在他地产的一部分——那片林子里——作沉思漫步，赫然看见一个男孩，顿时怒从中来。

他本就不是个喜欢男孩的人，哪怕男孩们待在名正言顺该待的地方；如今见一个出现在他林子正中，在营巢的雉鸡之间轻盈地又蹦又跳，他那颗从来谈不上多平静的心，便被搅了个底朝天。

他大喝一声。

那团幻影停住了。

「喂！嘿！你那小子！」

「先生，有何见教？」那少年露出一个动人的微笑，以丹奥赛（D'Orsay，以翩翩风度著称的伯爵）式的派头举了举帽子。

===Original===
“What business have you in my wood?”

“Not business,” corrected the visitor, “pleasure.”

“Come here!” shrilled the novelist.

The stranger receded coyly.

Mr. Watson advanced at the double.

His quarry dodged behind a tree.

===Chinese===
「你到我的林子里来办什么事？」

「不是办事，」来客纠正道，「是来找乐子。」

「过来！」小说家尖声叫道。

那陌生人羞怯地往后退去。

沃森先生便以跑步的步法挺进。

他的猎物闪身躲到一棵树后。

===Original===
For five minutes the great man devoted his powerful mind solely to the task of catching his visitor.

The latter, however, proved as elusive as the point of a half-formed epigram, and at the end of the five minutes he was no longer within sight.

Mr. Watson went off and addressed his keeper in terms which made that worthy envious for a week.

“It’s eddication,” he said subsequently to a friend at the Cowslip Inn. “You and me couldn’t talk like that. It wants eddication.”

For the next few days the keeper’s existence was enlivened by visits from what appeared to be a most enthusiastic bird’s-nester. By no other theory could he account for it. Only a boy with a collection to support would run such risks.

===Chinese===
整整五分钟，这位伟人把他那颗强有力的头脑，全副奉献给了逮住来客这项任务。

然而后者被证明，像一句半成形警句的妙处一样难以捉摸，五分钟到头，他已在视野之外了。

沃森先生离去，对他家守林人发表了一通言辞，听得那位可敬的乡人艳羡了整整一个星期。

「那叫有文化，」他后来在库斯利普客栈（the Cowslip Inn）对一位朋友说，「你跟我可说不出那样的话。那得有文化才成。」

随后几天，一位看上去极其热衷掏鸟窝的角色频频来访，给守林人的生活添了不少生趣。除此之外，他想不出别的解释。只有身负一整座鸟蛋收藏要养的男孩，才会去冒这样的险。

===Original===
To the keeper’s mind the human boy up to the age of twenty or so had no object in life except to collect eggs. After twenty, of course, he took to poaching. This was a boy of about seventeen.

On the fifth day he caught him, and conducted him into the presence of Mr. Montagu Watson.

Mr. Watson was brief and to the point. He recognised his visitor as the boy for whose benefit he had made himself stiff for two days.

The keeper added further damaging facts.

“Bin here every day, he ’as, sir, for the last week. Well, I says to myself, supposition is he’ll come once too often. He’ll come once too often, I says. And then, I says, I’ll cotch him. And I cotched him.”

The keeper’s narrative style had something of the classic simplicity of Julius Caesar’s.

===Chinese===
在这位守林人看来，人类男孩自出生至二十岁上下，人生除了采鸟蛋别无目标。二十岁之后嘛，自然就转行偷猎了。眼前这位，约莫十七岁。

到了第五天，他把他逮住了，并押解到蒙塔古·沃森先生驾前。

沃森先生言简意赅，直奔要害。他认出来客，正是那个害他连着两天把自己弄得浑身僵直的男孩。

守林人又补充了几条更不利的事实。

「他天天都来，先生，整整一个礼拜了。俺就寻思，保不齐他来的回数一多，早晚有闪失。早晚有闪失，俺寻思。到时候，俺说，俺就逮他。俺这就逮着了。」

这位守林人的叙事文风，颇有几分尤利乌斯·凯撒（Julius Caesar）式的古典简洁。

===Original===
Mr. Watson bit his pen.

“What you boys come for I can’t understand,” he said irritably. “You’re from the school, of course?”

“Yes,” said the captive.

“Well, I shall report you to your housemaster. What is your name?”

“Dunstable.”

“Your house?”

“Day’s.”

===Chinese===
沃森先生咬了咬笔杆。

「你们这些男孩到底图个什么，我实在无法理解，」他烦躁地说，「你是学校来的，没错吧？」

「是。」俘虏答道。

「好，我要把这事报给你的舍监。你叫什么名字？」

「邓斯特布尔。」

「哪个宿舍的？」

「戴家（Day's）。」

===Original===
“Very good. That is all.”

Dunstable retired.

His next appearance in public life was in Mr. Day’s study. Mr. Day had sent for him after preparation. He held a letter in his hand, and he looked annoyed.

“Come in, Dunstable. I have just received a letter complaining of you. It seems that you have been trespassing.”

===Chinese===
「很好。就这样吧。」

邓斯特布尔退了下去。

他下一次在公众生活中露面，是在戴先生的书房。戴先生在晚自习后派人把他叫了去。他手里捏着一封信，面有愠色。

「进来，邓斯特布尔。我刚收到一封投诉你的信。看来你擅闯了别人的地界。」

===Original===
“Yes, sir.”

“I am surprised, Dunstable, that a sensible boy like you should have done such a foolish thing. It seems so objectless. You know how greatly the headmaster dislikes any sort of friction between the school and the neighbours, and yet you deliberately trespass in Mr. Watson’s wood.”

“I’m very sorry, sir.”

“I have had a most indignant letter from him—you may see what he says. You do not deny it?”

Dunstable ran his eye over the straggling, untidy sentences.

“No, sir. It’s quite true.”

===Chinese===
「是，先生。」

「我很诧异，邓斯特布尔，像你这样明白事理的孩子，竟会干出这么一件蠢事。看起来毫无目的可言。你知道校长何等厌恶学校与左邻右舍之间发生任何摩擦，可你还是蓄意闯进了沃森先生的林子。」

「我很抱歉，先生。」

「我收到了他一封怒不可遏的信——他说了什么，你可以亲自过目。你不否认吧？」

邓斯特布尔的目光扫过那满纸散乱邋遢的句子。

「不否认，先生。句句属实。」

===Original===
“In that case I shall have to punish you severely. You will write me out the Greek numerals ten times, and show them up to me on Tuesday.”

“Yes, sir.”

“That will do.”

At the door Dunstable paused.

“Well, Dunstable?” said Mr. Day.

“Er—I’m glad you’ve got his autograph after all, sir,” he said.

===Chinese===
「既然如此，我不得不重重罚你。你把希腊数字抄十遍，周二呈交给我。」

「是，先生。」

「就这样吧。」

到了门口，邓斯特布尔停了停。

「怎么，邓斯特布尔？」戴先生说。

「呃——您到底还是弄到了他的亲笔签名，我很替您高兴，先生。」他说。

===Original===
Then he closed the door.

As he was going to bed that night, Dunstable met the housemaster on the stairs.

“Dunstable,” said Mr. Day.

“Yes, sir.”

“On second thoughts, it would be better if, instead of the Greek numerals ten times, you wrote me the first ode of the first book of Horace. The numerals would be a little long, perhaps.”

===Chinese===
说完他关上了门。

当晚就寝时分，邓斯特布尔在楼梯上遇见了舍监。

「邓斯特布尔。」戴先生说。

「是，先生。」

「再一想，不抄十遍希腊数字了，改把贺拉斯（Horace）第一卷的第一首颂歌抄给我，这样更好。抄数字嘛，或许要稍长一点。」

## The Babe and the Dragon / 宝贝儿与恶龙

===Original===
The annual inter-house football cup at St. Austin’s lay between Dacre’s, who were the holders, and Merevale’s, who had been runner-up in the previous year, and had won it altogether three times out of the last five. The cup was something of a tradition in Merevale’s, but of late Dacre’s had become serious rivals, and, as has been said before, were the present holders.

This year there was not much to choose between the two teams. Dacre’s had three of the First Fifteen and two of the Second; Merevale’s two of the First and four of the Second. St. Austin’s being not altogether a boarding-school, many of the brightest stars of the teams were day boys, and there was, of course, always the chance that one of these would suddenly see the folly of his ways, reform, and become a member of a House.

This frequently happened, and this year it was almost certain to happen again, for no less a celebrity than MacArthur, commonly known as the Babe, had been heard to state that he was negotiating with his parents to that end. Which House he would go to was at present uncertain. He did not know himself, but it would, he said, probably be one of the two favourites for the cup. This lent an added interest to the competition, for the presence of the Babe would almost certainly turn the scale. The Babe’s nationality was Scots, and, like most Scotsmen, he could play football more than a little. He was the safest, coolest centre three-quarter the School had, or had had for some time. He shone in all branches of the game, but especially in tackling. To see the Babe spring apparently from nowhere, in the middle of an inter-school match, and bring down with violence a man who had passed the back, was an intellectual treat. Both Dacre’s and Merevale’s, therefore, yearned for his advent exceedingly. The reasons which finally decided his choice were rather curious. They arose in the following manner:

===Chinese===
圣奥斯汀（St. Austin's）一年一度的宿舍橄榄球杯，今年将在达克家（Dacre's）与梅雷维尔家（Merevale's）之间决出高下：达克家是现任捧杯人，梅雷维尔家去年屈居亚军，而最近五届统共三度捧杯。这只奖杯在梅雷维尔家多少已成一种传统，但近来达克家已成了劲敌，而且——如前文所述——如今捧杯的正是他们。

今年两队实力相去无几。达克家有三人入选一线队（First Fifteen），两人入选二线队；梅雷维尔家则两人入一线，四人入二线。圣奥斯汀并非全然的寄宿学校，各队里最亮的一些球星多为走读生，于是乎，其中一位随时可能突然悟出往日行径之愚妄，洗心革面，皈依某座宿舍，成为其寄宿成员。

这种事屡有发生，而今年几乎笃定要再度发生，因为响当当的名将麦克阿瑟（MacArthur）——人送绰号「宝贝儿」（the Babe）——已有人听他放话，说他正就此与父母磋商。他将投奔哪座宿舍，眼下尚在未定之天。他自己心里也没数，但据他说，多半会是杯赛的两大热门之一。这就给这场争夺平添了兴味，因为宝贝儿一旦驾到，天平几乎必定倾斜。宝贝儿原籍苏格兰，而一如多数苏格兰人，他打起橄榄球来绝非泛泛。他是全校现有、乃至多年来所曾有的最稳、最冷静的中路后锋（centre three-quarter）。这门运动样样他都出彩，尤以扑搂见长。在校际比赛正酣之际，眼看宝贝儿凭空跃出，将一名已甩开后卫的对手狠狠掀翻在地，实为一场智识的盛宴。因此之故，达克家与梅雷维尔家无不对他的降临渴慕之极。最终左右他抉择的缘由颇为离奇。事情是这么来的：

===Original===
The Babe’s sister was at Girton. A certain Miss Florence Beezley was also at Girton. When the Babe’s sister revisited the ancestral home at the end of the term, she brought Miss Beezley with her to spend a week. What she saw in Miss Beezley was to the Babe a matter for wonder, but she must have liked her, or she would not have gone out of her way to seek her company. Be that as it may, the Babe would have gone a very long way out of his way to avoid her company. He led a fine, healthy, out-of-doors life during that week, and doubtless did himself a lot of good. But times will occur when it is imperative that a man shall be under the family roof. Mealtimes, for instance. The Babe could not subsist without food, and he was obliged, Miss Beezley or no Miss Beezley, to present himself on these occasions. This, by the way, was in the Easter holidays, so that there was no school to give him an excuse for absence.

Breakfast was a nightmare, lunch was rather worse, and as for dinner, it was quite unspeakable. Miss Beezley seemed to gather force during the day. It was not the actual presence of the lady that revolted the Babe, for that was passable enough. It was her conversation that killed. She refused to let the Babe alone. She was intensely learned herself, and seemed to take a morbid delight in dissecting his ignorance, and showing everybody the pieces. Also, she persisted in calling him Mr. MacArthur in a way that seemed somehow to point out and emphasize his youthfulness. She added it to her remarks as a sort of afterthought or echo.

===Chinese===
宝贝儿的姐姐在格顿学院（Girton）读书。某位弗洛伦斯·比兹利小姐（Miss Florence Beezley）也在格顿。学期末，宝贝儿的姐姐回祖宅省亲，便把比兹利小姐一并带回，小住一周。姐姐究竟看中了比兹利小姐哪一点，在宝贝儿是一桩悬案，但她必定是喜欢她的，否则不会特意绕道去寻她做伴。姑且不论这个，为了躲开比兹利小姐的陪伴，宝贝儿自己肯绕的道可远得多。那一周里，他过着优质、健康、露天的户外生活，想必大有裨益。然而人活一世，总有非回到自家屋檐下不可的时刻。比如饭点。宝贝儿不吃东西没法活，于是不管有没有比兹利小姐，这种场合他都非露面不可。顺带一提，这正是复活节假期，学校无从为他提供缺席的口实。

早餐是一场噩梦，午餐更糟，至于晚餐，简直无以言状。比兹利小姐似乎随着白昼的推进而不断积攒功力。令宝贝儿反胃的倒不是这位女士的肉身在场——单论在场，还过得去。要命的是她的谈吐。她死活不肯放过宝贝儿。她自己满腹渊博，又似乎以解剖他的无知为乐——且是病态的乐——剖完还要把碎块陈列给众人过目。此外，她一口一个麦克阿瑟先生，那种叫法总好像在点明并强调他的少不更事。她把这个称呼缀在话尾，俨然一种补笔，或曰回声。

===Original===
“Do you read Browning, Mr. MacArthur?” she would say suddenly, having apparently waited carefully until she saw that his mouth was full.

The Babe would swallow convulsively, choke, blush, and finally say—

“No, not much.”

“Ah!” This in a tone of pity not untinged with scorn.

“When you say ‘not much,’ Mr. MacArthur, what exactly do you mean? Have you read any of his poems?”

“Oh, yes, one or two.”

===Chinese===
「麦克阿瑟先生，您读勃朗宁（Browning）吗？」她会突然发问，而且显然一直审慎守候，直到看见他嘴里塞得满满当当。

宝贝儿便猛地一咽，噎住，涨红了脸，末了说——

「不，不怎么读。」

「啊！」此语声调怜悯，又不无轻蔑。

「麦克阿瑟先生，您说『不怎么读』，究竟作何解？他的诗，您可曾读过几首？」

「哦，读过的，一两首吧。」

===Original===
“Ah! Have you read ‘Pippa Passes’?”

“No, I think not.”

“Surely you must know, Mr. MacArthur, whether you have or not. Have you read ‘Fifine at the Fair’?”

“No.”

“Have you read ‘Sordello’?”

“No.”

“What *have* you read, Mr. MacArthur?”

===Chinese===
「啊！那您读过《皮帕走过》（'Pippa Passes'）吗？」

「没有，我想没读过。」

「麦克阿瑟先生，读没读过，您总归是知道的吧。那您读过《集市上的菲菲娜》（'Fifine at the Fair'）吗？」

「没有。」

「读过《索尔代洛》（'Sordello'）吗？」

「没有。」

「那您*到底*读过什么呀，麦克阿瑟先生？」

===Original===
Brought to bay in this fashion, he would have to admit that he had read “The Pied Piper of Hamelin,” and not a syllable more, and Miss Beezley would look at him for a moment and sigh softly. The Babe’s subsequent share in the conversation, provided the Dragon made no further onslaught, was not large.

One never-to-be-forgotten day, shortly before the end of her visit, a series of horrible accidents resulted in their being left to lunch together alone. The Babe had received no previous warning, and when he was suddenly confronted with this terrible state of affairs he almost swooned. The lady’s steady and critical inspection of his style of carving a chicken completed his downfall. His previous experience of carving had been limited to those entertainments which went by the name of “study-gorges,” where, if you wanted to help a chicken, you took hold of one leg, invited an accomplice to attach himself to the other, and pulled.

But, though unskilful, he was plucky and energetic. He lofted the bird out of the dish on to the tablecloth twice in the first minute. Stifling a mad inclination to call out “Fore!” or something to that effect, he laughed a hollow, mirthless laugh, and replaced the errant fowl. When a third attack ended in the same way, Miss Beezley asked permission to try what she could do. She tried, and in two minutes the chicken was neatly dismembered. The Babe reseated himself in an overwrought state.

===Chinese===
被这样逼到绝境，他只得招认自己读过《哈默尔恩的花衣魔笛手》（"The Pied Piper of Hamelin"），此外再无一字，比兹利小姐便端详他片刻，轻轻一叹。此后，只要那恶龙（the Dragon）不再发起攻势，宝贝儿在谈话中分得的份额便寥寥无几。

她行将结束访问之前，有一个永难忘怀的日子，一连串可怕的意外，导致两人被单独留下共进午餐。宝贝儿事先未获任何警报，待到骤然直面这可怕的局势，他几乎晕厥。这位女士对他切鸡手法那沉着而挑剔的审视，将他彻底推落深渊。他以往操刀分肉的经验，仅限于那些号称「书房大宴」（study-gorges）的款待——在那样的场合，你若想分一只鸡，便一手攥住一条鸡腿，邀一位同伙搭上另一条，然后对拉。

然而他技虽不精，胆气与干劲俱全。头一分钟内，他两度将那只鸡高高挑出盘外，送上桌布。他强忍住狂呼一声「看球！」（Fore!）或诸如此类的疯狂冲动，发出一记空洞的、无欢的笑，把那只误入歧途的家禽放归原位。第三次进攻以同样方式告终之后，比兹利小姐请求获准一试身手。她试了，两分钟之内，那只鸡已被利落肢解完毕。宝贝儿重新落座，神经紧绷，心绪难平。

===Original===
“Tell me about St. Austin’s, Mr. MacArthur,” said Miss Beezley, as the Babe was trying to think of something to say—not about the weather. “Do you play football?”

“Yes.”

“Ah!”

A prolonged silence.

“Do you—” began the Babe at last.

“Tell me—” began Miss Beezley, simultaneously.

“I beg your pardon,” said the Babe; “you were saying—?”

“Not at all, Mr. MacArthur. *You* were saying—?”

===Chinese===
「跟我讲讲圣奥斯汀吧，麦克阿瑟先生。」比兹利小姐说，其时宝贝儿正搜肠刮肚想找句话说——只要不谈天气。「您打橄榄球吗？」

「打。」

「啊！」

一段漫长的沉默。

「您——」宝贝儿终于开了口。

「跟我讲讲——」比兹利小姐同时开了口。

「请您先讲，」宝贝儿说，「您方才要讲——？」

「哪里的话，麦克阿瑟先生。是*您*方才要讲——？」

===Original===
“I was only going to ask you if you played croquet?”

“Yes; do you?”

“No.”

“Ah!”

“If this is going to continue,” thought the Babe, “I shall be reluctantly compelled to commit suicide.”

There was another long pause.

===Chinese===
「我不过是想请问，您打槌球吗？」

「打的；您打吗？」

「不打。」

「啊！」

「这情形若再继续下去，」宝贝儿想，「我将不得不忍痛自尽了。」

又是一阵长长的冷场。

===Original===
“Tell me the names of some of the masters at St. Austin’s, Mr. MacArthur,” said Miss Beezley. She habitually spoke as if she were an examination paper, and her manner might have seemed to some to verge upon the autocratic, but the Babe was too thankful that the question was not on Browning or the higher algebra to notice this. He reeled off a list of names.

“… Then there’s Merevale—rather a decent sort—and Dacre.”

“What sort of a man is Mr. Dacre?”

“Rather a rotter, I think.”

“What is a rotter, Mr. MacArthur?”

“Well, I don’t know how to describe it exactly. He doesn’t play cricket or anything. He’s generally considered rather a crock.”

“Really! This is very interesting, Mr. MacArthur. And what is a crock? I suppose what it comes to,” she added, as the Babe did his best to find a definition, “is this, that you yourself dislike him.” The Babe admitted the impeachment. Mr. Dacre had a finished gift of sarcasm which had made him writhe on several occasions, and sarcastic masters are rarely very popular.

“Ah!” said Miss Beezley. She made frequent use of that monosyllable. It generally gave the Babe the same sort of feeling as he had been accustomed to experience in the happy days of his childhood when he had been caught stealing jam.

===Chinese===
「跟我讲讲圣奥斯汀几位老师的名字，麦克阿瑟先生。」比兹利小姐说。她惯于用考卷的口吻说话，那派头在有些人看来未免近乎专横，好在问题不出在勃朗宁或高等代数上，宝贝儿感激不尽，竟无暇计较这一层。他一口气报出一串名字。

「……再有是梅雷维尔——人挺正派——还有达克。」

「达克先生是怎样一个人？」

「我看，是个相当讨人嫌的家伙。」

「『讨人嫌的家伙』是什么意思，麦克阿瑟先生？」

「呃，我说不清该怎么确切形容。他不打板球，也不沾任何运动。大家普遍认为他是个十足的草包。」

「真的！这可真有意思，麦克阿瑟先生。那么『草包』又是什么？依我看，归结起来，」见宝贝儿竭力搜寻定义，她补充道，「无非是这么回事：您本人不喜欢他。」宝贝儿对这项指控照单全收。达克先生身怀一手炉火纯青的挖苦功夫，曾几度害得他浑身发扭，而挖功了得的老师难得大受欢迎。

「啊！」比兹利小姐说。这个单音节字她使用得颇为频繁。它通常给宝贝儿带来一种感觉，与童年幸福岁月里偷果酱被当场拿住的滋味一般无二。

===Original===
Miss Beezley went at last, and the Babe felt like a convict who has just received a free pardon.

One afternoon in the following term he was playing fives with Charteris, a prefect in Merevale’s House. Charteris was remarkable from the fact that he edited and published at his own expense an unofficial and highly personal paper, called *The Glow Worm*, which was a great deal more in demand than the recognized School magazine, *The Austinian*, and always paid its expenses handsomely.

Charteris had the journalistic taint very badly. He was always the first to get wind of any piece of School news. On this occasion he was in possession of an exclusive item. The Babe was the first person to whom he communicated it.

===Chinese===
比兹利小姐终于走了，宝贝儿自觉俨然一名刚获特赦的囚徒。

下学期的一个下午，他正和查特里斯（Charteris）打墙手球（fives），查特里斯是梅雷维尔家的级长。此君的奇特之处在于：他自掏腰包，编辑发行一份非官方而极带个人色彩的报纸，名叫《萤火虫》（*The Glow Worm*），比那份正式的校刊《奥斯汀人》（*The Austinian*）抢手得多，而且进项总能漂漂亮亮地抵偿开销。

查特里斯害着相当重的新闻瘾。校内任何一条新闻，他总是第一个嗅到风声。这一回，他手里正攥着一条独家消息。宝贝儿是他通报的第一人。

===Original===
“Have you heard the latest romance in high life, Babe?” he observed, as they were leaving the court. “But of course you haven’t. You never do hear anything.”

“Well?” asked the Babe, patiently.

“You know Dacre?”

“I seem to have heard the name somewhere.”

“He’s going to be married.”

“Yes. Don’t trouble to try and look interested. You’re one of those offensive people who mind their own business and nobody else’s. Only I thought I’d tell you. Then you’ll have a remote chance of understanding my quips on the subject in next week’s *Glow Worm*. You laddies frae the north have to be carefully prepared for the subtler flights of wit.”

“Thanks,” said the Babe, placidly. “Good night.”

===Chinese===
「最近上流社会的一桩罗曼史，你听说了吗，宝贝儿？」两人正走出球场时，他发话道，「不过你当然没听说。你是什么也听不见的。」

「哦？」宝贝儿耐心地问。

「你认识达克吧？」

「这名字我好像在哪儿听见过。」

「他要结婚了。」

「是啊。你也不必费劲装出感兴趣的样子。你就是那种可恨的家伙，只管自家的事，旁人的事一概不问。我只是想着还是告诉你一声，这样下星期的《萤火虫》上我就这个题目写的俏皮话，你还能有一线渺茫的机会看懂。你们这些打北边儿来的小家伙，得先经过悉心预习，才领略得了机智那较为精妙的凌空翱翔。」

「多谢，」宝贝儿心平气和地说，「晚安。」

===Original===
The Headmaster intercepted the Babe a few days after he was going home after a scratch game of football. “MacArthur,” said he, “you pass Mr. Dacre’s House, do you not, on your way home? Then would you mind asking him from me to take preparation tonight? I find I shall be unable to be there.” It was the custom at St. Austin’s for the Head to preside at preparation once a week; but he performed this duty, like the celebrated Irishman, as often as he could avoid it.

The Babe accepted the commission. He was shown into the drawing room. To his consternation, for he was not a society man, there appeared to be a species of tea-party going on. As the door opened, somebody was just finishing a remark.

“… faculty which he displayed in such poems as ‘Sordello,’ ” said the voice.

===Chinese===
几天之后，宝贝儿打完一场临时凑队的橄榄球赛正往家走，被校长半路截住。「麦克阿瑟，」他说，「你回家的路上要经过达克先生的宿舍，对吧？那就麻烦你替我捎话，请他今晚主持晚自习。我发现我去不成了。」按圣奥斯汀的惯例，校长每周须亲临主持一次晚自习；不过他履行这份职责的勤勉程度，一如那位大名鼎鼎的爱尔兰人——能免几回是几回。

宝贝儿领受了这桩差事。他被引进客厅。令他惊愕的是——他本非应酬场上的人——屋里似乎正进行着一场茶会。门开之际，有人的一句话刚好收尾。

「……他在《索尔代洛》一类诗篇中所展现的那种才华。」那嗓音说。

===Original===
The Babe knew that voice.

He would have fled if he had been able, but the servant was already announcing him. Mr. Dacre began to do the honours.

“Mr. MacArthur and I have met before,” said Miss Beezley, for it was she. “Curiously enough, the subject which we have just been discussing is one in which he takes, I think, a great interest. I was saying, Mr. MacArthur, when you came in, that few of Tennyson’s works show the poetic faculty which Browning displays in ‘Sordello.’”

===Chinese===
那嗓音，宝贝儿是认得的。

若是来得及，他早逃了，可仆人已在通传他的名姓。达克先生开始尽地主之谊。

「麦克阿瑟先生与我从前有过一面之缘，」比兹利小姐说——正是她。「说来也巧，我们方才讨论的题目，我想，恰是他极感兴趣的。麦克阿瑟先生，您进来时我正说：丁尼生（Tennyson）的作品里，鲜有能显出勃朗宁在《索尔代洛》里显出的那种诗才的。」

===Original===
The Babe looked helplessly at Mr. Dacre.

“I think you are taking MacArthur out of his depth there,” said Mr. Dacre. “Was there something you wanted to see me about, MacArthur?”

The Babe delivered his message.

“Oh, yes, certainly,” said Mr. Dacre. “Shall you be passing the School House tonight? If so, you might give the Headmaster my compliments, and say I shall be delighted.”

The Babe had had no intention of going out of his way to that extent, but the chance of escape offered by the suggestion was too good to be missed. He went.

===Chinese===
宝贝儿孤立无援地望向达克先生。

「我看，这话可让麦克阿瑟够不着底了。」达克先生说。「麦克阿瑟，你找我有什么事？」

宝贝儿传达了口信。

「哦，是的，当然。」达克先生说。「你今晚经过学舍（School House）吗？若经过，就替我向校长致意，并说我乐意之至。」

宝贝儿本没打算为这事绕这么远的路，但这话送上门来的脱身良机，好得不容错过。他去了。

===Original===
On his way he called at Merevale’s, and asked to see Charteris.

“Look here, Charteris,” he said, “you remember telling me that Dacre was going to be married?”

“Yes.”

“Well, do you know her name by any chance?”

“I ken it weel, ma braw Hielander. She is a Miss Beezley.”

“Great Scott!” said the Babe.

“Hullo! Why, was your young heart set in that direction? You amaze and pain me, Babe. I think we’d better have a story on the subject in *The Glow Worm*, with you as hero and Dacre as villain. It shall end happily, of course. I’ll write it myself.”

===Chinese===
路上他在梅雷维尔家停脚，求见查特里斯。

「我说，查特里斯，」他说，「你还记得你告诉过我达克要结婚了？」

「记得。」

「那么，你可知道那位小姐姓甚名谁？」

「俺知道得清清楚楚，俺俊俏的高地小子。她是一位比兹利小姐。」

「我的天！」宝贝儿说。

「嚯！怎么，你那颗少年的心竟是朝着那个方向安放的？你可叫我又惊又痛啊，宝贝儿。我看咱们最好在《萤火虫》上就这个题目登一篇小说，你当男主角，达克当反派。结局自然皆大欢喜。我亲自执笔。」

===Original===
“You’d better,” said the Babe, grimly. “Oh, I say, Charteris.”

“Well?”

“When I come as a boarder, I shall be a House-prefect, shan’t I, as I’m in the Sixth?”

“Yes.”

“And prefects have to go to breakfast and supper, and that sort of thing, pretty often with the House-beak, don’t they?”

“Such are the facts of the case.”

“Thanks. That’s all. Go away and do some work. Good night.”

===Chinese===
「你最好是写。」宝贝儿阴沉沉地说。「哦，我说，查特里斯。」

「怎么？」

「等我入校当寄宿生，我会做个宿舍级长，对吧——既然我在六年级？」

「对。」

「而级长们三天两头得跟舍监大人一起用早餐、进晚餐，诸如此类，是不是？」

「事实确系如此。」

「多谢。没别的事了。走开干活去吧。晚安。」

===Original===
The cup went to Merevale’s that year. The Babe played a singularly brilliant game for them.

===Chinese===
那一年，奖杯归了梅雷维尔家。宝贝儿为他们打出了一场格外出彩的比赛。

## The Guardian / 监护人

===Original===
In his Sunday suit (with ten shillings in specie in the right-hand trouser pocket) and a brand-new bowler hat, the youngest of the Shearnes, Thomas Beauchamp Algernon, was being launched by the combined strength of the family on his public-school career. It was a solemn moment. The landscape was dotted with relatives—here a small sister, awed by the occasion into refraining from insult; there an aunt, vaguely admonitory. “Well, Tom,” said Mr. Shearne, “you’ll soon be off now. You’re sure to like Eckleton. Remember to cultivate your bowling. Everyone can bat nowadays. And play forward, not outside. The outsides get most of the fun, certainly, but then if you’re a forward, you’ve got eight chances of getting into a team.”

===Chinese===
谢恩家（the Shearnes）的小儿子托马斯·博尚·阿尔杰农（Thomas Beauchamp Algernon），身着礼拜日礼服（右手裤袋里揣着十先令硬币），头戴一顶崭新的圆顶礼帽，正被全家的合力推上公学求学之路。这是一个庄严的时刻。四下景物间亲属星星点点——这边一个小妹妹，被此情此景震慑，暂时收敛了挖苦；那边一位姨妈，隐约带着训诫之意。「唔，汤姆，」谢恩先生说，「你这就快动身了。你准会喜欢埃克尔顿（Eckleton）。记着好好练你的投球。如今人人都会击球。还有，要踢前锋，别踢边锋。边锋固然最出风头，可你要是踢前锋，进球队的机会就有八份。」

===Original===
“All right, father.”

“Oh, and work hard.” This by way of an afterthought.

“All right, father.”

“And, Tom,” said Mrs. Shearne, “you are sure to be comfortable at school, because I asked Mrs. Davy to write to her sister, Mrs. Spencer, who has a son at Eckleton, and tell her to tell him to look after you when you get there. He is in Mr. Dencroft’s house, which is next door to Mr. Blackburn’s, so you will be quite close to one another. Mind you write directly you get there.”

“All right, mother.”

===Chinese===
「好的，爸爸。」

「哦，还有，要用功。」此话权充补笔。

「好的，爸爸。」

「还有，汤姆，」谢恩太太说，「你在学校里保管舒舒服服，因为我已经托戴维太太（Mrs. Davy）写信给她姐姐斯宾塞太太（Mrs. Spencer）——她有个儿子在埃克尔顿——让她转告他，等你到了那边要照应你。他住在登克罗夫特先生（Mr. Dencroft）的宿舍，紧挨着布莱克本先生（Mr. Blackburn）的宿舍，所以你们俩住得挺近。记着一到就写信回来。」

「好的，妈妈。」

===Original===
“And look here, Tom.” His eldest brother stepped to the front and spoke earnestly. “Look here, don’t you forget what I’ve been telling you?”

“All right.”

“You’ll be right enough if you don’t go sticking on side. Don’t forget that, however much of a blood you may have been at that rotten little private school of yours, you’re not one at Eckleton.”

“All right.”

“You look clean, which is the great thing. There’s nothing much wrong with you except cheek. You’ve got enough of that to float a ship. Keep it under.”

“All right. Keep your hair on.”

===Chinese===
「还有，你听着，汤姆。」他的大哥挺身向前，恳切地说道。「你听着，可别忘了我一路跟你念叨的话？」

「好的。」

「只要不摆架子，你准没事。别忘了：不管你在你那所烂透了的小私校里是多大的红人，到了埃克尔顿，你就不是红人了。」

「好的。」

「你看着干净体面，这是头等要事。你别的毛病不大，就是嘴欠。你那嘴欠的本钱，足够浮起一艘船。都给我收敛着点。」

「好的。你别上火了。」

===Original===
“There you go,” said the expert, with gloomy triumph. “If you say that sort of thing at Eckleton, you’ll get jolly well sat on, by Jove!”

“Bai Jove, old chap!” murmured the younger brother, “we’re devils in the Forty-twoth!”

The other, whose chief sorrow in life was that he could not get the smaller members of the family to look with proper awe on the fact that he had just passed into Sandhurst, gazed wistfully at the speaker, but, realising that there was a locked door between them, tried no active measures.

“Well, anyhow,” he said, “you’ll soon get it knocked out of you, that’s one comfort. Look here, if you do get scrapping with anybody, don’t forget all I’ve taught you. And I should go on boxing there if I were you, so as to go down to Aldershot some day. You ought to make a fairly decent featherweight if you practise.”

===Chinese===
「瞧瞧，来了不是，」这位行家带着阴郁的得意说道。「这种话你若是在埃克尔顿说出口，保管被人狠狠压服，老天爷在上！」

「拜——佐夫，老伙计！」做弟弟的咕哝道，「咱们四十二团里可全是小魔王！」

前者此生最大的伤心事，便是无法令家中更年幼的成员们以应有的敬畏看待他刚刚考入桑德赫斯特皇家军事学院（Sandhurst）这一事实；他怅然凝视着这位发言者，但随即意识到两人之间隔着一道上了锁的门，也就没有采取任何积极措施。

「唉，反正，」他说，「你这张嘴很快就有人替你敲打干净了，这是一桩安慰。听着，你要是真跟谁动起手来，可别忘了我的全部传授。换了我，到了那儿就接着练拳击，将来好去奥尔德肖特（Aldershot）露一手。只要你肯练，当个相当像样的羽量级拳手不在话下。」

===Original===
“All right.”

“Let’s know when Eckleton’s playing Haileybury, and I’ll come and look you up. I want to see that match.”

“All right.”

“Goodbye.”

“Goodbye, Tom.”

“Goodbye, Tom, dear.”

Chorus of aunts and other supers: “Goodbye, Tom.”

Tom (comprehensively): “G’bye.”

The train left the station.

===Chinese===
「好的。」

「埃克尔顿跟哈利伯里（Haileybury）比赛的时候言语一声，我来看你。这场比赛我是要看的。」

「好的。」

「再见。」

「再见，汤姆。」

「再见，亲爱的汤姆。」

众姨妈与其他跑龙套的齐声合唱：「再见，汤姆。」

汤姆（一总地）：「再会。」

火车驶出了车站。

---

===Original===
Kennedy, the head of Dencroft’s, said that when he wanted his study turned into a beastly furnace, he would take care to let Spencer know. He pointed out that just because it was his habit to warm the study during the winter months, there was no reason why Spencer should light the gas stove on an afternoon in the summer term when the thermometer was in the eighties. Spencer thought he might want some muffins cooked for tea, did he? Kennedy earnestly advised Spencer to give up thinking, as Nature had not equipped him for the strain. Thinking necessitated mental effort, and Spencer, in Kennedy’s opinion, had no mind, but rubbed along on a cheap substitute of mud and putty.

More chatty remarks were exchanged, and then Spencer tore himself away from the pleasant interview, and went downstairs to the junior study, where he remarked to his friend Phipps that Life was getting a bit thick.

===Chinese===
登克罗夫特家的舍长肯尼迪（Kennedy）表示，哪天他若想把书房变成一座可恶的火炉，一定会知会斯宾塞（Spencer）。他指出，不能仅仅因为他习惯在冬季数月里给书房生火取暖，斯宾塞就有理由在夏季学期里温度计蹿上华氏八十多度的某个下午点燃煤气炉。斯宾塞八成是以为他要点松饼当茶点吧，是不是？肯尼迪恳切地奉劝斯宾塞戒掉思考这门营生，因为造物主压根没给他配备承受这种劳损的零件。思考须费心力，而在肯尼迪看来，斯宾塞根本没有心智，全靠一种由烂泥和油灰拼凑的廉价代用品将就度日。

双方又交换了若干促膝之谈，随后斯宾塞才从这场愉快的会晤中恋恋不舍地抽身，下楼来到低年级书房，向他的朋友菲普斯（Phipps）发表感慨：日子未免稠得有点化不开了。

===Original===
“What’s up now?” inquired Phipps.

“Everything. We’ve just had a week of term, and I’ve been in extra once already for doing practically nothing, and I’ve got a hundred lines, and Kennedy’s been slanging me for lighting the stove. How was I to know he didn’t want it lit? Wish I was fagging for somebody else.”

“All the while you’re jawing,” said Phipps, “there’s a letter for you on the mantelpiece, staring at you?”

“So there is. Hullo!”
