“Yes, sir,” said Harrison.
There was a curious lack of enthusiasm in his voice.

===Chinese===
「什么事，先生？」
「校长要见你——又来一回。」
「好的，先生。」哈里森说。
他的嗓音里，有一种耐人寻味的意兴阑珊。

## The Politeness of Princes / 王子的礼貌

===Original===
The painful case of G. Montgomery Chapple, bachelor, of Seymour's house, Wrykyn. Let us examine and ponder over it.

It has been well said that this is the age of the specialist. Everybody, if they wish to leave the world a better and happier place for their stay in it, should endeavour to adopt some speciality and make it their own. Chapple's speciality was being late for breakfast. He was late not once or twice, but every day. Sometimes he would scramble in about the time of the second cup of coffee, buttoning his waistcoat as he sidled to his place. Generally he would arrive just as the rest of the house were filing out; when, having lurked hidden until Mr. Seymour was out of the way, he would enter into private treaty with Herbert, the factotum, who had influence with the cook, for Something Hot and maybe a fresh brew of coffee. For there was nothing of the amateur late-breakfaster about Chapple. Your amateur slinks in with blushes deepening the naturally healthy hue of his face, and, bolting a piece of dry bread and gulping down a cup of cold coffee, dashes out again, filled more with good resolutions for the future than with food. Not so Chapple. He liked his meals. He wanted a good deal here below, and wanted it hot and fresh. Conscience had but a poor time when it tried to bully Chapple. He had it weak in the first round.

===Chinese===
兹呈一桩令人痛心的案例：瑞金（Wrykyn）西摩家（Seymour's）的单身汉G·蒙哥马利·查普尔（G. Montgomery Chapple）。且让我们细细省察，再三玩味。

常言道得好：这是个专家的时代。人人若盼着自己逗留过的世界因这一遭而变得更美好、更幸福，就该努力选定一门专长，据为己有。查普尔的专长，是吃早饭迟到。他不是迟到一回两回，而是天天迟到。有时他赶在第二杯咖啡的当儿手脚并用地钻进门来，一边侧身蹭向自己的座位一边扣着马甲扣子。多数时候他到得恰巧，正赶上全宿舍的人鱼贯而出；这时他便先躲起来，等西摩先生（Mr. Seymour）走得没了影，再去同杂役赫伯特（Herbert）私下缔约——赫伯特在厨娘跟前说得上话——讨一点热乎吃食，说不定还有一壶现煮的咖啡。因为在迟到的早饭客里，查普尔绝无半点业余票友的做派。业余票友溜进来时，脸红得给原本健康的肤色又添几层晕，抓块干面包囫囵吞下，灌一杯凉咖啡，便又冲出门去——满肚子装的对未来的美好决心，多于装的食物。查普尔绝非这路人。他讲究吃。他在这个尘世上所要颇多，而且要热乎的、新鲜的。良心想欺负查普尔时，日子总不好过。头一个回合，它就被打蔫了。

===Original===
But there was one more powerful than Conscience—Mr. Seymour. He had marked the constant lateness of our hero, and disapproved of it.

Thus it happened that Chapple, having finished an excellent breakfast one morning some twenty minutes after everybody else, was informed as he sat in the junior day-room trying, with the help of an illustrated article in a boys' paper, to construct a handy model steam engine out of a reel of cotton and an old notebook—for his was in many ways a giant brain—that Mr. Seymour would like to have a friendly chat with him in his study. Laying aside his handy model steam engine, he went off to the housemaster's study.

===Chinese===
可比良心更厉害的还大有人在——西摩先生。我们这位主人公的一贯迟到，他早看在眼里，且颇为不满。

于是便有了这样一幕：某天早晨，查普尔比众人晚二十来分钟用完一顿丰盛的早餐，正坐在低年级活动室里，借着一本少年画报上的图解文章，试图用一轴棉线和一本旧笔记本造出一台轻便模型蒸汽机——须知他的脑子在许多方面都是个巨无霸——就在此时，有人来传话：西摩先生想在他的书房里同他友好地聊一聊。他把轻便模型蒸汽机搁置一旁，动身去了舍监的书房。

===Original===
"You were late for breakfast today," said Mr. Seymour, in the horrid, abrupt way housemasters have.

"Why, yes, sir," said Chapple, pleasantly.

"And the day before."

"Yes, sir."

"And the day before that."

Chapple did not deny it. He stood on one foot and smiled a propitiating smile. So far Mr. Seymour was entitled to demand a cigar or coconut every time.

===Chinese===
「你今天早饭迟到了。」西摩先生说道，用的是舍监们那种骇人的、直来直去的口吻。

「呃，是的，先生。」查普尔和颜悦色地答道。

「昨天也迟了。」

「是的，先生。」

「前天也迟了。」

查普尔不予否认。他单腿独立，堆出一脸息事宁人的笑。照此情形，西摩先生已有资格每回都讨一支雪茄或一只椰子了。

===Original===
The housemaster walked to the window, looked out, returned to the mantelpiece, and shifted the position of a china vase two and a quarter inches to the left. Chapple, by way of spirited repartee, stood on the other leg and curled the disengaged foot round his ankle. The conversation was getting quite intellectual.

"You will write out—"

"Sir, please, sir—" interrupted Chapple in an "I-represent-the-defendant-m'lud" tone of voice.

"Well?"

"It's awfully hard to hear the bell from where I sleep, sir."

===Chinese===
舍监踱到窗前，朝外望了望，又回到壁炉架旁，把一只瓷花瓶向左挪了两英寸又四分之一。查普尔则机锋相对，改用另一条腿站立，把腾出来的那只脚勾回到脚踝上。这场谈话眼看愈发高深起来了。

「你要罚抄——」

「先生，请容禀告，先生——」查普尔打断道，用的是一种「法官大人，本人谨为被告之辩护人」的腔调。

「讲。」

「从我睡觉的地方，实在很难听见铃响，先生。」

===Original===
Owing to the increased numbers of the house this term Chapple had been removed from his dormitory proper to a small room some distance away.

"Nonsense. The bell can be heard perfectly well all over the house."

There was reason in what he said. Herbert, who woke the house of a morning, did so by ringing a bell. It was a big bell, and he enjoyed ringing it. Few sleepers, however sound, could dream on peacefully through Herbert's morning solo. After five seconds of it they would turn over uneasily. After seven they would sit up. At the end of the first quarter of a minute they would be out of bed, and you would be wondering where they picked up such expressions.

Chapple murmured wordlessly in reply. He realised that his defence was a thin one. Mr. Seymour followed up his advantage.

===Chinese===
由于本学期宿舍人数增多，查普尔被从原本的寝室挪到了一间相距颇远的小屋里。

「胡说。那口铃全宿舍上下都听得一清二楚。」

他这话在理。每天早晨负责叫醒全宿舍的正是赫伯特，其手段便是摇铃。那是一口大铃，而且他摇得兴致盎然。任凭睡得再死，能伴着赫伯特的晨间独奏安然续梦的瞌睡虫少之又少。铃响五秒，他们便翻覆不安；七秒，便坐起身来；头一刻钟完结，人已站在床外，而你会纳闷这些字眼他们都是从哪儿学来的。

查普尔无言地咕哝了一声，权作回答。他意识到自己的辩词单薄得很。西摩先生乘胜追击。

===Original===
"You will write a hundred lines of Virgil," he said, "and if you are late again tomorrow I shall double them."

Chapple retired.

This, he felt, was a crisis. He had been pursuing his career of unpunctuality so long that he had never quite realised that a time might come when the authorities would drop on him. For a moment he felt that it was impossible, that he could not meet Mr. Seymour's wishes in the matter; but the bulldog pluck of the true Englishman caused him to reconsider this. He would at least have a dash at it.

===Chinese===
「你罚抄维吉尔一百行，」他说，「明天再迟到，就翻倍。」

查普尔告退了。

他感到，这是一场危机。他干不守时这一行当为时太久，久到从未想过当局兴许有朝一日会拿他开刀。有一瞬间他觉得这事没法办到，无法在此事上遂西摩先生的愿；但真正英国人那股斗牛犬式的胆气促使他重新掂量。好歹也要放手一搏。

===Original===
"I'll tell you what to do," said his friend, Brodie, when consulted on the point over a quiet pot of tea that afternoon. "You ought to sleep without so many things on the bed. How many blankets do you use, for instance?"

"I don't know," said Chapple. "As many as they shove on."

It had never occurred to him to reckon up the amount of his bedclothes before retiring to rest.

"Well, you take my tip," said Brodie, "and only sleep with one on. Then the cold'll wake you in the morning, and you'll get up because it'll be more comfortable than staying in bed."

This scientific plan might have worked. In fact, to a certain extent it did work. It woke Chapple in the morning, as Brodie had predicted; but it woke him at the wrong hour. It is no good springing out of bed when there are still three hours to breakfast. When Chapple woke at five the next morning, after a series of dreams, the scenes of which were laid mainly in the Arctic regions, he first sneezed, then he piled upon the bed everything he could find, including his boots, and then went to sleep again. The genial warmth oozed through his form, and continued to ooze until he woke once more, this time at eight-fifteen. Breakfast being at eight, it occurred to him that his position with Mr. Seymour was not improved. While he was devoting a few moments' profound meditation to this point the genial warmth got in its fell work once again. When he next woke, the bell was ringing for school. He lowered the world's record for rapid dressing, and was just in time to accompany the tail of the procession into the form-room.

===Chinese===
「我告诉你该怎么办，」当天下午，他的朋友布罗迪（Brodie）就此问题受讨教、两人对坐静品一壶茶时说道，「你睡觉不该在床上堆那么多东西。比方说，你盖几条毯子？」

「不知道，」查普尔说，「他们往上铺多少，我盖多少。」

就寝之前先清点自己被褥的数目，这种事他还从来没想起来过。

「这么着，听我的准没错，」布罗迪说，「只留一条盖着睡。这样早晨冷会把你冻醒，你就起来了，因为起床反倒比赖在床上舒服。」

这个科学的方案本可能奏效。事实上，它在一定程度上也确实奏效了。正如布罗迪所预言，它早晨把查普尔弄醒了；只是弄醒的时辰不对。距早饭还有三个钟头，从床上蹿起来毫无意义。第二天早晨五点，查普尔从一连串梦中醒来——那些梦的布景大多设在北极地区——他先打了个喷嚏，随即把寻得着的一切统统堆回床上，靴子亦在其列，接着重新睡去。融融暖意渗遍他的身子，并且继续渗下去，直到他再度醒来，这回是八点一刻。早饭八点开饭，他悟到自己在西摩先生那里的处境并未改善。他正就此点作片刻深沉的冥想，那融融暖意又施展起它罪恶的功夫来。待他下次醒来，上课铃已经响了。他把快速穿衣的世界纪录缩短了一截，堪堪赶上队伍的尾巴，一同进了教室。

===Original===
"You were late again this morning," said Mr. Seymour, after dinner.

"Yes, sir. I overslebbed myselb, sir," replied Chapple, who was suffering from a cold in the head.

"Two hundred lines."

"Yes, sir."

Things had now become serious. It was no good going to Brodie again for counsel. Brodie had done for himself, proved himself a fraud, an idiot. In fine, a rotter. He must try somebody else. Happy thought. Spenlow. It was a cold day, when Spenlow got left behind. He would know what to do. *There* was a chap for you, if you liked! Young, mind you, but what a brain! Colossal!

===Chinese===
「你今天早晨又迟到了。」午饭后，西摩先生说。

「是的，先生。我睡过头了，先生。」查普尔答道，他正患着伤风。

「两百行。」

「是的，先生。」

事态如今严峻起来了。再去向布罗迪讨主意已无用处。布罗迪算是把自己坑了个透，证明了自己是个骗子、一个白痴。总而言之，是个不中用的家伙。他得另请高明。灵光一闪：斯潘洛（Spenlow）。要说斯潘洛也有被难住的一天，除非太阳打西边出来。他准知道该怎么办。瞧，*这*才是块人物材料，就看你识不识货！年轻是年轻，可那副脑子！了不得！

===Original===
"What *I* should do," said Spenlow, "is this. I should put my watch on half an hour."

"What 'ud be the good of that?"

"Why, don't you see? You'd wake up and find it was ten to eight, say, by your watch, so you'd shove on the pace dressing, and nip downstairs, and then find that you'd really got tons of time. What price that?"

"But I should remember I'd put my watch on," objected Chapple.

"Oh, no, probably not. You'd be half asleep, and you'd shoot out of bed before you remembered, and that's all you'd want. It's the getting out of bed that's so difficult. If you were once out, you wouldn't want to get back again."

===Chinese===
「换了*我*，」斯潘洛说，「就这么办：把表拨快半个钟头。」

「那有什么用？」

「嘿，你还不明白？你一觉醒来，比如说，看见表上差十分八点，于是你加快手脚穿衣，一溜烟下了楼，结果发现其实时间绰绰有余。你说这法子值不值？」

「可我会记得自己把表拨快了呀。」查普尔提出异议。

「哦，不会，多半不会。你半睡半醒的，人还没等记起来就蹿出被窝了，这就齐活了。难的本来就是下床这一关。你只要人出来了，就断然不想再爬回去。」

===Original===
"Oh, shouldn't I?" said Chapple.

"Well, you might want to, but you'd have the sense not to do it."

"It's not a bad idea," said Chapple. "Thanks."

That night he took his Waterbury, prised open the face with a pocketknife as if he were opening an oyster, put the minute hand on exactly half an hour, and retired to bed satisfied. There was going to be no nonsense about it this time.

===Chinese===
「哦，是么？」查普尔说。

「呃，想或许还是会想的，但你会懂点分寸，不至于真那么干。」

「这主意不赖，」查普尔说，「多谢。」

当晚，他取出那块沃特伯里（Waterbury）表，用随身小刀撬开表盖，那架势活像在撬一只牡蛎，把分针整整拨快半个钟头，然后心满意足地上床安歇。这一回，可再不容出什么乱子了。

===Original===
I am sorry to disappoint the reader, but facts are facts, and I must not tamper with them. It is, therefore, my duty to state, however reluctantly, that Chapple was not in time for breakfast on the following morning. He woke at seven o'clock, when the hands of the watch pointed to seven-thirty. Primed with virtuous resolutions, he was just about to leap from his couch, when his memory began to work, and he recollected that he had still an hour. Punctuality, he felt, was an excellent thing, a noble virtue, in fact, but it was no good overdoing it. He could give himself at least another half hour. So he dozed off. He woke again with something of a start. He seemed to feel that he had been asleep for a considerable time. But no. A glance at the watch showed the hands pointing to twenty-five to eight. Twenty-five minutes more. He had a good long doze this time. Then, feeling that now he really must be getting up, he looked once more at the watch, and rubbed his eyes. It was still twenty-five to eight.

The fact was that, in the exhilaration of putting the hands on, he had forgotten that other and even more important operation, winding up. The watch had stopped.

There are few more disturbing sensations than that of suddenly discovering that one has no means of telling the time. This is especially so when one has to be in a certain place by a certain hour. It gives the discoverer a weird, lost feeling, as if he had stopped dead while all the rest of the world had moved on at the usual rate. It is a sensation not unlike that of the man who arrives on the platform of a railway station just in time to see the tail-end of his train disappear.

===Chinese===
我很抱歉让读者失望，可事实就是事实，我不敢篡改。因此，尽管万般不情愿，我也必须申明：第二天早晨，查普尔仍旧没能赶上早饭。他七点钟醒来，表针指着七点半。满怀崇高的决心，他正要从卧榻上一跃而起，记忆却运转起来，提醒自己还有一个钟头富余。他觉得，守时是桩美事，实为一种高贵的德行，但犯不着做过头。他至少还能再宽限自己半个钟头。于是他打起了盹。再醒来时他吓了一跳，觉得自己似乎已睡了许久。可是并不。一瞥表盘，指针指着差二十五分八点。还有二十五分钟。这一回他打了个又长又香的盹。随后，觉得此刻实在非起不可了，他再看一眼表，揉了揉眼睛。表上仍是差二十五分八点。

事实是，他光顾着拨针时那一阵兴奋，竟忘了另一桩更要紧的工序——上发条。表停了。

世上少有比猛然发觉自己无从得知时刻更叫人心慌意乱的感觉了。当一个人必须在某个钟点赶到某个地点时，尤其如此。这给发觉者一种古怪的、失落的感觉，仿佛他自己原地僵死了，而世上其余的一切都在照常速前进。这种滋味，同那种赶到站台、恰好来得及目送自己那趟车的尾巴消失的人所尝到的，相去不远。

===Original===
Until that morning the world's record for dressing (set up the day before) had been five minutes, twenty-three and a fifth seconds. He lowered this by two seconds, and went downstairs.

The house was empty. In the passage that led to the dining-room he looked at the clock, and his heart turned a somersault. *It was five minutes past nine.* Not only was he late for breakfast, but late for school, too. Never before had he brought off the double event.

There was a little unpleasantness in his form room when he stole in at seven minutes past the hour. Mr. Dexter, his form-master, never a jolly sort of man to have dealings with, was rather bitter on the subject.

===Chinese===
迄至那天早晨，穿衣的世界纪录（此前一天创下）为五分二十三又五分之一秒。他把这项纪录缩短了两秒，随即下楼去了。

宿舍里空无一人。在通往饭厅的过道里，他看了看钟，心肝儿翻了个跟头。*已是九点过五分。*他不光误了早饭，连上课也误了。这种双料战绩，他此前还从未一举拿下过。

当他在过了七分钟之后溜进教室时，教室里起了一点小小的不愉快。他的班主任德克斯特先生（Mr. Dexter）——同此人打交道从来算不上一件乐事——就这个题目发表了颇为刻毒的言论。

===Original===
"You are incorrigibly lazy and unpunctual," said Mr. Dexter, towards the end of the address. "You will do me a hundred lines."

"Oo-o-o, sir-r," said Chapple. But he felt at the time that it was not much of a repartee. After dinner there was the usual interview with Mr. Seymour.

"You were late again this morning," he said.

===Chinese===
「你懒惰成性，屡教不改地不守时，」训话临近尾声，德克斯特先生说道。「给我罚抄一百行。」

「呜——呜——呜，先——生。」查普尔应道。但他当时便觉出，这算不得什么妙对。午饭后，与西摩先生的例行会谈如期而至。

「你今天早晨又迟到了。」他说。

===Original===
"Yes, sir," said Chapple.

"Two hundred lines."

"Yes, sir."

The thing was becoming monotonous.

Chapple pulled himself together. This must stop. He had said that several times previously, but now he meant it. Nor poppy, nor mandragora, nor all the drowsy syrups of the world should make him oversleep himself again. This time he would try a combination of schemes.

===Chinese===
「是的，先生。」查普尔说。

「两百行。」

「是的，先生。」

这套流程正日渐单调乏味。

查普尔重整旗鼓。此风断不可再长。这话他先前已说过好几回，但这一回是动了真格的。罂粟也罢，曼德拉草也罢，普天下一切催眠的糖浆也罢，都休想再叫他睡过头。这一回，他要来个方案大联合。

===Original===
Before he went to bed that night he put his watch on half an hour, wound it up, and placed it on a chair at his bedside. Then he seized his rug and all the blankets except one, and tore them off. Then he piled them in an untidy heap in the most distant corner of the room. He meant to put temptation out of his reach. There should be no genial warmth on this occasion.

Nor was there. He woke at six feeling as if he were one solid chunk of ice. He put up with it in a torpid sort of way till seven. Then he could stand it no longer. It would not be pleasant getting up and going downstairs to the cheerless junior day-room, but it was the only thing to do. He knew that if he once wrapped himself in the blankets which stared at him invitingly from the opposite corner of the room, he was lost. So he crawled out of bed, shivering, washed unenthusiastically, and he proceeded to put on his clothes.

===Chinese===
那天夜里就寝之前，他把表拨快半个钟头，上了发条，搁在床边一把椅子上。然后他抄起旅行毯和除一条以外的全部毯子，一把扯了下来，胡乱堆到屋里最远的角落。他要把诱惑安置到自己够不着的地方。这一回，不许再有融融暖意。

也确实没有。他六点醒来，只觉得自己整块人是冰凿成的。他麻木迟钝地硬撑到七点，便再也扛不住了。起床下楼、去那间凄冷的低年级活动室固然不会好受，但除此之外别无他途。他清楚，只要往对面角落里那几条正向他频送秋波的毯子里一裹，他就全完了。于是他哆哆嗦嗦爬下床，兴味索然地洗了洗，动手穿起衣裳。

===Original===
Downstairs it was more unpleasant than one would have believed possible. The day-room was in its usual state of disorder. The fire was not lit. There was a vague smell of apples. Life was very, very grey. There seemed no brightness in it at all.

He sat down at the table and began once more the task of constructing a handy model steam engine, but he speedily realised, what he had suspected before, that the instructions were the work of a dangerous madman. What was the good of going on living when gibbering lunatics were allowed to write for weekly papers?

About this time his gloom was deepened by the discovery that a tin labelled mixed biscuits, which he had noticed in Brodie's locker, was empty.

He thought he would go for a stroll. It would be beastly, of course, but not so beastly as sitting in the junior day-room.

It is just here that the tragedy begins to deepen.

===Chinese===
楼下比人们所能相信的还要难熬。活动室一如既往地凌乱。炉火没有生。空气里飘着一股淡淡的苹果味。人生非常、非常灰暗，其中似乎没有半点亮色。

他在桌边坐下，重新投入制造轻便模型蒸汽机的大业，但很快便证实了他先前的疑心：那份说明书是某个危险疯子的手笔。既然满口呓语的痴子都获准给周刊写稿，再活下去还有什么意思？

大约就在此时，他又发现布罗迪储物柜里那听标着「什锦饼干」的铁罐竟空空如也——他先前还留意过它——愁云于是愈发深重。

他想，不如出去遛个弯儿。出去固然够呛，但总不至于像坐在低年级活动室里那么够呛。

正是在此处，悲剧开始走向深重。

===Original===
Passing out of Seymour's gate he met Brooke, of Appleby's. Brooke wore an earnest, thoughtful expression.

"Hullo, Brooke," said Chapple, "where are you off to?"

It seemed that Brooke was off to the carpenter's shop. Hence the earnest, thoughtful expression. His mind was wrestling with certain pieces of wood which he proposed to fashion into photograph frames. There was always a steady demand in the school for photograph frames, and the gifted were in the habit of turning here and there an honest penny by means of them.

The artist soul is not always unfavourable to a gallery. Brooke said he didn't mind if Chapple came along, only he wasn't to go rotting about or anything. So Chapple went along.

Arrived at the carpenter's shop, Brooke was soon absorbed in his labours. Chapple watched him for a time with the interest of a brother-worker, for had he not tried to construct handy model steam engines in his day? Indeed, yes. After a while, however, the role of spectator began to pall. He wanted to *do* something. Wandering round the room he found a chisel, and upon the instant, in direct contravention of the treaty respecting rotting, he sat down and started carving his name on a smooth deal board which looked as if nobody wanted it. The pair worked on in silence, broken only by an occasional hard breath as the toil grew exciting. Chapple's tongue was out and performing mystic evolutions as he carved the letters. He felt inspired.

===Chinese===
出了西摩家的大门，他迎面遇上阿普尔比家（Appleby's）的布鲁克（Brooke）。布鲁克面带一种恳切而深思的表情。

「喂，布鲁克，」查普尔说，「你上哪儿去？」

原来布鲁克是要去木工房。那副恳切深思的表情即由此而来。他的心思正与几块木料较劲，打算把它们做成相框。学校里对相框的需求向来稳定，有才华的同学便惯于借此四处挣几个清白的铜板。

艺术家的灵魂并不总是排斥看客。布鲁克说，查普尔若要同去他并不介意，只是不许他捣乱生事什么的。于是查普尔便同去了。

到了木工房，布鲁克很快便埋头于劳作之中。查普尔怀着同行兄长般的兴趣观摩了一阵——想当年，他又何尝没造过轻便模型蒸汽机？千真万确。然而不多时，看客这个角色便叫他腻味了。他想要亲手*干*点什么。他在屋里转悠，觅得一把凿子，当即——公然践踏关于不得捣乱的条约——坐下动手，在一块看上去无人认领的光洁松木板上刻起自己的名字。两人默默干着活，只是随着劳作渐入佳境，偶尔一声粗重的呼吸打破寂静。查普尔刻字母时舌头吐在外头，做着种种神秘的回旋。他自觉灵感沛然。

===Original===
He was beginning the A when he was brought to earth again by the voice of Brooke.

"You *are* an idiot," said Brooke, complainingly. "That's *my* board, and now you've spoilt it."

Spoilt it! Chapple liked that! Spoilt it, if you please, when he had done a beautiful piece of carving on it!

"Well, it can't be helped now," said Brooke, philosophically. "I suppose it's not your fault you're such an ass. Anyhow, come on now. It's struck eight."

===Chinese===
他正要落刀刻那个A，布鲁克的声音又将他拽回了人间。

「你可*真是*个蠢材，」布鲁克抱怨道，「那是*我的*木板，这下叫你给毁了。」

毁了！亏他说得出口！请诸位评评理——他分明在那上头完成了一幅精美的雕刻，居然说毁了！

「唉，事已至此，没法子了，」布鲁克一副达观的样子，「你天生是这么头驴，大概也怪不得你。不管怎样，走吧，已经敲过八点了。」

===Original===
"It's what?" gasped Chapple.

"Struck eight. But it doesn't matter. Appleby never minds one being a bit late for breakfast."

"Oh," said Chapple. "Oh, doesn't he!"

===Chinese===
「什么？」查普尔倒抽一口气。

「敲过八点了。不过不要紧，早饭晚到一小会儿，阿普尔比先生是从不计较的。」

「哦，」查普尔说，「哦，他老人家可真不计较！」

---

===Original===
Go into Seymour's at eight sharp any morning and look down the table, and you will see the face of G. M. Chapple—obscured every now and then, perhaps, by a coffee cup or a slice of bread and marmalade. He has not been late for three weeks. The spare room is now occupied by Postlethwaite, of the Upper Fourth, whose place in Milton's dormitory has been taken by Chapple. Milton is the head of the house, and stands alone among the house prefects for the strenuousness of his methods in dealing with his dormitory. Nothing in this world is certain, but it is highly improbable that Chapple will be late again. There are swagger-sticks.

===Chinese===
如今随便哪个早晨八点整走进西摩家，朝长桌那头望下去，你都会看见G·M·查普尔的那张脸——兴许不时会被一只咖啡杯或一片果酱面包遮去片刻。他已有三个星期不曾迟到了。那间原先的小屋如今住着上四年级的波斯尔思韦特（Postlethwaite），查普尔则顶了他的缺，搬进了弥尔顿（Milton）的寝室。弥尔顿是宿舍长，且在全舍诸位级长之中，以治寝手段之雷厉独步一时。世事无绝对，但查普尔再迟到的可能性微乎其微。那儿可有指挥杖（swagger-stick）呢。

## The Prize Poem / 诗歌奖

===Original===
Some quarter of a century before the period with which this story deals, a certain rich and misanthropic man was seized with a bright idea for perpetuating his memory after death, and at the same time harassing a certain section of mankind. So in his will he set aside a portion of his income to be spent on an annual prize for the best poem submitted by a member of the Sixth Form of St. Austin’s College, on a subject to be selected by the Headmaster. And, he added﻿—one seems to hear him chuckling to himself﻿—every member of the form must compete. Then he died. But the evil that men do lives after them, and each year saw a fresh band of unwilling bards goaded to despair by his bequest. True, there were always one or two who hailed this ready market for their sonnets and odes with joy. But the majority, being barely able to rhyme “dove” with “love,” regarded the annual announcement of the subject chosen with feelings of the deepest disgust.

The chains were thrown off after a period of twenty-seven years in this fashion.

===Chinese===
在本故事所涉年代之前约莫二十五年，有一位家财万贯而愤世嫉俗的先生灵光一闪，想出一个既能让自己身后留名、又可顺便折磨一干人类的高招。于是他在遗嘱里拨出收入的一股，充作年度奖金，奖给圣奥斯汀学院（St. Austin's College）六年级某位成员所呈交的最佳诗篇，题目由校长圈定。而且——他补充道——人们仿佛听得见他躲在一边咯咯窃笑——该年级人人必须参赛。随后他便去世了。然而，人所造的孽身后犹存，年复一年，他的遗赠都把一批新的、心不甘情不愿的行吟诗人逼入绝境。诚然，总有一两位把这里当作自家十四行诗与颂歌的现成市场，欢天喜地地来赶这个集。但大多数人连拿「dove」去押「love」的韵都勉为其难，对每年一度公布题目的消息，唯有报以最深切的嫌恶。

这副枷锁如此佩戴了二十七年之后，终以下述方式被挣脱。

===Original===
Reynolds of the Remove was indirectly the cause of the change. He was in the infirmary, convalescing after an attack of German measles, when he received a visit from Smith, an ornament of the Sixth.

“By Jove,” remarked that gentleman, gazing enviously round the sickroom, “they seem to do you pretty well here.”

“Yes, not bad, is it? Take a seat. Anything been happening lately?”

“Nothing much. I suppose you know we beat the M.C.C. by a wicket?”

“Yes, so I heard. Anything else?”

“Prize poem,” said Smith, without enthusiasm. He was not a poet.

===Chinese===
这场变革的间接肇因，是过渡班（the Remove）的雷诺兹（Reynolds）。他出过一场德国麻疹，正在医务室里将养，接待了六年级翘楚史密斯（Smith）的来访。

「我的老天，」那位先生环视病房四壁，艳羡地说道，「他们待你可不赖嘛。」

「是啊，挺不错，对吧？坐。近来有什么新鲜事么？」

「没什么大不了的。我们以一个三柱门的优势赢了 M.C.C.，这你想必知道了吧？」

「嗯，听说了。还有呢？」

「诗歌奖呗。」史密斯说，兴致缺缺。他不是诗人。

===Original===
Reynolds became interested at once. If there was one role in which he fancied himself (and, indeed, there were a good many), it was that of a versifier. His great ambition was to see some of his lines in print, and he had contracted the habit of sending them up to various periodicals, with no result, so far, except the arrival of rejected MSS. at mealtimes in embarrassingly long envelopes. Which he blushingly concealed with all possible speed.

“What’s the subject this year?” he asked.

“The College﻿—of all idiotic things.”

“Couldn’t have a better subject for an ode. By Jove, I wish I was in the Sixth.”

“Wish I was in the infirmary,” said Smith.

Reynolds was struck with an idea.

===Chinese===
雷诺兹顿时来了兴致。若说有哪一种角色最令他自我期许（事实上，这种角色着实不少），那便是韵文作者。他平生的大志，是亲眼见到自己的几行诗句变成铅字，为此养成了向各路刊物投稿的习惯——迄今毫无结果，唯一的动静，是每逢开饭，总有超长得叫人难为情的信封，把退稿的手稿送到面前。他红着脸，以最快的速度把它们藏匿了事。

「今年出什么题？」他问。

「《学院》——天底下的蠢题目全让它占了。」

「作颂歌再没有比这更好的题目了。老天在上，我真恨不得自己就在六年级。」

「我还盼自己在医务室呢。」史密斯说。

雷诺兹灵光一闪。

===Original===
“Look here, Smith,” he said, “if you like I’ll do you a poem, and you can send it up. If it gets the prize﻿—”

“Oh, it won’t get the prize,” Smith put in eagerly. “Rogers is a cert for that.”

“If it gets the prize,” repeated Reynolds, with asperity, “you’ll have to tell the Old Man all about it. He’ll probably curse a bit, but that can’t be helped. How’s this for a beginning?”

===Chinese===
「我说，史密斯，」他开口，「你要是愿意，我替你作一首，你交上去便是。要是得了奖——」

「哦，它得不了奖，」史密斯急急插话，「那奖罗杰斯（Rogers）稳拿。」

「要是得了奖，」雷诺兹语气尖刻地重说一遍，「你就得把原委统统告诉老头子（the Old Man，指校长）。他多半会骂上几句，可那也顾不得了。拿这个开头如何？」

===Original===
> “Imposing pile, reared up ’midst pleasant grounds,
> The scene of many a battle, lost or won,
> At cricket or at football; whose red walls
> Full many a sun has kissed ’ere day is done.”

===Chinese===
> 「巍峨的楼台，耸立在宜人的庭园，
> 曾见证多少战役，或输或赢，
> 或在板球场上，或足球场上；你那红墙
> 曾承受多少轮落日之吻，趁一日未终。」

===Original===
“Grand. Couldn’t you get in something about the M.C.C. match? You could make cricket rhyme with wicket.” Smith sat entranced with his ingenuity, but the other treated so material a suggestion with scorn.

“Well,” said Smith, “I must be off now. We’ve got a House-match on. Thanks awfully about the poem.”

===Chinese===
「妙极。能不能再塞点 M.C.C. 那场比赛进去？你可以拿『cricket』跟『wicket』押韵嘛。」史密斯为自家的急智而陶然，对方却对如此实惠的建议嗤之以鼻。

「好了，」史密斯说，「我得走了。还有一场宿舍对抗赛等着呢。诗的事，多谢了。」

===Original===
Left to himself, Reynolds set himself seriously to the composing of an ode that should do him justice. That is to say, he drew up a chair and table to the open window, wrote down the lines he had already composed, and began chewing a pen. After a few minutes he wrote another four lines, crossed them out, and selected a fresh piece of paper. He then copied out his first four lines again. After eating his pen to a stump, he jotted down the two words “boys” and “joys” at the end of separate lines. This led him to select a third piece of paper, on which he produced a sort of *edition de luxe* in his best handwriting, with the title “Ode to the College” in printed letters at the top. He was admiring the neat effect of this when the door opened suddenly and violently, and Mrs. Lee, a lady of advanced years and energetic habits, whose duty it was to minister to the needs of the sick and wounded in the infirmary, entered with his tea. Mrs. Lee’s method of entering a room was in accordance with the advice of the Psalmist, where he says, “Fling wide the gates.” She flung wide the gate of the sickroom, and the result was that what is commonly called “a thorough draught” was established. The air was thick with flying papers, and when calm at length succeeded storm, two editions of “Ode to the College” were lying on the grass outside.

===Chinese===
屋里只剩他一人，雷诺兹便郑重其事地着手炮制一首足以彰显其才的颂歌。也就是说，他把一把椅子和一张桌子拉到敞开的窗前，默写下已成的诗句，开始啃笔杆。几分钟后他又写了四行，划掉，另择一张新纸，把头四行重新誊了一遍。待到笔杆被啃成一截残桩，他在两行诗的末尾分别记下「boys」和「joys」两个词。这促使他选定第三张纸，在纸上以其最工整的书法出品了一种*豪华版*（edition de luxe），顶端用印刷体字母题着《学院颂》（Ode to the College）。他正欣赏着这份成品的雅洁效果，房门突然被人猛地推开——李太太（Mrs. Lee），一位年事已高而精力充沛的女士，职责是在医务室里照拂伤病员，端着他的茶进来了。李太太进屋的路数，深得《诗篇》作者的教诲——「众门哪，要大大敞开」。她把病房这扇门大大敞开，结果便形成了俗称「过堂风」的东西。空中纸片乱飞，等到风暴终于归于平静，《学院颂》的两个版本已经躺在外头的草地上了。

===Original===
Reynolds attacked the tea without attempting to retrieve his vanished work. Poetry is good, but tea is better. Besides, he argued within himself, he remembered all he had written, and could easily write it out again. So, as far as he was concerned, those three sheets of paper were a closed book.

===Chinese===
雷诺兹向茶发起进攻，并不打算去追索失踪的作品。诗固可贵，茶价更高。何况他心中自忖：写过的句句都还记着，重录一遍易如反掌。于是，就他而论，那三张纸已是合上的书一卷了。

===Original===
Later on in the afternoon, Montgomery of the Sixth happened to be passing by the infirmary, when Fate, aided by a sudden gust of wind, blew a piece of paper at him. “Great Scott,” he observed, as his eye fell on the words “Ode to the College.” Montgomery, like Smith, was no expert in poetry. He had spent a wretched afternoon trying to hammer out something that would pass muster in the poem competition, but without the least success. There were four lines on the paper. Two more, and it would be a poem, and capable of being entered for the prize as such. The words “imposing pile,” with which the fragment in his hand began, took his fancy immensely. A poetic afflatus seized him, and in less than three hours he had added the necessary couplet,

> How truly sweet it is for such as me
> To gaze on thee.

===Chinese===
当天下午迟些时候，六年级的蒙哥马利（Montgomery）恰好打医务室旁经过，命运借一阵狂风之助，朝他吹来一张纸。「乖乖，」他目光落在「学院颂」几个字上，评了一句。蒙哥马利和史密斯一样，于诗道并非行家。他为锤炼一篇能在诗赛里勉强交差的东西，熬过了一个苦不堪言的下午，却全无成效。纸上恰有四行。再添两行，它便是一首诗，便可据此报名参赛。手中这截残篇开头的「巍峨的楼台」几个字，令他大为倾倒。一阵诗的灵风攫住了他，不出三个钟头，必要的一联已然添就：

> 似我这般之人，凝望着你，
> 是何等真正甜美的事。

===Original===
“And dashed neat, too,” he said, with satisfaction, as he threw the manuscript into his drawer. “I don’t know whether ‘me’ shouldn’t be ‘I,’ but they’ll have to lump it. It’s a poem, anyhow, within the meaning of the act.” And he strolled off to a neighbour’s study to borrow a book.

Two nights afterwards, Morrison, also of the Sixth, was enjoying his usual during prep siesta in his study. A tap at the door roused him. Hastily seizing a lexicon, he assumed the attitude of the seeker after knowledge, and said, “Come in.” It was not the Housemaster, but Evans, Morrison’s fag, who entered with pride on his face and a piece of paper in his hand.

===Chinese===
「而且还干脆利落。」他一边把稿子扔进抽屉，一边满意地说。「我不晓得这个『me』是不是本该作『I』，可他们只好认了。反正依照法令释义，这是一首诗。」说罢，他溜达着上邻室书房借书去了。

两夜之后，同在六年级的莫里森（Morrison），正在自家书房里享用晚自习时的例行盹儿。叩门声把他惊醒。他慌忙抓过一部辞典，摆出求知者的姿态，说道：「进来。」进来的不是舍监，而是他的学仆（fag）埃文斯（Evans），脸上写满得意，手里捏着一张纸。

===Original===
“I say,” he began, “you remember you told me to hunt up some tags for the poem. Will this do?”

Morrison took the paper with a judicial air. On it were the words:

> Imposing pile, reared up ’midst pleasant grounds,
> The scene of many a battle, lost or won,
> At cricket or at football; whose red walls
> Full many a sun has kissed ’ere day is done.

===Chinese===
「我说，」他开口道，「您还记得吩咐我给那首诗寻几句可用的诗料吧。这个成么？」

莫里森以法官的气度接过纸来。纸上写着：

> 巍峨的楼台，耸立在宜人的庭园，
> 曾见证多少战役，或输或赢，
> 或在板球场上，或足球场上；你那红墙
> 曾承受多少轮落日之吻，趁一日未终。

===Original===
“That’s ripping, as far as it goes,” said Morrison. “Couldn’t be better. You’ll find some apples in that box. Better take a few. But look here,” with sudden suspicion, “I don’t believe you made all this up yourself. Did you?”

Evans finished selecting his apples before venturing on a reply. Then he blushed, as much as a member of the junior school is capable of blushing.

“Well,” he said, “I didn’t exactly. You see, you only told me to get the tags. You didn’t say how.”

“But how did you get hold of this? Whose is it?”

“Dunno. I found it in the field between the Pavilion and the infirmary.”

“Oh! well, it doesn’t matter much. They’re just what I wanted, which is the great thing. Thanks. Shut the door, will you?” Whereupon Evans retired, the richer by many apples, and Morrison resumed his siesta at the point where he had left off.

===Chinese===
「就现有的部分而言，帅极了，」莫里森说，「再好没有。那只箱子里有苹果，拿几个去。可是慢着，」他忽然起了疑，「我不信这些全是你自己想出来的。是不是？」

埃文斯拣完了苹果，才敢作答。随后他红了脸——以一个低年级生所能红到的最大限度。

「嗯，」他说，「也不全是。您瞧，您只吩咐我弄诗料，没说怎么弄。」

「可你是从哪儿弄来的？这是谁的？」

「不知道。在看亭（Pavilion）和医务室之间那块地上捡的。」

「哦！那也不要紧。正合我用，这才是要紧的。谢了。带上门，好吗？」于是埃文斯满载苹果退场，莫里森则从方才中断之处，继续打他的盹儿。

===Original===
“Got that poem done yet?” said Smith to Reynolds, pouring out a cup of tea for the invalid on the following Sunday.

“Two lumps, please. No, not quite.”

“Great Caesar, man, when’ll it be ready, do you think? It’s got to go in tomorrow.”

“Well, I’m really frightfully sorry, but I got hold of a grand book. Ever read﻿—?”

===Chinese===
「那首诗写完没有？」下一个礼拜天，史密斯给病号倒着茶，问雷诺兹道。

「请放两块糖。还没，还差一点。」

「我的天，老兄，你估摸着什么时候能写好？明天就得交卷了。」

「唉，实在对不住得很，可我弄到了一部了不得的好书。你读过——」

===Original===
“Isn’t any of it done?” asked Smith.

“Only the first verse, I’m afraid. But, look here, you aren’t keen on getting the prize. Why not send in only the one verse? It makes a fairly decent poem.”

“Hum! Think the Old ’Un’ll pass it?”

“He’ll have to. There’s nothing in the rules about length. Here it is if you want it.”

===Chinese===
「一点儿都没写？」史密斯问。

「恐怕只有第一节。不过你听着，你又不指望得奖。何不只交这一节？它也算一首蛮像样的诗。」

「哼！你觉得老头子会放行吗？」

「他不肯也得肯。规则里可没规定篇幅。你要的话，就在这儿。」

===Original===
“Thanks. I suppose it’ll be all right? So long! I must be off.”

The Headmaster, known to the world as the Rev. Arthur James Perceval, M.A., and to the School as the Old ’Un, was sitting at breakfast, stirring his coffee, with a look of marked perplexity upon his dignified face. This was not caused by the coffee, which was excellent, but by a letter which he held in his left hand.

===Chinese===
「谢了。我想总没问题吧？回见！我得走了。」

校长——世人称他阿瑟·詹姆斯·珀西瓦尔牧师（Rev. Arthur James Perceval），文学硕士，学校里则唤他「老头子」——正坐在早餐桌前搅着咖啡，那张威严的脸上分明写满困惑。这困惑并非咖啡所致——咖啡好得很——而是他左手握着的那封信所致。

===Original===
“Hum!” he said. Then “Umph!” in a protesting tone, as if someone had pinched him. Finally, he gave vent to a long-drawn “Um-m-m,” in a deep bass. “Most extraordinary. Really, most extraordinary. Exceedingly. Yes. Um. Very.” He took a sip of coffee.

“My dear,” said he, suddenly. Mrs. Perceval started violently. She had been sketching out in her mind a little dinner, and wondering whether the cook would be equal to it.

“Yes,” she said.

“My dear, this is a very extraordinary communication. Exceedingly so. Yes, very.”

“Who is it from?”

===Chinese===
「哼！」他说。接着又是一声「嗯呣！」，语调里透着抗议，仿佛有谁掐了他一把。最后，他以深沉的男低音发出一声悠长的『嗯————』。「异乎寻常。真的，异乎寻常。极其。是的。嗯。非常。」他呷了一口咖啡。

「亲爱的。」他突然开口。珀西瓦尔太太猛地一惊。她方才正在心里打一桌小宴的腹稿，盘算着厨娘能否胜任。

「嗯。」她说。

「亲爱的，这封信写得实在异乎寻常。异乎寻常之至。是的，非常。」

「是谁寄来的？」

===Original===
Mr. Perceval shuddered. He was a purist in speech. “*From whom*, you should say. It is from Mr. Wells, a great College friend of mine. I﻿—ah﻿—submitted to him for examination the poems sent in for the Sixth Form Prize. He writes in a very flippant style. I must say, very flippant. This is his letter:﻿—‘Dear Jimmy (really, really, he should remember that we are not so young as we were); dear﻿—ahem﻿—Jimmy. The poems to hand. I have read them, and am writing this from my sickbed. The doctor tells me I may pull through even yet. There was only one any good at all, that was Rogers’s, which, though﻿—er﻿—squiffy (tut!) in parts, was a long way better than any of the others. But the most taking part of the whole programme was afforded by the three comedians, whose efforts I enclose. You will notice that each begins with exactly the same four lines. Of course, I deprecate cribbing, but you really can’t help admiring this sort of thing. There is a reckless daring about it which is simply fascinating. A horrible thought﻿—have they been pulling your dignified leg? By the way, do you remember’﻿—the rest of the letter is﻿—er﻿—on different matters.”

===Chinese===
珀西瓦尔先生打了个寒颤。他在语言上是个纯正派。「应该说，『由何人所寄』。是韦尔斯（Wells）先生寄来的，我大学时代的一位至交。我——啊——把六年级诗歌奖收到的参赛诗作，送去请他评阅。他的信写得极为轻佻。不得不说，非常轻佻。信是这样写的：——「亲爱的吉米（真是的，真是的，他该记住咱们已不复当年年轻了）；亲爱的——咳——吉米。诗作收讫。我已拜读，此刻正从病榻上写这封信。医生说，我说不定还能挺过去。只有一首还算得上好，是罗杰斯的那首，虽然——呃——个别地方歪歪扭扭（啧！），还是比较其余诸作高出老大一截。但整台节目最抓人的部分，是由那三位喜剧演员贡献的，他们的大作随信附上。你当会注意到，每一篇的开头都是分毫不差的同样四行。当然，我不赞成抄袭，可这种事你实在不能不佩服。那股不管不顾的莽劲儿，简直迷人。一个可怕的念头——他们该不会一直在扯你那条庄严的老腿——拿你寻开心吧？顺便一提，你可还记得」——信的其余部分——呃——另涉他事。

===Original===
“James! How extraordinary!”

“Um, yes. I am reluctant to suspect﻿—er﻿—collusion, but really here there can be no doubt. No doubt at all. No.”

“Unless,” began Mrs. Perceval, tentatively. “No doubt at all, my dear,” snapped Reverend Jimmy. He did not wish to recall the other possibility, that his dignified leg was being pulled.

===Chinese===
「詹姆斯！真是异乎寻常！」

「嗯，是的。我不愿疑心——呃——串通，可眼下这桩，实在无可置疑。半点无疑。没有。」

「除非——」珀西瓦尔太太试探着开了口。「半点无疑也没有，亲爱的，」吉米牧师疾言厉色地截住。他不愿想起另一种可能：他那条庄严的老腿，正被人扯着玩呢。

===Original===
“Now, for what purpose did I summon you three boys?” asked Mr. Perceval, of Smith, Montgomery, and Morrison, in his room after morning school that day. He generally began a painful interview with this question. The method had distinct advantages. If the criminal were of a nervous disposition, he would give himself away upon the instant. In any case, it was likely to startle him. “For what purpose?” repeated the Headmaster, fixing Smith with a glittering eye.

“I will tell you,” continued Mr. Perceval. “It was because I desired information, which none but you can supply. How comes it that each of your compositions for the Poetry Prize commences with the same four lines?” The three poets looked at one another in speechless astonishment.

“Here,” he resumed, “are the three papers. Compare them. Now,”﻿—after the inspection was over﻿—“what explanation have you to offer? Smith, are these your lines?”

===Chinese===
「那么，我把你们三个叫来，所为何事？」当天上午课毕，珀西瓦尔先生在书房里对史密斯、蒙哥马利和莫里森发问。一场难堪的谈话，他照例以此问开场。此法好处分明：倘若犯错的那位神经脆弱，便会当场不打自招；即便不然，也多半能唬他一跳。「所为何事？」校长又问了一遍，一双亮眼死死盯住史密斯。

「我这就告诉你们，」珀西瓦尔先生接着说，「因为我想要一桩情报，而除你们三位之外无人能够提供。你们为诗歌奖呈交的三篇大作，何以每一篇都拿一模一样的四行开头？」三位诗人面面相觑，惊得说不出话。

「这儿，」他又说，「是那三份卷子。比对比对。好了，」——等三人验看完毕——「你们有何解释？史密斯，这几行是你的吗？」

===Original===
“I﻿—er﻿—ah﻿—*wrote* them, sir.”

“Don’t prevaricate, Smith. Are you the author of those lines?”

“No, sir.”

“Ah! Very good. Are you, Montgomery?”

“No, sir.”

“Very good. Then you, Morrison, are exonerated from all blame. You have been exceedingly badly treated. The first-fruit of your brain has been﻿—ah﻿—plucked by others, who toiled not neither did they spin. You can go, Morrison.”

===Chinese===
「我——呃——啊——是我*写*的，先生。」

「别支吾，史密斯。这几行诗的作者是你不是？」

「不是，先生。」

「啊！很好。蒙哥马利，你呢？」

「不是，先生。」

「很好。那么你，莫里森，一切罪责皆可豁免。你受了天大的亏待。你头脑的初熟果实——啊——已被他人摘去，而他们既不劳作，也不纺线。你可以走了，莫里森。」

===Original===
“But, sir﻿—”

“Well, Morrison?”

“I didn’t write them, sir.”

“I﻿—ah﻿—don’t quite understand you, Morrison. You say that you are indebted to another for these lines?”

“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===
「可是，先生——」

「怎么，莫里森？」

「那也不是我写的，先生。」

「我——啊——不甚明白你的意思，莫里森。你是说，这几行诗你受惠于他人？」

「是的，先生。」

===Original===
“To Smith?”

“No, sir.”

“To Montgomery?”

“No, sir.”

“Then, Morrison, may I ask to whom you are indebted?”

===Chinese===
「受惠于史密斯？」

「不是，先生。」

「受惠于蒙哥马利？」

「不是，先生。」

「那么，莫里森，可否请教你受惠于何人？」

===Original===
“I found them in the field on a piece of paper, sir.” He claimed the discovery himself, because he thought that Evans might possibly prefer to remain outside this tangle.

“So did I, sir.” This from Montgomery. Mr. Perceval looked bewildered, as indeed he was.

“And did you, Smith, also find this poem on a piece of paper in the field?” There was a metallic ring of sarcasm in his voice.

“No, sir.”

“Ah! Then to what circumstance were you indebted for the lines?”

“I got Reynolds to do them for me, sir.”

===Chinese===
「是在那块地上一张纸上捡到的，先生。」这桩发现之功，他径自认领了，因为他想，埃文斯说不定更乐意置身这团乱麻之外。

「我也是捡的，先生。」这回是蒙哥马利。珀西瓦尔先生露出大惑不解之色，他也确实大惑不解。

「那么你呢，史密斯，这首诗也是你在这块地上一张纸上捡到的么？」他的话音里带着金属般铿锵的讥讽。

「不是，先生。」

「啊！那么这几行诗，你所受惠的是何种机缘？」

「是我请雷诺兹替我写的，先生。」

===Original===
Montgomery spoke. “It was near the infirmary that I found the paper, and Reynolds is in there.”

“So did I, sir,” said Morrison, incoherently.

“Then am I to understand, Smith, that to gain the prize you resorted to such underhand means as this?”

“No, sir, we agreed that there was no danger of my getting the prize. If I had got it, I should have told you everything. Reynolds will tell you that, sir.”

“Then what object had you in pursuing this deception?”

“Well, sir, the rules say everyone must send in something, and I can’t write poetry at all, and Reynolds likes it, so I asked him to do it.”

===Chinese===
蒙哥马利开了口。「我那张纸，是在医务室近旁捡的，而雷诺兹正住在里头。」

「我那张也是，先生。」莫里森说得语无伦次。

「那么我是否应当理解为，史密斯：你为求获奖，竟使出这等上不得台面的手段？」

「不是的，先生。我们事先讲定，我根本没有得奖的危险。真要是得了奖，我会把一切据实相告。雷诺兹可以为此作证，先生。」

「那么你行此骗术，所图何在？」

「是这样的，先生：规则说人人必须交上一篇，可我压根不会作诗，雷诺兹又偏偏好这一口，所以我便请他代劳了。」

===Original===
And Smith waited for the storm to burst. But it did not burst. Far down in Mr. Perceval’s system lurked a quiet sense of humour. The situation penetrated to it. Then he remembered the examiner’s letter, and it dawned upon him that there are few crueller things than to make a prosaic person write poetry.

“You may go,” he said, and the three went.

And at the next Board Meeting it was decided, mainly owing to the influence of an exceedingly eloquent speech from the Headmaster, to alter the rules for the Sixth Form Poetry Prize, so that from thence onward no one need compete unless he felt himself filled with the immortal fire.

===Chinese===
史密斯候着雷霆发作。可雷霆没有发作。在珀西瓦尔先生的身心深处，蛰伏着一份不动声色的幽默感。眼前这局面径直渗到了它那里。他又想起评卷人的信，恍然悟到：世上比逼一个毫无诗性的人作诗更残忍的事，寥寥无几。

「去吧。」他说。三人便去了。

于是在下一次校董会议上——主要得力于校长一篇极其雄辩的演说——六年级诗歌奖的规则终获修改：自此以后，除非自觉胸中燃满不朽的诗情之火，任何人再无参赛之义务。

## “The Tabby Terror” / 「虎斑恐怖」

===Original===
The struggle between Prater’s cat and Prater’s cat’s conscience was short, and ended in the hollowest of victories for the former. The conscience really had no sort of chance from the beginning. It was weak by nature and flabby from long want of exercise, while the cat was in excellent training, and was, moreover, backed up by a strong temptation. It pocketed the stakes, which consisted of most of the contents of a tin of sardines, and left unostentatiously by the window. When Smith came in after football, and found the remains, he was surprised, and even pained. When Montgomery entered soon afterwards, he questioned him on the subject.

===Chinese===
普拉特家（Prater's）的猫与普拉特家的猫的良心之间的较量为期甚短，以猫方大获一场再空洞不过的胜利告终。良心从一开始就毫无胜算：它天生孱弱，又因长期缺乏锻炼而松松垮垮；猫却正处于极佳的竞技状态，况且背后还有一桩强劲的诱惑撑腰。它把赌注尽数收入囊中——战利品是一罐沙丁鱼的大半内容物——随后不事声张地自窗口退场。史密斯（Smith）踢完足球进屋，见到残局，又是惊讶，又是痛心。不多时蒙哥马利（Montgomery）走进屋来，他便就此事对他展开盘问。

===Original===
“I say, have you been having a sort of preliminary canter with the banquet?”

“No,” said Montgomery. “Why?”

“Somebody has,” said Smith, exhibiting the empty tin. “Doesn’t seem to have had such a bad appetite, either.”

“This reminds me of the story of the great bear, the medium bear, and the little ditto,” observed Montgomery, who was apt at an analogy. “You may remember that when the great bear found his porridge tampered with, he﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我说，你刚才是不是先给这场宴席来了个热身小跑？」

「没有，」蒙哥马利说，「怎么这么问？」

「反正有人跑过了，」史密斯说着，出示那只空罐头。「而且那一位的胃口看着还不赖。」

「这让我想起大熊、中熊和小小同款那档子故事，」擅长类比的蒙哥马利评论道。「你总记得吧，大熊发现自己的麦片粥遭了毒手之后，他——」

===Original===
At this point Shawyer entered. He had been bidden to the feast, and was feeling ready for it.

“Hullo, tea ready?” he asked.

Smith displayed the sardine tin in much the same manner as the conjurer shows a pack of cards when he entreats you to choose one, and remember the number.

“You haven’t finished already, surely? Why, it’s only just five.”

===Chinese===
正说间，肖耶（Shawyer）进来了。他也是这场宴席的受邀者，而且自觉腹中已然就绪。

「喂，茶点备好了？」他问。

史密斯亮出那只沙丁鱼罐头，那派头像极了魔术师请你从一副纸牌里任挑一张、并记住牌号时的光景。

「你们总不至于这就吃完了？嗨，才刚五点哪。」

===Original===
“We haven’t even begun,” said Smith. “That’s just the difficulty. The question is, who has been on the raid in here?”

“No human being has done this horrid thing,” said Montgomery. He always liked to introduce a Holmes-Watsonian touch into the conversation. “In the first place, the door was locked, wasn’t it, Smith?”

“By Jove, so it was. Then how on earth﻿—?”

“Through the window, of course. The cat, equally of course. I should like a private word with that cat.”

“I suppose it must have been.”

===Chinese===
「我们压根儿还没开吃，」史密斯说，「麻烦恰恰在这里。问题是：是谁进屋扫荡了？」

「这桩丑事绝非人类所为，」蒙哥马利说。他向来喜欢在谈话里注入一点福尔摩斯配华生的笔触。「首先，门是上了锁的，对吧，史密斯？」

「老天作证，可不是嘛。那究竟是打哪儿——？」

「自然是打窗户。自然也就是那只猫。我倒真想同那只猫单独谈几句。」

「想来必是它了。」

===Original===
“Of course it was. Apart from the merely circumstantial evidence, which is strong enough to hang it off its own bat, we have absolute proof of its guilt. Just cast your eye over that butter. You follow me, Watson?”

The butter was submitted to inspection. In the very centre of it there was a footprint.

“*I* traced his little footprints in the butter,” said Montgomery. “Now, is that the mark of a human foot?”

===Chinese===
「当然是它。且不说那些旁证——单凭旁证便足以给它定罪——我们手里还有它罪责的铁证。请把尊目投向那块黄油。听明白了吗，华生？」

黄油被呈堂备验。其正中央赫然有一个脚印。

「*我*可在黄油上追到了它的小脚印，」蒙哥马利说。「现在请看：这是人类脚掌的印记吗？」

===Original===
The jury brought in a unanimous verdict of guilty against the missing animal, and over a sorrowful cup of tea, eked out with bread and jam﻿—butter appeared to be unpopular﻿—discussed the matter in all its bearings. The cat had not been an inmate of Prater’s House for a very long time, and up till now what depredation it had committed had been confined to the official larder. Now, however, it had evidently got its hand in, and was about to commence operations upon a more extensive scale. The Tabby Terror had begun. Where would it end? The general opinion was that something would have to be done about it. No one seemed to know exactly what to do. Montgomery spoke darkly of bricks, bits of string, and horseponds. Smith rolled the word “rat-poison” luxuriously round his tongue. Shawyer, who was something of an expert on the range, babbled of airguns.

===Chinese===
陪审团对这只缺席到庭的动物一致裁定有罪。众人就着一杯凄凄惨惨的茶——以面包果酱勉强凑数，黄油似乎失了人心——将此事的方方面面讨论了个遍。这只猫入普拉特家为食客，时日尚浅，而此前它的劫掠行径一直局限于公用食品柜。如今它显然已经练熟了手，正要开展规模更宏大的行动。「虎斑恐怖」开始了。它将止于何处？普遍的意见是，总得对它有所处置。只是似乎谁也不知道究竟该怎么处置。蒙哥马利语带玄机地提到砖头、绳头和马塘。史密斯把「耗子药」三个字在舌尖上翻来覆去地咂摸，津津有味。肖耶——此君在靶场上算得一把好手——则一路念叨着气枪。

===Original===
At tea on the following evening the first really serious engagement of the campaign took place. The cat strolled into the tearoom in the patronizing way characteristic of his kind, but was heavily shelled with lump-sugar, and beat a rapid retreat. That was the signal for the outbreak of serious hostilities. From that moment its paw was against every man, and the tale of the things it stole is too terrible to relate in detail. It scored all along the line. Like Death in the poem, it knocked at the doors of the highest and the lowest alike. Or rather, it did not exactly knock. It came in without knocking. The palace of the prefect and the hovel of the fag suffered equally. Trentham, the head of the House, lost sausages to an incredible amount one evening, and the next day Ripton, of the Lower Third, was robbed of his one ewe lamb in the shape of half a tin of anchovy paste. Panic reigned.

===Chinese===
次日晚间的茶会上，本次战役中第一场真正要紧的交锋打响了。那只猫以其一族特有的居高临下之姿，信步踱入茶室，随即遭到方糖的密集炮击，只得仓皇撤退。这便是全面战事爆发的信号。自那一刻起，它的爪子与全宿舍之人为敌，而它所窃之物的账目，惨烈得不堪细表。它在整条战线上处处得手。一如诗中的死神，无论朱门蓬户，它一概叩门。或者不如说，它并不怎么叩门——它不叩门便径直进来。级长的深宫与低年级跑腿生的窝棚同罹其害。某晚，宿舍长特伦特姆（Trentham）损失的香肠数量骇人听闻；翌日，三年级低班的里普顿（Ripton）又被劫走了他的独一份心肝——半罐鳀鱼酱。一时恐慌弥漫。

===Original===
It was after this matter of the sausages that a luminous idea occurred to Trentham. He had been laid up with a slight football accident, and his family, reading between the lines of his written statement that he “had got crocked at footer, nothing much, only (rather a nuisance) might do him out of the House-matches,” a notification of mortal injuries, and seeming to hear a death-rattle through the words “felt rather chippy yesterday,” had come down en masse to investigate. En masse, that is to say, with the exception of his father, who said he was too busy, but felt sure it was nothing serious. (“Why, when I was a boy, my dear, I used to think nothing of an occasional tumble. There’s nothing the matter with Dick. Why, etc., etc.”)

===Chinese===
正是这场香肠风波之后，特伦特姆灵光乍现。他因一桩小小的足球事故卧床休养，家里人从他那份书面声明的字里行间——声明说他「踢球挂了彩，没什么大事，只是（颇为讨厌）兴许要错过宿舍赛」——读出了重伤通报，又仿佛从「昨天觉得有点发冷」几个字里听到了临终的喉鸣，于是举家浩浩荡荡杀到学校查探情由。所谓举家，也就是说，除去他的父亲——后者自称太忙，但断定绝非大事。（「嗐，亲爱的，我做孩子那会儿，偶尔摔个跟头算得了什么。迪克根本没毛病。想当年，如此等等，如此等等。」）

---

===Original===
Trentham’s sister was his first visitor.

“I say,” said he, when he had satisfied her on the subject of his health, “would you like to do me a good turn?”

She intimated that she would be delighted, and asked for details.

“*Buy the beak’s cat*,” hissed Trentham, in a hoarse whisper.

===Chinese===
特伦特姆的姐姐是第一位探视者。

「我说，」待他就健康状况令她彻底放了心，他开言道，「愿不愿意帮我个忙？」

她表示乐意之至，并请他细说端详。

「*把老学究的猫买下来*。」特伦特姆以嘶哑的耳语嘶声道。

===Original===
“Dick, it *was* your leg that you hurt, wasn’t it? Not﻿—not your head?” she replied. “I mean﻿—”

“No, I really mean it. Why can’t you? It’s a perfectly simple thing to do.”

“But what *is* a beak? And why should I buy its cat?”

“A beak’s a master. Surely you know that. You see, Prater’s got a cat lately, and the beast strolls in and raids the studies. Got round over half a pound of prime sausages in here the other night, and he’s always bagging things everywhere. You’d be doing everyone a kindness if you would take him on. He’ll get lynched some day if you don’t. Besides, you want a cat for your new house, surely. Keep down the mice, and that sort of thing, you know. This animal’s a demon for mice.” This was a telling argument. Trentham’s sister had lately been married, and she certainly had had some idea of investing in a cat to adorn her home. “As for beetles,” continued the invalid, pushing home his advantage, “they simply daren’t come out of their lairs for fear of him.”

===Chinese===
「迪克，你伤的*确确实实*是腿，对吧？不是——不是脑袋吧？」她应道，「我是说——」

「不，我是当真的。这有何难？再简单不过的事了。」

「可『老学究』*到底*是什么呀？我又凭什么去买他的猫？」

「老学究就是老师，这你总该知道。是这么回事：普拉特（Prater）先生新近养了一只猫，那畜生动辄晃进书房大肆劫掠。前两天夜里，光在这儿就叼走了半磅多上等香肠，而且它成天走哪儿顺哪儿。你若肯把它接过去，便是给大家行了一大善举。你不收留它，它早晚有一天要被私刑处死。再说了，你的新家总归要养只猫的吧。镇一镇耗子什么的，你懂的。这只捉起耗子来，简直是个魔头。」这一击颇见功力。特伦特姆的姐姐新近出阁，确实动过置办一只猫来装点门庭的念头。「至于蟑螂，」病号乘胜追击，继续说道，「它们纯粹是被它吓破了胆，连窝都不敢出。」

===Original===
“If he eats beetles,” objected his sister, “he can’t have a very good coat.”

“He doesn’t eat them. Just squashes them, you know, like a policeman. He’s a decent enough beast as far as looks go.”

“But if he steals things﻿—”

“No, don’t you see, he only does that here, because the Praters don’t interfere with him and don’t let us do anything to him. He won’t try that sort of thing on with you. If he does, get somebody to hit him over the head with a bootjack or something. He’ll soon drop it then. You might as well, you know. The House’ll simply black your boots if you do.”

===Chinese===
「它要是吃蟑螂，」他姐姐提出异议，「毛色肯定好不了。」

「它不吃。只是把它们拍扁了事，你懂的，跟警察一个路数。论卖相，这畜生还算体面。」

「可它要是偷东西——」

「不，你还没明白吗？它只有在这儿才来这套，因为普拉特夫妇不管教它，也不许我们对它动一根指头。到了你那儿，它不会故技重施的。真要犯贱，找个人拿靴拔子照它脑门敲那么一下什么的，它很快便改了。你就收下吧，啊？你若办成这事，全宿舍的人抢着替你擦鞋都愿意。」

===Original===
“But would Mr. Prater let me have the cat?”

“Try him, anyhow. Pitch it fairly warm, you know. Only cat you ever loved, and that sort of thing.”

“Very well. I’ll try.”

“Thanks, awfully. And, I say, you might just look in here on your way out and report.”

===Chinese===
「可普拉特先生肯把猫给我吗？」

「好歹试试嘛。话说得动情些，你懂的。什么『生平只爱过这一只猫』之类的。」

「好吧。我试试。」

「多谢多谢。哦对了，你出去的时候顺道来这儿一趟，汇报汇报。」

---

===Original===
Mrs. James Williamson, née Miss Trentham, made her way dutifully to the Merevale’s part of the House. Mrs. Prater had expressed a hope that she would have some tea before catching her train. With tea it is usual to have milk, and with milk it is usual, if there is a cat in the house, to have feline society. Captain Kettle, which was the name thought suitable to this cat by his godfathers and godmothers, was on hand early. As he stood there pawing the mat impatiently, and mewing in a minor key, Mrs. Williamson felt that here was the cat for her. He certainly was good to look upon. His black heart was hidden by a sleek coat of tabby fur, which rendered stroking a luxury. His scheming brain was out of sight in a shapely head.

===Chinese===
詹姆斯·威廉姆森太太（Mrs. James Williamson）——闺名特伦特姆小姐——于是恪尽职守地往宿舍里梅雷维尔家（Merevale's）的那一头去了。普拉特太太（Mrs. Prater）已表示，希望她赶火车之前用些茶点。有茶便照例有奶；家中有猫而又有奶，便照例有猫先生的社交。凯特尔上尉（Captain Kettle）——教父教母们认为适合这只猫的名字——早已恭候多时。它站在那儿，不耐烦地刨着地垫，用小调咪咪叫着，威廉姆森太太当下便觉得：这就是属于我的猫。它确实经得起端详。那颗黑心藏在一身油光水滑的虎斑皮毛之下，抚摸起来便成了一种享受。那颗诡计多端的大脑，则收拢在一颗匀停俊俏的头颅里，深藏不露。

===Original===
“Oh, what a lovely cat!” said Mrs. Williamson.

“Yes, isn’t he,” agreed Mrs. Prater. “We are very proud of him.”

“Such a beautiful coat!”

“And such a sweet purr!”

“He looks so intelligent. Has he any tricks?”

===Chinese===
「哦，多俊的一只猫！」威廉姆森太太说。

「可不是嘛，」普拉特太太深表赞同。「我们得意着呢。」

「这身皮毛真漂亮！」

「呼噜打得也甜！」

「瞧着这么伶俐。它会什么把戏吗？」

===Original===
Had he any tricks! Why, Mrs. Williamson, he could do everything except speak. Captain Kettle, you bad boy, come here and die for your country. Puss, puss.

Captain Kettle came at last reluctantly, died for his country in record time, and flashed back again to the saucer. He had an important appointment. Sorry to appear rude and all that sort of thing, don’t you know, but he had to see a cat about a mouse.

===Chinese===
它会不会把戏！嗐，威廉姆森太太，它除了开口说话，样样都会。凯特尔上尉，你这个坏孩子，过来，为国捐躯。咪咪，咪咪。

凯特尔上尉磨蹭再三，总算过来，以破纪录的速度为国捐了躯，随即一闪身又扑回碟子旁边。它有一桩要紧的约会。失陪之处还望海涵，诸如此类的规矩您是懂的——本猫得去会一只老鼠。

===Original===
“Well?” said Trentham, when his sister looked in upon him an hour later.

“Oh, Dick, it’s the nicest cat I ever saw. I shall never be happy if I don’t get it.”

“Have you bought it?” asked the practical Trentham.

“My dear Dick, I couldn’t. We couldn’t bargain about a cat during tea. Why, I never met Mrs. Prater before this afternoon.”

===Chinese===
「怎么样？」一小时后姐姐过来探望时，特伦特姆问。

「哦，迪克，那是我平生所见最好的猫。要是得不到它，我这辈子都快活不起来。」

「买了没有？」讲求实效的特伦特姆问。

「亲爱的迪克，哪能呢。总不能在喝茶的当口为一只猫讨价还价吧。再说我今天下午才头一回见到普拉特太太。」

===Original===
“No, I suppose not,” admitted Trentham, gloomily. “Anyhow, look here, if anything turns up to make the beak want to get rid of it, I’ll tell him you’re dead nuts on it. See?”

For a fortnight after this episode matters went on as before. Mrs. Williamson departed, thinking regretfully of the cat she had left behind her.

===Chinese===
「嗯，这倒是。」特伦特姆阴郁地承认。「反正这么着吧：回头但凡出点什么岔子，让那位老学究动了打发它的念头，我就告诉他，你迷它迷得不行。懂了吗？」

这桩公案之后，有两个礼拜光景，一切照旧运转。威廉姆森太太动身走了，一路怅然想着被她留在身后的那只猫。

===Original===
Captain Kettle died for his country with moderate regularity, and on one occasion, when he attempted to extract some milk from the very centre of a fag’s tea-party, almost died for another reason. Then the end came suddenly.

===Chinese===
凯特尔上尉以中等的规律为国捐躯；其间有一回，它试图从一位低年级跑腿生茶会的正中央榨取一点牛奶，险些为另一个缘故捐了躯。随后，结局来得猝不及防。

===Original===
Trentham had been invited to supper one Sunday by Mr. Prater. When he arrived it became apparent to him that the atmosphere was one of subdued gloom. At first he could not understand this, but soon the reason was made clear. Captain Kettle had, in the expressive language of the man in the street, been and gone and done it. He had been left alone that evening in the drawing room, while the House was at church, and his eye, roaming restlessly about in search of evil to perform, had lighted upon a cage. In that cage was a special sort of canary, in its own line as accomplished an artiste as Captain Kettle himself. It sang with taste and feeling, and made itself generally agreeable in a number of little ways. But to Captain Kettle it was merely a bird. One of the poets sings of an acquaintance of his who was so constituted that “a primrose by the river’s brim a simple primrose was to him, and it was nothing more.” Just so with Captain Kettle. He was not the cat to make nice distinctions between birds. Like the cat in another poem, he only knew they made him light and salutary meals. So, with the exercise of considerable ingenuity, he extracted that canary from its cage and ate it. He was now in disgrace.

===Chinese===
某个礼拜天，特伦特姆受邀到普拉特先生处用晚饭。他一到场便察觉，满室气氛是一派压抑的愁云。起初他不明就里，但很快原委便水落石出。用街头百姓那句传神的话说，凯特尔上尉这回可是「闯下大祸喽」。那天晚上全宿舍的人去教堂做礼拜，它被独自留在客厅里；它的眼珠四处游弋，不安分地搜寻可做之恶，终于定格在一只鸟笼上。笼中是一只名贵品种的金丝雀，在它那一行里，是与凯特尔上尉本人同样造诣湛深的艺术家。它歌喉隽雅，情致缠绵，还在种种小处惹人怜爱。可对凯特尔上尉而言，它不过是一只鸟。某位诗人曾咏过他的一位旧识，说此人生就一副心肠，「河边的樱草花，在他眼里只是一株寻常的黄色樱草花，再无其他」。凯特尔上尉正是如此。它可不是那种会在鸟类之间细加分辩的猫。一如另一首诗里的猫，它只懂得一点：鸟儿能做成一顿清淡而滋补的美餐。于是，凭借相当可观的机巧，它把那只金丝雀从笼中抽取出来，吃掉了。它如今彻底失宠了。

===Original===
“We shall have to get rid of him,” said Mr. Prater.

“I’m afraid so,” said Mrs. Prater.

“If you weren’t thinking of giving him to anyone in particular, sir,” said Trentham, “my sister would be awfully glad to take him, I know. She was very keen on him when she came to see me.”

“That’s excellent,” said Prater. “I was afraid we should have to send him to a home somewhere.”

“I suppose we can’t keep him after all?” suggested Mrs. Prater.

Trentham waited in suspense.

===Chinese===
「咱们只好把它打发掉了。」普拉特先生说。

「恐怕只好如此。」普拉特太太说。

「先生，您若没有打算把它特别送给什么人，」特伦特姆说，「我姐姐准乐意收留它，这我知道。她来看我那阵子，就顶喜欢它了。」

「那再好不过，」普拉特说。「我还发愁咱们得把它送到哪家收容所去呢。」

「我们终究还是留不得它了？」普拉特太太试探道。

特伦特姆屏息悬望。

===Original===
“No,” said Prater, decidedly. “I think *not*.” So Captain Kettle went, and the House knew him no more, and the Tabby Terror was at an end.

===Chinese===
「留不得，」普拉特斩钉截铁地说。「我*看*留不得。」于是凯特尔上尉走了，全宿舍从此与它缘尽，虎斑恐怖遂告终结。

## The Tom Brown Question / 汤姆·布朗问题

===Original===
The man in the corner had been trying to worry me into a conversation for some time. He had asked me if I objected to having the window open. He had said something rather bitter about the War Office, and had hoped I did not object to smoking. Then, finding that I stuck to my book through everything, he made a fresh attack.

===Chinese===
角落里的那位先生想方设法要把我拖进一场交谈，已经有些时候了。他问过我是否介意开着车窗。他就陆军部发过一通颇为刻薄的议论，又希望我不介意他抽烟。随后，他见我任凭什么都只管埋头读书，便发起了新一轮攻势。

===Original===
“I see you are reading *Tom Brown’s Schooldays*,” he said.

This was a plain and uninteresting statement of fact, and appeared to me to require no answer. I read on.

===Chinese===
「我瞧见您在读《汤姆·布朗的求学时代》，」他说。

这是一句平平无奇的事实陈述，在我看来无须作答。我继续读我的书。

===Original===
“Fine book, sir.”

“Very.”

“I suppose you have heard of the Tom Brown Question?”

I shut my book wearily, and said I had not.

===Chinese===
「好书啊，先生。」

「很好。」

「想必您听说过『汤姆·布朗问题』了吧？」

我倦倦地合上书，说没听说过。

===Original===
“It is similar to the Homeric Question. You have heard of that, I suppose?”

I knew that there was a discussion about the identity of the author of the Iliad. When at school I had been made to take down notes on the subject until I had grown to loathe the very name of Homer.

===Chinese===
「它与荷马问题（Homeric Question）性质相仿。那一个您总听说过吧？」

我知道世人对《伊利亚特》作者究竟是何人，确有一场争论。上学那阵子，我曾被迫就这个题目记下满纸笔记，直到对荷马这个名字本身都恨之入骨。

===Original===
“You see,” went on my companion, “the difficulty about *Tom Brown’s Schooldays* is this. It is obvious that part one and part two were written by different people. You admit that, I suppose?”

“I always thought Mr. Hughes wrote the whole book.”

“Dear me, not really? Why, I thought everyone knew that he only wrote the first half. The question is, who wrote the second. I know, but I don’t suppose ten other people do. No, sir.”

===Chinese===
「您瞧，」我的同伴继续说道，「《汤姆·布朗的求学时代》的疑难就在这里：第一部与第二部显然出自不同人之手。这一点您总得承认吧？」

「我一向以为全书都出自休斯先生（Mr. Hughes）一人之手。」

「哎呀，当真如此？咳，我还以为人人皆知他只写了前半部呢。问题就在：后半部是谁写的。我知道，可我想除我之外，再没有十个人知道了。没有，先生。」

===Original===
“What makes you think he didn’t write the second part?”

“My dear sir, just read it. Read part one carefully, and then read part two. Why, you can see in a minute.”

I said I had read the book three times, but had never noticed anything peculiar about it, except that the second half was not nearly so interesting as the first.

===Chinese===
「您凭什么认为第二部不是他写的？」

「我亲爱的先生，您只管去读。细细读完第一部，再读第二部。咳，一眼便见分晓。」

我说这本书我已读过三遍，却从未察觉任何异样之处，只除了后半部远不及前半部有趣。

===Original===
“Well, just tell me this. Do you think the same man created East and Arthur? Now then.”

I admitted that it was difficult to understand such a thing.

===Chinese===
「那么，只请您回答我一句：您以为伊斯特（East）和亚瑟（Arthur）是同一个人创造出来的吗？说吧。」

我承认，这样一桩事实在叫人难以理解。

===Original===
“There was a time, of course,” continued my friend, “when everybody thought as you do. The book was published under Hughes’s name, and it was not until Professor Burkett-Smith wrote his celebrated monograph on the subject that anybody suspected a dual, or rather a composite, authorship. Burkett-Smith, if you remember, based his arguments on two very significant points. The first of these was a comparison between the football match in the first part and the cricket match in the second. After commenting upon the truth of the former description, he went on to criticize the latter. Do you remember that match? You do? Very well. You recall how Tom wins the toss on a plumb wicket?”

===Chinese===
「当然，曾有一个时期，人人都和您想的一样，」我的朋友继续说道。「书是以休斯先生的名义出版的，直到伯基特-史密斯教授（Professor Burkett-Smith）就这一题目写出了他那部鼎鼎大名的专著，方才有人疑心此书出自双人——毋宁说众人拼合——之手。您如果还记得，伯基特-史密斯的论证建立在两个极有分量的要点之上。头一个要点，是把第一部里的橄榄球赛与第二部里的板球赛作一番比较。他在称道了前一描写之逼真以后，转而批评后一场比赛。您还记得那场比赛吧？记得？很好。您可记得，汤姆是怎么在一条状态绝佳的球道上掷币获胜的？」

===Original===
“Yes.”

“Then with the usual liberality of young hands (I quote from the book) he put the M.C.C. in first. Now, my dear sir, I ask you, would a school captain do that? I am young, says one of Gilbert’s characters, the Grand Duke, I think, but, he adds, I am not so young as that. Tom may have been young, but would he, *could* he have been young enough to put his opponents in on a true wicket, when he had won the toss? Would the Tom Brown of part one have done such a thing?”

“Never,” I shouted, with enthusiasm.

===Chinese===
「记得。」

「随后，他便以年轻手笔惯有的豪爽（此语引自原书），让 M.C.C.（玛丽勒本板球俱乐部）率先上场击球。好了，我亲爱的先生，我倒要请问，一位校队队长会干出这种事来吗？『我还年轻』，吉尔伯特（Gilbert）笔下一个人物说——我想是那位大公爵——『不过，』他又补上一句，『我可没有年轻到那个地步。』汤姆也许是年轻，可他会、*能够*年轻到这个地步，以至掷币赢了之后，竟让对手在一条完好的球道上先行击球吗？第一部里的那个汤姆·布朗会干出这种事来吗？」

「绝不会，」我满怀热忱地高声喊道。

===Original===
“But that’s nothing to what he does afterwards. He permits, he actually sits there and permits, comic songs and speeches to be made during the luncheon interval. Comic Songs! Do you hear me, sir? *Comic Songs!!* And this when he wanted every minute of time he could get to save the match. Would the Tom Brown of part one have done such a thing?”

“Never, never.” I positively shrieked the words this time.

===Chinese===
「可这比起他后来的行径，还算不得什么。他准许——他当真坐在那儿，听任——午餐间歇里有人唱滑稽歌、发表演说。滑稽歌！您听见我的话了吗，先生？*滑稽歌！！*而这还是在他巴不得一分钟掰成两半用、好歹保住这场比赛的时候。第一部里的那个汤姆·布朗会干出这种事来吗？」

「绝不会，绝不会。」这一回，我简直是尖着嗓子喊出这几个字的。

===Original===
“Burkett-Smith put that point very well. His second argument is founded on a single remark of Tom’s, or rather—”

“Or rather,” I interrupted, fiercely, “or rather of the wretched miserable—”

“Contemptible,” said my friend.

“Despicable, scoundrelly, impostor who masquerades as Tom in the second half of the book.”

===Chinese===
「伯基特-史密斯把这一点阐述得十分透辟。他的第二个论据，则建立在汤姆的一句话上，或者不如说——」

「不如说，」我怒气冲冲地打断了他，「不如说是那个卑劣下流的——」

「可鄙的。」我的朋友说。

「可恨的、恶棍一般的、在书的后半部冒充汤姆的那个骗子。」

===Original===
“Exactly,” said he. “Thank you very much. I have often thought the same myself. The remark to which I refer is that which he makes to the master while he is looking on at the M.C.C. match. In passing, sir, might I ask you whether the Tom Brown of part one would have been on speaking terms with such a master?”

===Chinese===
「正是，」他说。「多谢您了。我自己也时常作如是想。我所指的那句话，是他旁观 M.C.C. 那场比赛时，对那位老师说的。顺带请教，先生，第一部里的汤姆·布朗，会跟这样一位老师有说话的交情吗？」

===Original===
I shook my head violently. I was too exhausted to speak.

“You remember the remark? The master commented on the fact that Arthur is a member of the first eleven. I forget Tom’s exact words, but the substance of them is this, that, though on his merits Arthur was not worth his place, he thought it would do him such a lot of good being in the team. Do I make myself plain, sir? He—thought—it—would—do—him—such—a—lot—of—good—being—in—the—team!!!”

===Chinese===
我狠狠地摇头。我已筋疲力尽，话都说不动了。

「您记得那句话吧？那位老师当时点评说，亚瑟竟成了一线校队（first eleven）的一员。汤姆的原话我记不真了，大意却是：虽说论真本领，亚瑟配不上他的位置，但他认为，进队对他大有好处。我把话说清楚了吗，先生？他——认为——入队——对他——大有——好处！！！」

===Original===
There was a pause. We sat looking at one another, forming silently with our lips the words that still echoed through the carriage.

===Chinese===
一阵沉默。我们对坐相望，双唇无声地翕动，复诵着那仍在车厢里回荡的几个字。

===Original===
“Burkett-Smith,” continued my companion, “makes a great deal of that remark. His peroration is a very fine piece of composition. ‘Whether (concludes he) the captain of a school cricket team who could own spontaneously to having been guilty of so horrible, so terrible an act of favouritismical jobbery, who could sit unmoved and see his team being beaten in the most important match of the season (and, indeed, for all that the author tells us it may have been the only match of the season), for no other reason than that he thought a first eleven cap would prove a valuable tonic to an unspeakable personal friend of his, whether, I say, the Tom Brown who acted thus could have been the Tom Brown who headed the revolt of the fags in part one, is a question which, to the present writer, offers no difficulties. I await with confidence the verdict of a free, enlightened, and conscientious public of my fellow-countrymen.’ Fine piece of writing, that, sir?”

===Chinese===
「伯基特-史密斯，」我的同伴继续说道，「对那句话大加发挥。他的收束之笔是一篇漂亮至极的文章。『一名学校板球队的队长，（他如此作结）竟能自动招认自己犯下了这样骇人、这样可怕的偏袒徇私、任人唯亲式舞弊之罪；竟能无动于衷地坐着，眼看本队在全季最要紧的一场比赛里败下阵来（而且，就作者肯告诉我们的来看，那未尝不是全季唯一的一场比赛）——理由无他，只因他认为一顶一线校队的球帽，于他一位不可名状的私交好友将是一剂珍贵的补药；这样的一个人，我说，究竟还是不是第一部里率领低年级跑腿生揭竿而起的那个汤姆·布朗——这个问题，在笔者看来，毫无疑难可言。笔者满怀信心，静候我自由、开明、有良知的同胞公众作出裁决。』妙文一篇哪，先生，您说是不是？」

===Original===
“Very,” I said.

“That pamphlet, of course, caused a considerable stir. Opposing parties began to be formed, some maintaining that Burkett-Smith was entirely right, others that he was entirely wrong, while the rest said he might have been more wrong if he had not been so right, but that if he had not been so mistaken he would probably have been a great deal more correct. The great argument put forward by the supporters of what I may call the ‘One Author’ view, was, that the fight in part two could not have been written by anyone except the author of the fight with Flashman in the schoolhouse hall. And this is the point which has led to all the discussion. Eliminate the Slogger Williams episode, and the whole of the second part stands out clearly as the work of another hand. But there is one thing that seems to have escaped the notice of everybody.”

“Yes?” I said.

===Chinese===
「很好。」我说。

「那本小册子自然引起了相当一场轰动。对立各派随即开始形成：一派坚称伯基特-史密斯全然正确，另一派断言他全然错误，其余诸公则表示，他若不是对了这么多，本可以错得更远；但若他没有错成那样，多半又会正确得多。拥护我不妨称之为『独一作者』说的诸君所祭出的头号论据是：第二部里那场恶斗，除了校舍大厅里弗拉什曼（Flashman）一战的原作者，谁也写不出来。而正是这一点，引出了全部的争论。把斯洛格·威廉姆斯（Slogger Williams）那段插曲剔除出去，整个第二部便赫然显出是另一只手的作品。不过有一件事，似乎逃过了所有人的眼睛。」

「哦？」我说。

===Original===
He leant forward impressively, and whispered. “Only the actual fight is the work of the genuine author. The interference of Arthur has been interpolated!”

“By Jove!” I said. “Not really?”

“Yes. Fact, I assure you. Why, think for a minute. Could a man capable of describing a fight as that fight is described, also be capable of stopping it just as the man the reader has backed all through is winning? It would be brutal. Positively brutal, sir!”

===Chinese===
他郑重其事地俯身向前，压低嗓音。「只有打斗本身出自真正作者之手。亚瑟的横加干预，是后人窜插进去的！」

「天哪！」我说。「不会吧？」

「会的。千真万确，我向您保证。咳，您且想上一分钟。一个能把一场打斗写到这等地步的人，会在读者从头到尾押注的那一位眼看就要得胜之际，又忍心把它生生拦停吗？那未免太残忍了。简直是残忍透顶，先生！」

===Original===
“Then, how do you explain it?”

“A year ago I could not have told you. Now I can. For five years I have been unravelling the mystery by the aid of that one clue. Listen. When Mr. Hughes had finished part one, he threw down his pen and started to Wales for a holiday. He had been there a week or more, when one day, as he was reclining on the peak of a mountain looking down a deep precipice, he was aware of a body of men approaching him. They were dressed soberly in garments of an inky black. Each had side whiskers, and each wore spectacles. ‘Mr. Hughes, I believe?’ said the leader, as they came up to him.

===Chinese===
「那么，您作何解释？」

「一年之前，我还无从奉告。如今可以了。五年来，我就凭着那一条线索，把这桩谜案层层解开。听着。休斯先生写完第一部，掷下笔，便动身去威尔士（Wales）度假。他在那里盘桓了一个多礼拜。有一天，他正斜倚在一座山的峰顶上，俯瞰一道深不见底的悬崖，忽觉有一队人朝他走来。那些人一身墨黑的衣装，穿得肃穆整肃。人人蓄着连鬓胡，人人戴着眼镜。『休斯先生吧？』为首的一位走到他跟前时说道。

===Original===
“ ‘Your servant, sir,’ said he.

“ ‘We have come to speak to you on an important matter, Mr. Hughes. We are the committee of the Secret Society For Putting Wholesome Literature Within The Reach Of Every Boy, And Seeing That He Gets It. I, sir, am the president of the S.S.F.P.W.L.W.T.R.O.E.B.A.S.T.H.G.I.’ He bowed.

“ ‘Really, sir, I—er—don’t think I have the pleasure,’ began Mr. Hughes.

===Chinese===
「『愿为您效劳，先生。』他答道。

「『我们是为一件要事前来面见，休斯先生。我们是「使有益文学置于每个男孩伸手可及之处、并确保他真正到手秘密协会」的委员会。本人，先生，即是 S.S.F.P.W.L.W.T.R.O.E.B.A.S.T.H.G.I. 之会长。』他鞠了一躬。

「『当真吗，先生，我——呃——怕是没有这份荣幸吧。』休斯先生开口道。

===Original===
“ ‘You shall have the pleasure, sir. We have come to speak to you about your book. Our representative has read Part I, and reports unfavourably upon it. It contains no moral. There are scenes of violence, and your hero is far from perfect.’

“ ‘I think you mistake my object,’ said Mr. Hughes; ‘Tom is a boy, not a patent medicine. In other words, he is not supposed to be perfect.’

===Chinese===
「『您这就会有这份荣幸了，先生。我们是来同您谈谈您那本书的。本会代表已拜读第一部，所呈报告极为不利。书中毫无道德教益。有暴力的场面，而您的主人公又远非完人。』

「『我想诸位错会了我的宗旨，』休斯先生说；『汤姆是个孩子，不是一瓶专利成药。换句话说，本来就没打算让他十全十美。』

===Original===
“ ‘Well, I am not here to bandy words. The second part of your book must be written to suit the rules of our Society. Do you agree, or shall we throw you over that precipice?’

“ ‘Never. I mean, I don’t agree.’

“ ‘Then we must write it for you. Remember, sir, that you will be constantly watched, and if you attempt to write that second part yourself—’ ” (he paused dramatically). “So the second part was written by the committee of the Society. So now you know.”

===Chinese===
「『好了，本人此来不为斗嘴。您那本书的第二部，必须遵照本会章程来写。您是应承呢，还是我们这就把您扔下那道悬崖？』

「『绝不。呃，我是说，我不应承。』

「『那么，我们就只好代劳捉刀了。记住，先生，您将受到时刻监视，倘若您企图亲自动笔去写那第二部——』」（他戏剧性地顿了一顿。）「于是，第二部便由协会的委员会写成了。这下您知道了。」

===Original===
“But,” said I, “how do you account for the fight with Slogger Williams?”

“The president relented slightly towards the end, and consented to Mr. Hughes inserting a chapter of his own, on condition that the Society should finish it. And the Society did. See?”

“But—”

===Chinese===
「可是，」我说，「与斯洛格·威廉姆斯那场打斗，您又作何解释？」

「会长临到末了略发了一点善心，允准休斯先生插进一章他本人手笔，条件是那一章须由协会来收尾。协会也确实收了尾。明白了吗？」

「可是——」

---

===Original===
“Ticket.”

“Eh?”

“Ticket, please, sir.”

===Chinese===
「车票。」

「啊？」

「请出示车票，先生。」

===Original===
I looked up. The guard was standing at the open door. My companion had vanished.

===Chinese===
我抬起头来。列车员正站在敞开的车门旁。我那位同伴已经无影无踪。

===Original===
“Guard,” said I, as I handed him my ticket, “where’s the gentleman who travelled up with me?”

“Gentleman, sir? I haven’t seen nobody.”

“Not a man in tweeds with red hair? I mean, in tweeds and owning red hair.”

===Chinese===
「列车员，」我一边把车票递给他，一边说，「同我一道坐车来的那位先生呢？」

「先生？啥先生？俺可没瞅见啥人。」

「不是有位穿花呢衣裳、长红头发的先生吗？我是说，穿花呢衣裳、并且持有红头发的那位。」

===Original===
“No, sir. You’ve been alone in the carriage all the way up. Must have dreamed it, sir.”

Possibly I did.

===Chinese===
「没有，先生。您这一路上，一个人独占着车厢。准是做梦了，先生。」

也兴许真是做梦。

## Work / 功课

===Original===
> With a pleasure that’s emphatic
> We retire to our attic
> With the satisfying feeling that our duty has been done.
> Oh! philosophers may sing
> Of the troubles of a king
> But of pleasures there are many and of troubles there are none,
> And the culminating pleasure
> Which we treasure beyond measure
> Is the satisfying feeling that our duty has been done.
> — W. S. Gilbert

===Chinese===
> 怀着一种响当当的欢喜，
> 我们退回自家的阁楼里，
> 心头盛满差事已毕的惬意。
> 啊！哲人们尽可歌吟
> 为王者治世的万般烦辛，
> 可这厢乐事数不尽、苦处无处寻，
> 而那压轴的一份欢喜，
> 我们珍爱得无可估拟，
> 便是差事已毕的这份惬意。
> —— W·S·吉尔伯特（W. S. Gilbert）

===Original===
Work is supposed to be the centre round which school life revolves—the hub of the school wheel, the lodestar of the schoolboy’s existence, and a great many other things. “You come to school to work,” is the formula used by masters when sentencing a victim to the wailing and gnashing of teeth provided by two hours’ extra tuition on a hot afternoon. In this, I think, they err, and my opinion is backed up by numerous scholars of my acquaintance, who have even gone so far—on occasions when they themselves have been the victims—as to express positive disapproval of the existing state of things. In the dear, dead days (beyond recall), I used often to long to put the case to my form-master in its only fair aspect, but always refrained from motives of policy. Masters are so apt to take offence at the well-meant endeavours of their form to instruct them in the way they should go.

What I should have liked to have done would have been something after this fashion. Entering the sanctum of the Headmaster, I should have motioned him to his seat—if he were seated already, have assured him that to rise was unnecessary. I should then have taken a seat myself, taking care to preserve a calm fixity of demeanour, and finally, with a preliminary cough, I should have embarked upon the following moving address: “My dear sir, my dear Reverend Jones or Brown (as the case may be), believe me when I say that your whole system of work is founded on a fallacious dream and reeks of rottenness. No, no, I beg that you will not interrupt me. The real state of the case, if I may say so, is briefly this: a boy goes to school to enjoy himself, and, on arriving, finds to his consternation that a great deal more work is expected of him than he is prepared to do. What course, then, Reverend Jones or Brown, does he take? He proceeds to do as much work as will steer him safely between the, ah—I may say, the Scylla of punishment and the Charybdis of being considered what my, er—fellow-pupils euphoniously term a swot. That, I think, is all this morning. *Good* day. Pray do not trouble to rise. I will find my way out.” I should then have made for the door, locked it, if possible, on the outside, and, rushing to the railway station, have taken a through ticket to Spitzbergen or some other place where Extradition treaties do not hold good.

===Chinese===
据说，功课是学校生活环绕其旋转的中心——是校轮的轮毂，是学童生存的指路星，还有许许多多别的名头。「你到学校是来念书的」——这句套话，是老师们给受害者量刑时用的：刑名是在一个大热天的下午接受两小时额外补课，外加随之奉送的哀号与切齿。在这一点上，我以为他们错了；而且我这见解，还有我相识的众多学者撑腰——他们甚至曾在自家沦为受害者之际，对现状表达过斩钉截铁的不满。在那逝去的好时光里（一去不复返矣），我常常渴望向我的班主任陈明此案唯一公允的一面，但每每出于策略考虑而作罢。老师们实在太容易见怪，每当他们的班级出于好意、试图指点他们走当行的路时。

我当年真正想干的，本该是这么一出。跨进校长的圣所，我先示意他就座——倘若他已坐定，便向他保证大可不必起身。然后我自找个座位坐下，注意保持仪态的沉着与凝定，末了，咳上一声清嗓开讲，发表如下动人的演说：「我亲爱的先生，我亲爱的琼斯牧师或布朗牧师（视情况而定），请相信我这一句话：您的整套功课外系，建立在一个谬误的梦上，浑身透着腐朽气。不，不，请千万莫打断我。恕我直言，事情的真实情形简述如下：一个孩子进学校，是为了享乐；到得地方，却惊愕地发现，人家指望他做的功课，远远多于他打算奉送的。那么，琼斯牧师或布朗牧师，他采取什么方针呢？他的方针是做分量恰好够用的功课，以便在两块礁石之间安全操舵——一块是，啊——不妨说，惩罚的斯库拉（Scylla）；另一块，是被视作我那些，呃——同窗学友美其名曰『书呆子』（swot）的那种东西的卡律布狄斯（Charybdis）。我想，今天上午就到这里。*日*安。请勿劳驾起身，出去的路我自家认得。」说罢我便直奔房门，有可能的话从外面反锁，随即冲到火车站，买一张直通斯匹次卑尔根（Spitzbergen）或任何引渡条约鞭长莫及之地的联程票。

===Original===
But ’twas not mine to play the Tib. Gracchus, to emulate the O. Cromwell. So far from pouring my opinions like so much boiling oil into the ear of my taskmaster, I was content to play the part of audience while *he* did the talking, my sole remark being “Yes’r” at fixed intervals.

===Chinese===
可惜，扮提比略·格拉古（Tib. Gracchus）、追慕奥利弗·克伦威尔（O. Cromwell）的命数，不在我这里。非但我没把自家的见解当作沸油，一勺勺灌进我那位功课监工的耳朵，反倒心甘情愿充任听众，由*他*包揽发言，我全部的台词，不过是每隔一段固定的时候应一声「是，先生」。

===Original===
And yet I knew that I was in the right. My bosom throbbed with the justice of my cause. For why? The ambition of every human new boy is surely to become like J. Essop of the first eleven, who can hit a ball over two ponds, a wood, and seven villages, rather than to resemble that pale young student, Mill-Stuart, who, though he can speak Sanskrit like a native of Sanskritia, couldn’t score a single off a slow long-hop.

===Chinese===
然而我深知自己占理。我的胸膛为我这事业的正义而激荡。为什么呢？每一个心智健全的新生，其志向必然是成为一队的J·埃索普（J. Essop）那号人物——此公一击能把球送过两口池塘、一片树林，外加七个村庄——而不是去效仿那位面色苍白的年轻学子米尔-斯图尔特（Mill-Stuart）：此君固然梵语讲得像梵语国（Sanskritia）土著一般地道，可面对一记慢悠悠的高弹短球，连一分也挣不着。

===Original===
And this ambition is a laudable one. For the athlete is the product of nature—a step towards the more perfect type of animal, while the scholar is the outcome of artificiality. What, I ask, does the scholar gain, either morally or physically, or in any other way, by knowing who was tribune of the people in 284 BC or what is the precise difference between the various constructions of *cum*? It is not as if ignorance of the tribune’s identity caused him any mental unrest. In short, what excuse is there for the student? “None,” shrieks Echo enthusiastically. “None whatever.”

===Chinese===
而这志向，是可敬可嘉的。因为运动员乃自然之造物——是迈向更完善的动物类型的一个台阶；学者却是人工造作的产物。敢问，学者知道了公元前 284 年的人民保民官是何许人，又知道了 *cum* 的各种结构之间精确的差别何在，于道德、于体格、于任何别的方面，能捞着什么好处？他又不会因为不知道那位保民官的尊姓大名而心神不宁。总而言之，学生还有什么借口？「没有，」回声（Echo）热切地尖叫道，「丝毫没有。」

===Original===
Our children are being led to ruin by this system. They will become Dons and think in Greek. The victim of the craze stops at nothing. He puns in Latin. He quips and quirks in Ionic and Doric. In the worst stages of the disease he will edit Greek plays and say that Merry quite misses the fun of the passage, or that Jebb is mediocre. Think, I beg of you, paterfamilias, and you, mater ditto, what your feelings would be were you to find Henry or Archibald Cuthbert correcting proofs of the *Agamemnon*, and inventing “nasty ones” for Mr. Sidgwick! Very well then. Be warned.

Our bright-eyed lads are taught insane constructions in Greek and Latin from morning till night, and they come for their holidays, in many cases, without the merest foundation of a batting style. Ask them what a Yorker is, and they will say: “A man from York, though I presume you mean a Yorkshireman.” They will read Herodotus without a dictionary for pleasure, but ask them to translate the childishly simple sentence: “Trott was soon in his timber-yard with a length’un that whipped across from the off,” and they’ll shrink abashed and swear they have not skill at that, as Gilbert says.

===Chinese===
这套制度正把我们的孩子引向毁灭。他们将成为大学导师（Don），用希腊语思考。染上这狂热的病号，什么事都干得出。他用拉丁语编双关，他用爱奥尼亚与多里斯方言插科打诨。到了病势最凶的阶段，他会动手编注希腊戏剧，还说梅里（Merry）全没领会那段文字的妙趣，或者说杰布（Jebb）不过尔尔。我求你们想一想，各位为人父者（paterfamilias），还有照此办理的为人母者：倘若你们撞见亨利（Henry）或阿奇博尔德·卡斯伯特（Archibald Cuthbert）正在校订《阿伽门农》（*Agamemnon*）的清样，还在替西奇威克先生（Mr. Sidgwick）炮制「毒辣评语」，你们心中将是何等滋味！很好。那么，引以为戒。

我们这些眸子锃亮的少年，从早到晚被人灌着希腊语与拉丁语的疯癫句式，到放假回家时，多半连击球姿势的最起码根基都没打下。问他们什么叫约克球（Yorker），他们会答：「约克（York）来的人，不过我猜您想说的是约克郡人。」他们能不带字典读希罗多德（Herodotus）以自娱，可是请他们翻译这句简单到孩子气的话——「特罗特不多时便把人送进了木料场：一记长线球，自偏外一侧斜鞭进来」——他们便臊得缩回去，赌咒说这等本事自己没有，正如吉尔伯特所言。

===Original===
The papers sometimes contain humorous forecasts of future education, when cricket and football shall come to their own. They little know the excellence of the thing they mock at. When we get schools that teach nothing but games, then will the sun definitely refuse to set on the roast beef of old England. May it be soon. Some day, mayhap, I shall gather my great-great-grandsons round my knee, and tell them—as one tells tales of Faery—that I can remember the time when Work was considered the be-all and the end-all of a school career. Perchance, when my great-great-grandson John (called John after the famous Jones of that name) has brought home the prize for English Essay on “Rugby *v.* Association,” I shall pat his head (gently) and the tears will come to my old eyes as I recall the time when I, too, might have won a prize—for that obsolete subject, Latin Prose—and was only prevented by the superior excellence of my thirty-and-one fellow students, coupled, indeed, with my own inability to conjugate *sum*.

===Chinese===
报章上偶尔登些关于未来教育的滑稽预测，说有朝一日板球与足球将各得其所、扬眉吐气。他们哪里知道，自己嘲笑的东西有何等妙处。等我们办起只教游戏的学校，太阳便断然拒绝再从老英格兰的烤牛肉上落下。愿这一天早日降临。说不定哪一天，我会把玄孙们拢到膝前，对他们讲——像讲仙境奇谈那样——我竟记得有过那样一个年代，功课被当作求学生涯的一切之一切。又说不定，等我的玄孙约翰（他袭那位以此名驰名的鼎鼎大名的琼斯之讳，也唤作约翰）捧着英语作文的奖状回家——题目是《拉格比对足协足球》（Rugby *v.* Association）——我会（轻轻地）拍拍他的头，老泪涌上我这双老眼，想起当年我也本可以夺一座奖——就在拉丁散文（Latin Prose）那门早已过时的科目上——只可惜被三十一位同窗的出类拔萃阻了道，当然，也少不了我自己不会变位 *sum* 这一条。

===Original===
Such days, I say, may come. But now are the Dark Ages. The only thing that can possibly make Work anything but an unmitigated nuisance is the prospect of a ’Varsity scholarship, and the thought that, in the event of failure, a ’Varsity career will be out of the question.

===Chinese===
我说，这样的日子兴许会来。可眼下还是黑暗时代。唯一能让功课免于沦为彻头彻尾之祸害的，是大学奖学金（’Varsity scholarship）的前景，外加一个念头：一朝失手，大学生涯便无从谈起。

===Original===
With this thought constantly before him, the student can put a certain amount of enthusiasm into his work, and even go to the length of rising at five o’clock o’ mornings to drink yet deeper of the cup of knowledge. I have done it myself. ’Varsity means games and yellow waistcoats and Proctors, and that sort of thing. It is worth working for.

===Chinese===
有这念头时时在眼前晃，学生便能往功课里注入一定剂量的热忱，甚至不惜走到这一步：每天清晨五点起身，把知识之杯往更深处再饮一口。这我自己就干过。上大学意味着各种游戏、黄马甲，还有训导官（Proctor），诸如此类。为此用功，值。

===Original===
But for the unfortunate individual who is barred by circumstances from participating in these joys, what inducement is there to work? Is such a one to leave the school nets in order to stew in a stuffy room over a Thucydides? I trow not.

===Chinese===
可对于那些时运不济、无福参与这些乐事的倒霉蛋，用功的诱饵又在哪里？难道要他离开学校的板球练习网，钻进一间闷罐似的屋子，对着修昔底德文火慢炖？我看不然。

===Original===
Chapter one of my great forthcoming work, *The Compleat Slacker*, contains minute instructions on the art of avoiding preparation from beginning to end of term. Foremost among the words of advice ranks this maxim: Get an official list of the books you are to do, and examine them carefully with a view to seeing what it is possible to do unseen.
