Thus, if Virgil is among these authors, you can rely on being able to do him with success. People who ought to know better will tell you that Virgil is hard. Such a shallow falsehood needs little comment. A scholar who cannot translate ten lines of *The Aeneid* between the time he is put on and the time he begins to speak is unworthy of pity or consideration, and if I meet him in the street I shall assuredly cut him. Aeschylus, on the other hand, is a demon, and needs careful watching, though in an emergency you can always say the reading is wrong.

===Chinese===
笔者即将问世的煌煌巨著《偷懒大全》（*The Compleat Slacker*）第一章，载有如何从学期头一路偷懒到学期尾的精细指南。在全部忠告里，头一条箴言是：弄一份官方书单来，细细查验，专看哪些书目可以即席对付。如此一来，倘若维吉尔名列其中，你尽管放心，保管旗开得胜。那些本该更有见识的人会对你说，维吉尔很难。如此浅薄的谎言，几乎不值一驳。一名学者，若在被点到与张口之间译不出《埃涅阿斯纪》（*The Aeneid*）十行，便不配得到怜悯或体谅；我若在街上遇见他，断然当他空气。埃斯库罗斯（Aeschylus）则不然，那是个魔头，须严加提防——不过情急之下，你总可以说通行读本此处有误。

===Original===
Sometimes the compleat slacker falls into a trap. The saddest case I can remember is that of poor Charles Vanderpoop. He was a bright young lad, and showed some promise of rising to heights as a slacker. He fell in this fashion. One Easter term his form had half-finished a speech of Demosthenes, and the form-master gave them to understand that they would absorb the rest during the forthcoming term. Charles, being naturally anxious to do as little work as possible during the summer months, spent his Easter holidays carefully preparing this speech, so as to have it ready in advance. What was his horror, on returning to school at the appointed date, to find that they were going to throw Demosthenes over altogether, and patronize Plato. Threats, entreaties, prayers—all were accounted nothing by the master who had led him into this morass of troubles. It is believed that the shock destroyed his reason. At any rate, the fact remains that that term (the summer term, mark you) he won two prizes. In the following term he won three. To recapitulate his outrages from that time to the present were a harrowing and unnecessary task. Suffice it that he is now a Regius Professor, and I saw in the papers a short time ago that a lecture of his on “The Probable Origin of the Greek Negative,” created quite a *furore*. If this is not Tragedy with a big T, I should like to know what it is.

===Chinese===
有时，登峰造极的偷懒生也会掉进陷阱。我记忆中最惨的一案，是可怜的查尔斯·范德普普（Charles Vanderpoop）。他是个伶俐的少年，颇有希望攀上偷懒事业的高峰。他栽跟头的经过如下：某年复活节学期，他班上学了德摩斯梯尼（Demosthenes）一篇演说的前一半，班主任让他们明白，剩下的一半下学期再消化。查尔斯天生巴不得夏季那几个月少做点功课，便把复活节假期统统花在精心准备这篇演说上，好提前备足。谁知到了既定日期返校，他惊恐地发现：德摩斯梯尼将被整个抛下，改捧柏拉图（Plato）了。威胁、哀告、祈祷——在那位把他领进这片苦沼的先生那里，一概不作数。据说那一惊毁了他的理智。无论如何，事实俱在：那个学期（请注意，是夏季学期）他捧走两个奖。下一个学期，他捧走三个。要把他从那时直至今日的一桩桩暴行尽数清点，未免令人痛心，也大可不必。只消说一句：他如今已贵为钦定讲座教授（Regius Professor），前些日子我还在报上读到，他一场题为《希腊语否定词之可能起源》的演讲，引起了不小的轰动。如果这都不算首字母大写的「悲剧」，我倒要请教，什么才算。

===Original===
As an exciting pastime, unseen translation must rank very high. Everyone who has ever tried translating unseen must acknowledge that all other forms of excitement seem but feeble makeshifts after it. I have, in the course of a career of sustained usefulness to the human race, had my share of thrills. I have asked a strong and busy porter, at Paddington, when the Brighton train started. I have gone for the broad jump record in trying to avoid a motorcar. I have played Spillikins and Ping-Pong. But never again have I felt the excitement that used to wander athwart my moral backbone when I was put on to translate a passage containing a notorious crux and seventeen doubtful readings, with only that innate genius, which is the wonder of the civilized world, to pull me through. And what a glow of pride one feels when it is all over; when one has made a glorious, golden guess at the crux, and trampled the doubtful readings under foot with inspired ease. It is like a day at the seaside.

===Chinese===
论刺激性消遣，即席翻译必定位居头等。凡试过即席翻译的人，都得承认：经过它之后，其余一切刺激看上去不过是些疲软的代用品。本人在为人类持续造福的生涯中，也领受过属于自己的那份战栗。我曾在帕丁顿（Paddington）车站，向一位身强力壮而公务繁忙的搬运工，打听开往布莱顿（Brighton）的火车几点启程。我曾为躲一辆汽车而冲击跳远纪录。我玩过挑小棒和乒乓。可是，当年那种刺激从此再没有重来过——那时我被点名即席翻译一段文章，里头埋着一处臭名昭著的疑难考订点和十七处可疑异文，能渡我出险的，唯有那份令文明世界叹为奇观的天赋；那股刺激，曾在我道德的脊梁骨上横冲直撞地游走。而当一切收场，心头又是何等自豪的暖流：你对那疑难点做出了一次辉煌的、金灿灿的猜测，又以如有神助的从容，把可疑异文一一踩在脚下。那滋味，就像在海边痛快玩了一天。

===Original===
Work is bad enough, but Examinations are worse, especially the Board Examinations. By doing from ten to twenty minutes prep every night, the compleat slacker could get through most of the term with average success. Then came the Examinations. The dabbler in unseen translations found himself caught as in a snare. Gone was the peaceful security in which he had lulled to rest all the well-meant efforts of his guardian angel to rouse him to a sense of his duties. There, right in front of him, yawned the abyss of Retribution.

===Chinese===
功课已经够糟了，考试更糟，尤其是委员会考试。凭借每晚十到二十分钟的预习，登峰造极的偷懒生本可以平平常常地混过大半个学期。然后，考试来了。在即席翻译上浅尝辄止的人，发现自己像落进了套索。往日那太平安稳一去不返——在那安稳里，守护天使一片好心，想唤醒他的一点职责意识，却都被他哄着睡了过去。而此刻，就在他正前方，报应的深渊张开了大口。

===Original===
Alas! poor slacker. I knew him, Horatio; a fellow of infinite jest, of most excellent fancy. Where be his gibes now? How is he to cope with the fiendish ingenuity of the examiners? How is he to master the contents of a book of Thucydides in a couple of days? It is a fearsome problem. Perhaps he will get up in the small hours and work by candlelight from two till eight o’clock. In this case he will start his day a mental and physical wreck. Perhaps he will try to work and be led away by the love of light reading.

In any case he will fail to obtain enough marks to satisfy the examiners, though whether examiners ever are satisfied, except by Harry the hero of the school story (Every Lad’s Library, uniform edition, 2s. 6d.), is rather a doubtful question.

===Chinese===
唉！可怜的偷懒生。霍拉旭，我认识他；一个玩笑无穷的家伙，想象力顶顶出色。他的俏皮话如今何在？他拿什么招架考官们魔鬼般的巧思？他拿什么在两三天里啃下修昔底德一卷书？这是个骇人的难题。也许他会在下半夜起身，就着烛光从两点苦干到八点。果真如此，他这一天便将以身心俱残开场。也许他会试着用功，却又叫轻松读物的爱好勾了魂去。

无论哪条路，他终究凑不齐让考官满意的分数——尽管考官究竟满意过没有，本就是桩颇为可疑的公案；能让他们满意的，怕只有校园小说里的主人公哈利（Harry）——《少年文库》（Every Lad’s Library）丛书，统一版，两先令六便士。

===Original===
In such straits, matters resolve themselves into a sort of drama with three characters. We will call our hero Smith.

===Chinese===
身处这般窘境，事情便自行化作一出三个人物的小戏。我们的主人公，姑且唤作史密斯（Smith）。

===Original===
Scene: a Study

Dramatis Personae: **Smith**, **Conscience**, **Mephistopheles**

*Enter **Smith** down centre.*

*He seats himself at table and opens a Thucydides.*

*Enter **Conscience** through ceiling R., **Mephistopheles** through floor L.*

===Chinese===
场景：一间书房

人物表：**史密斯**、**良心**、**梅菲斯托费勒斯**（**Mephistopheles**）

***史密斯**自台中上。*

*他在桌前坐定，翻开一卷修昔底德。*

***良心**自右侧天花板穿入，**梅菲斯托费勒斯**自左侧地板穿入。*

===Original===
Conscience

*With a kindly smile.* Precisely what I was about to remark, my dear lad. A little Thucydides would be a very good thing. Thucydides, as you doubtless know, was a very famous Athenian historian. Date?

Smith

Er—um—let me see.

Mephistopheles

*Aside.* Look in the Introduction and pretend you did it by accident.

Smith

*Having done so.* 431 BC. circ.

***Conscience** wipes away a tear.*

===Chinese===
良心

*面带慈和的微笑。*「我正要说这句话呢，亲爱的孩子。读一点修昔底德会是件大好事。修昔底德，想必你也知道，是位鼎鼎大名的雅典史家。年代？」

史密斯

「呃——唔——容我想想。」

梅菲斯托费勒斯

*旁白。*「去查导言，再装作是碰巧翻到的。」

史密斯

*照办之后。*「约公元前 431 年。」

*良心拭去一滴眼泪。*

===Original===
Conscience

Thucydides made himself a thorough master of the concisest of styles.

Mephistopheles

And in doing so became infernally obscure. Excuse shop.

Smith

*Gloomily.* Hum!

Mephistopheles

*Sneeringly.* Ha!

*Long pause.*

===Chinese===
良心

「修昔底德把自己锤炼成了最精练文体的彻头彻尾的大师。」

梅菲斯托费勒斯

「也正因如此，变得晦涩得要命。恕我掉书袋。」

史密斯

*郁郁地。*「唔！」

梅菲斯托费勒斯

*哂笑着。*「哈！」

*长时间静场。*

===Original===
Conscience

*Gently.* Do you not think, my dear lad, that you had better begin? Time and tide, as you are aware, wait for no man. And—

Smith

Yes?

Conscience

Well, you know, that examination tomorrow.

===Chinese===
良心

*温言。*「亲爱的孩子，你不觉得最好这就动手吗？时光与潮汐，如你所知，不待任何人。而且——」

史密斯

「嗯？」

良心

「呃，你知道的，明天那场考试。」

===Original===
Smith

Yes?

Conscience

You have not, I fear, a very firm grasp of the subject. However, if you work hard till eleven—

Smith

*Gloomily.* Hum! Three hours!

===Chinese===
史密斯

「嗯？」

良心

「恐怕你对这门功课，掌握得还不够牢靠。不过，要是你用功到十一点——」

史密斯

*郁郁地。*「唔！三个钟头！」

===Original===
Mephistopheles

*Cheerily.* Exactly *so*. Three hours. A little more if anything. By the way, excuse me asking, but have you prepared the subject thoroughly during the term?

Smith

My *dear* sir! Of *course*!

Conscience

*Reprovingly.* ???!!??!

Smith

Well, perhaps, not quite so much as I might have done. Such a lot of things to do this term. Cricket, for instance.

===Chinese===
梅菲斯托费勒斯

*兴冲冲地。*「正是*如此*。三个钟头。真要说，只多不少。顺便，恕我多问一句——这学期你把这门功课彻头彻尾地准备过了吗？」

史密斯

「我*亲爱的*先生！*当然*！」

良心

*责备地。*「？？？！！？？」

史密斯

「唔，也许，没有做到本可以做到的那么多。这学期要干的事太多了。比方说，板球。」

===Original===
Mephistopheles

Rather. Talking of cricket, you seemed to be shaping rather well last Saturday. I had just run up on business, and someone told me you made eighty not out. Get your century all right?

Smith

*Brightening at the recollection.* Just a bit—117 not out. I hit—but perhaps you’ve heard?

Mephistopheles

Not at all, not at all. Let’s hear all about it.

***Conscience** seeks to interpose, but is prevented by **Mephistopheles**, who eggs **Smith** on to talk cricket for over an hour.*

===Chinese===
梅菲斯托费勒斯

「可不是嘛。说起板球，上礼拜六你打得挺像样。我那天正好有事跑了一趟，有人告诉我，你拿了八十分未出局。那一百分后来到手了吧？」

史密斯

*忆及此事，眼上一亮。*「何止一点——117 分未出局。我打出的那记——不过你多半已经听说了？」

梅菲斯托费勒斯

「没有的事，没有的事。快细细讲来。」

*良心试图插话，被梅菲斯托费勒斯拦住；后者怂恿史密斯谈板球，一谈就是一个多钟头。*

===Original===
Conscience

*At last; in an acid voice.* That is a history of the Peloponnesian war by Thucydides on the table in front of you. I thought I would mention it, in case you had forgotten.

Smith

Great Scott, yes! Here, I say, I must start.

Conscience

Hear! Hear!

===Chinese===
良心

*终于，语带酸味。*「你面前桌上摆着的，是修昔底德所著的伯罗奔尼撒战争史。我想还是提一句的好，免得你忘了。」

史密斯

「天哪，可不是！喂，我说，我得开工了。」

良心

「善哉！善哉！」

===Original===
Mephistopheles

*Insinuatingly.* *One* moment. Did you say you *had* prepared this book during the term? Afraid I’m a little hard of hearing. Eh, what?

Smith

Well—er no, I have not. Have you ever played billiards with a walking-stick and five balls?

Mephistopheles

Quite so, quite so. I quite understand. Don’t you distress yourself, old chap. You obviously can’t get through a whole book of Thucydides in under two hours, can you?

===Chinese===
梅菲斯托费勒斯

*循循善诱。*「*等*一下。你方才说，这学期你*确实*准备过这本书？恐怕我有点耳背。呃，什么来着？」

史密斯

「唔——呃，没准备过。你用手杖加五只球，打过台球吗？」

梅菲斯托费勒斯

「正是，正是。我完全明白。老伙计，你可别自寻烦恼。不到两个钟头，你显然啃不完整整一卷修昔底德，对不对？」

===Original===
Conscience

*Severely.* He might, by attentive application to study, master a considerable portion of the historian’s *chef d’oeuvre* in that time.

Mephistopheles

Yes, and find that not one of the passages he had prepared was set in the paper.

Conscience

At the least, he would, if he were to pursue the course which I have indicated, greatly benefit his mind.

Mephistopheles

*Gives a short, derisive laugh.*

*Long pause.*

===Chinese===
良心

*正色。*「他未尝不能。只要专心向学，在这点时间里，把这位史家*杰作*的相当一部分攻下来。」

梅菲斯托费勒斯

「是啊，然后发现他准备过的段落，考卷里一处也没出。」

良心

「至少，他若循我指明的路径而行，心智必将大有长进。」

梅菲斯托费勒斯

*发出一声短促的嗤笑。*

*长时间静场。*

===Original===
Mephistopheles

*Looking towards bookshelf.* Hullo, you’ve got a decent lot of books, pommy word you have. *Rodney Stone*, *Vice Versa*, *Many Cargoes*. Ripping. Ever read *Many Cargoes*?

Conscience

*Glancing at his watch.* I am sorry, but I must really go now. I will see you some other day.

*Exit sorrowfully.*

Mephistopheles

Well, thank goodness *he’s* gone. Never saw such a fearful old bore in my life. Can’t think why you let him hang on to you so. We may as well make a night of it now, eh? No use your trying to work at this time of night.

===Chinese===
梅菲斯托费勒斯

*朝书架望去。*「嚯，你这批书委实不赖，不骗你，委实不赖。《罗德尼·斯通》（*Rodney Stone*）、《父子易位》（*Vice Versa*）、《船货无数》（*Many Cargoes*）。绝了。读过《船货无数》吗？」

良心

*看了看表。*「抱歉，此刻我真的必须走了。改日再见。」

*怏怏下。*

梅菲斯托费勒斯

「哎，谢天谢地，*他*可走了。我平生没见过这么吓人的老厌物。想不通你干吗容他这般缠着你不放。这下咱们索性痛快玩它一整夜，怎么样？这么晚你再用功，也是白搭。」

===Original===
Smith

Not a bit.

Mephistopheles

Did you say you’d *not* read *Many Cargoes*?

Smith

Never. Only got it today. Good?

Mephistopheles

Simply ripping. All short stories. Make you yell.

===Chinese===
史密斯

「一点不假。」

梅菲斯托费勒斯

「你方才说，《船货无数》还*没*读过？」

史密斯

「从没读过。今天刚到手。好看吗？」

梅菲斯托费勒斯

「绝品，简直是。全是短篇。保你笑出眼泪。」

===Original===
Smith

*With a last effort.* But don’t you think—

Mephistopheles

Oh no. Besides, you can easily get up early tomorrow for the Thucydides.

Smith

Of course I can. Never thought of that. Heave us *Many Cargoes*. Thanks.

*Begins to read. **Mephistopheles** grins fiendishly, and vanishes through floor enveloped in red flame. Sobbing heard from the direction of the ceiling.*

*Scene closes.*

===Chinese===
史密斯

*作最后一搏。*「可你不觉得——」

梅菲斯托费勒斯

「哦，不觉得。再说，明早你大可以早点起来，搞修昔底德嘛。」

史密斯

「我当然可以。怎么早没想到。把《船货无数》扔过来。谢了。」

*开始读书。梅菲斯托费勒斯狞笑一声，裹在一团红焰中没入地板。天花板方向传来呜咽之声。*

*幕落。*

===Original===
Next morning, of course, he will oversleep himself, and his Thucydides paper will be of such a calibre that that eminent historian will writhe in his grave.

===Chinese===
翌日清晨，他自然要睡过头；而他的修昔底德答卷，也将「精彩」到教那位显赫的史家在坟墓里辗转反侧。
